--- Page 3 ---
<meta>This is the introductory page of the "Ghayat al-Hakim" (The Goal of the Wise), a foundational text of astral magic and hermetic philosophy. The author establishes the timeline of his writing and the pedagogical structure of the work.</meta>

# In the name of God, the Compassionate, the Merciful

Praise be to God, from whose light the veils of secrecy are illuminated, and from whom the wonders of destiny flow. To Him all traces return, and by His command night and day alternate. He is the bringer of things into existence after their non-existence. Praise be to God, the Creator of all beings and the Changer of their blessings. He is the Ordainer and the Originator of every being; He is neither mingled with them nor distinct from them. Attributes do not encompass Him, injustice does not touch Him, descriptions do not exhaust Him, and accidents <note>In philosophy, "accidents" refers to non-essential properties that change, unlike the eternal essence.</note> do not affect Him. May God bless the Master of Messengers and the Seal of the Prophets, upon whom the Book was revealed in a clear Arabic tongue, and who is mentioned in the scriptures of the ancients, and upon his virtuous, good, and pure family, and grant them peace.

## Know

O student, who are enticed to dive into the sciences of the philosophers, to look into their secrets, and to search for the wonders they have immortalized in their books: know that the motive that moved me to compose this book—which I have named *The Goal of the Wise* <note>Original: "Ghayat al-Hakim." This is the famous "Picatrix."</note> and the more deserving of the two results to be presented first—is what occurred to me during the composition of the book I wrote previously, which I named *The Rank of the Wise* <note>Original: "Rutbat al-Hakim." This was the author's work on alchemy.</note>. My composition of that book began in the year 343 <note>954-955 CE</note> and I completed it at the end of the year 348 <note>959-960 CE</note>.

What moved me to compose this is what I have seen most people of our time searching for regarding the matter of <term>talismans</term> <note>Objects inscribed with astrological symbols to capture celestial power.</note> and the various arts of magic, while they do not know what they seek nor which path they intend. Their lives have been wasted in a pursuit that has been shielded from them because the Sages concealed it and declined to reveal it, due to the destruction and ruin it could bring to the world. God forbids that, for the welfare of His world. For this reason, they erected the structures of the <term>temples</term> <note>Original: "Barabi," referring specifically to ancient Egyptian temples believed to hold hidden alchemical and magical secrets.</note> and placed what they immortalized in books using symbols and enigmas that none but a Sage like them can understand. Yet, they conveyed the truth in between those lines and explained the secrets to those who could comprehend them.

I saw fit to disseminate to the people and explain to them the obscured path of this result called <term>Simiya</term> <note>Natural or letter magic; here referring to the art of enchanting the senses or the soul.</note>, and to clarify what the Sages hid regarding this magical result, just as we did with the <term>alchemical result</term> <note>Original: "al-natija al-san'awiyya," referring to the "Art" of alchemy.</note>. I have divided this book into four treatises, each treatise being useful and summarized into chapters.

**The First Treatise** has seven chapters, intentionally corresponding to the seven fast-moving planets. The first chapter is on the nobility of wisdom. In this first treatise, I mention the celestial proportions and the forms of the spheres' proportions when constructing talismans, and how the fixed stars cast their rays upon the planets. I have deposited within it obscure meanings that the Sages kept hidden out of stinginess and greed at the beginning of the treatise.

**The Second Treatise** is on the celestial images <note>The constellations and decans.</note> and the explanation of the secrets the Sages obscured, and examples of how magic is borrowed into this world—called the <term>World of Generation and Corruption</term> <note>The sublunary world where things are born and die, as opposed to the eternal heavens.</note>—from the world of the ether. I also discuss what led Plato to speak of the "unification of forms."

**The Third Treatise** is on the properties of the planets within the <term>three kingdoms</term> <note>Original: "al-muwalladat al-thalath," meaning minerals, plants, and animals.</note>, for there is nothing in the world of generation and corruption that accepts magical work except these. The <term>elements</term> <note>Original: "al-istiqassat," from the Greek "stoicheia."</note> are impossible to work with directly as they are fluid and do not hold impressions; therefore, only these three remain. I shall mention the mixing of some with others to achieve the desired magical effects through the influence of elemental or natural heat, whether carried by incense to be burned, or by things intended for the stomach, such as food or drink.

**The Fourth Treatise** is on the magic of the Kurds, the Nabataeans, and the Abyssinians, and examples of the works of magical stratagems, which are the finest types of magic. I shall complete this purpose as required, without stinginess or concealment. I ask God for help in completing what we have intended; He is the Master of that, and with His help, I begin.

<summary>The author introduces "The Goal of the Wise" (Picatrix), explaining that he wrote it to clarify the secrets of talismans and magic which were hidden by ancient sages. He outlines the four treatises of the book, covering celestial proportions, planetary images, the three kingdoms of nature, and regional magical practices.</summary>
<keywords>Picatrix, Ghayat al-Hakim, talismans, alchemy, planetary magic, Simiya, generation and corruption, elements, Nabataean magic</keywords>

--- Page 4 ---
# The First Treatise

Know, O brother—may God enlighten your intellect—that wisdom is the most sublime of gifts and the best of acquisitions. For wisdom is the knowledge of the remote causes <note>original: "al-asbab al-ba'ida" - in Aristotelian philosophy, these are the ultimate or primary principles of existence</note> by which all existing things came to be, and the knowledge of the immediate causes for those things that possess them. This is achieved by demonstrating their existence and knowing what they are and how they are.

Though these causes are many, they ascend in an orderly fashion to a single Existent who is the cause for the existence of those remote things and the immediate things beneath them. That One is the First in reality; His subsistence does not depend on anything else. Rather, He is self-sufficient, needing no other to grant Him existence, nor is it possible for Him to receive existence from another at all. He cannot be a body <note>material substance</note> in any way, nor can He reside within a body. His existence is of a different kind, distinct from the existence of all other beings, sharing no common meaning with them at all. If they share a description, it is in name only, not in the actual meaning understood from that name.

There can only be one such Being. He is the One in truth, and He is the one who bestowed upon all other existents the unity by which we say they "exist." He exists without a cause or reason; He is the First Truth who bestows reality upon others, while His own reality is sufficient unto itself. It is impossible to imagine a perfection greater than His perfection—exalted be He—let alone for such a thing to exist. There is no existence more complete than His, no reality more absolute than His, and no unity more perfect than His.

Furthermore, one should know how all other beings derived existence and reality from Him, and what the portion <note>allotment or share</note> of existence, reality, and unity is for each one of them. One should understand how all other caused causes derived from Him, and know the ranks of all existents: some are first, some are intermediate, and some are final. The final ones have causes but are not causes for anything below them. The intermediate ones have a cause before them and are causes for things below them. The First is the cause for all that is below Him and has no cause above Him. One must know how the final things ascend toward one another until they reach the First, and how the order begins from the First and descends through the existents until it reaches the last of them.

This is wisdom in truth. So, what do you think of such a path, O seeker? Wisdom—may God strengthen you—is vast and majestic; seeking it is both a virtue and a religious duty. This is because it illuminates the intellect and the soul with eternal, beautiful light when they seek it, and it makes them renounce this decaying world once its meaning and essence are understood. It inspires a longing to occupy oneself with that high and sublime world, which is their origin and their point of return, and wherein is their resurrection. There, God shall comfort them and make known to them the cause of the world and its effect, and the reason for manifesting the "cause of the effect" <note>likely referring to the intermediary or Logos</note> which stands between them. Thus, they may know with necessary knowledge that God Almighty is the Cause of the world, the world is His effect, and the reason [for existence] is His service—that they might glorify and worship Him, as He announced: *I have not created jinn and mankind except to worship Me* <note>A quote from the Quran, Surah Adh-Dhariyat 51:56</note>. He sustains them and provides for them so they may thank and praise Him; whoever He wills shall be wretched, and whoever He intends shall be happy, remaining with Him in the bliss that never perishes.

->( Wisdom possesses three specific characteristics )<-

These are essential qualities: it grows and does not vanish; it shines and does not fade; and it reveals itself to be gazed upon and does not withdraw. It also possesses three powers of governance: it restrains, it disciplines, and it does not turn toward those who have no desire for it. Know that this "Result" <note>term: "al-Natija" - The Result. Here referring to the specific philosophical conclusion or the title of a section</note> which we are about to uncover could not exist without the existence of wisdom. The sages rightly called it a "Result," for among the masters of logic, the result is the fruit of the syllogism <note>term: "al-qiyas" - the logical process of deduction</note> and the finest part of the premises. The purpose of setting down these two results was nothing other than to incite the quest for knowledge; therefore, none shall attain it except the wise.

<vocab>Wisdom, Remote Causes, Existents, The One, The First Truth, Unity, Intellect, Soul, The Sublime World, Cause and Effect, Logic, The Result</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces the first treatise on the nature of Wisdom, defining it as the knowledge of ultimate causes and the recognition of God as the First Cause and Absolute One. It describes the hierarchical structure of existence and frames the pursuit of philosophy as a religious and spiritual obligation that illuminates the soul.</summary>
<keywords>Metaphysics, First Cause, Neoplatonism, Islamic Philosophy, Logic, Syllogism, Theology, Divine Unity, Teleology</keywords>

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical and alchemical discourse from the previous section, moving from the logical foundations of reasoning to the practical and occult applications of those principles in magic and alchemy.</meta>

[Page 4]

One who has encompassed knowledge of all branches of wisdom <term>Wisdom: here referring to *Hikma*, a blend of philosophy and science</term> according to their ranks, will find that the status of this final stage of wisdom is like the status of a conclusion in a syllogism <term>Syllogism: a form of logical reasoning where a conclusion is drawn from two premises</term>, which is the finest part of the premises. Understand this, for I have explained to you a wondrous secret. Know that the conclusion is the fruit of the two premises, which is called a "couple" <note>original: *qarinah*</note>, and when it produces a result, it is called a "collector" <note>original: *jami'ah*</note>, or in the language of the Greeks, *syllogismos* <note>original: *sumjus*, a transliteration of the Greek *syllogismos*</note>.

A premise consists of a subject and a predicate. The subject is the "starting noun" according to the grammarians, and the predicate is the "report" or "news." The "report" is that which can be characterized by truth or falsehood. The subject and the predicate—which is that which is predicated—is a statement that is neither limited nor defined, because all statements are of this kind (some statements are not limited or defined, while others are limited and others are defined). I mean that the informative statement is what is used in logical propositions. Other types of speech, according to the scholars, are not used [in logic], such as commands, inquiries, questions, and vocatives, because they do not yield truth or falsehood. To explain what we have mentioned would require a lengthy commentary that would deviate from our purpose; let whoever wishes seek it in its proper places.

## Chapter Two

Know that the "conclusion" is what is expressed by the term Magic <note>original: *Sihr*</note>. Magic is an absolute reality, just as it enchants the minds and as souls from all corners of existence submit to it. Actions [of magic] imply wonder, submission, listening, and approval. It is that which is difficult for the intellect to perceive, and its causes are hidden from the dull-witted. This is because it is a divine power with pre-existing causes set toward your perception; it is a science of obscure comprehension. 

Some of it is also practical, because its subject is "spirit within spirit"—this is *Niranj* <term>Niranj: a type of white magic or sorcery involving spells and illusions</term> and phantasmagoria—just as the subject of the Talisman is "spirit within a body." The subject of Alchemy is likewise "body within a body." In short, magic is that whose cause is mostly hidden and whose derivation is difficult to deduce. 

The reality of the Talisman <note>original: *Talsam*</note> is found in the reversal of its name: it is the "Ruler" <note>The author suggests a linguistic play where *Talsam* reversed sounds like *Musallat* (Ruler/Empowered)</note>, because it is the essence of the Moon. Ruling acts upon that which is composed of it through an act of dominance and subjugation, using numerous proportions, established celestial secrets, and specific bodies at favorable times, along with strengthening incenses that draw the spiritualities of that talisman. Its state is like that of the "Second Conclusion," which is expressed as the Elixir <note>original: *al-Iksir*</note>, which transforms bodies into its own likeness and stabilizes them. For it is a potent leaven that transforms things through their senses. It is a ruler, like a poison that flows through its own kind of bodies and transforms them into itself, in order to flip one person [or substance] into its own person by a power placed within it.

<insert>Know, my brother, that the reality of the leaven</insert> is an elixir composed of earth, air, water, and fire. When contained in its refined vessel, it directs whatever it is placed in toward its own essence and turns it into its own form. It acts upon its contents through the expansion and fragility it has acquired, until its digestion settles in the stomach and it is quickly transformed into nourishment.

Likewise, the Alchemical Elixir acts in this way: the body is quickly transformed into it, and it changes it from one nature to a more noble nature, clothing it in spirit, soul, and solidity, and removing from it corrosion <note>original: *tazanjur*, specifically verdigris or rust</note> and corruption. Such was its secret among the ancients. The word "Elixir" means the power that breaks <note>The author links *Iksir* to the Arabic root *K-S-R*, meaning to break</note> other powers, changing them through dominance and transforming them into its own essence until they become similar to it.

The Elixir only exists within the totality of animals and plants in a certain respect, as they constitute a "world" according to the sages. For through their union, the world exists and one part rectifies the other. Plants cannot subsist by themselves, nor can animals subsist by themselves; rather, they are in need of plants and other things. The mineral is in need of "cooking" and fieriness along with mercurial moisture; only then is it perfected. This is a secret we overlooked in the *Book of the Rank* <note>original: *Kitab al-Rutba*, a famous alchemical work attributed to Maslama al-Majriti</note>. Now I return to our purpose and say:

<summary>This page bridges formal Aristotelian logic (syllogisms and premises) with occult sciences, defining magic, talismans, and the alchemical elixir as the "conclusions" or practical results of these philosophical principles. It explains the transformative nature of the Elixir as a "leaven" that ennobles base metals into a higher state.</summary>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the classification of magic and the cosmological structure of the spheres, transitioning from general definitions to the specific mechanics of celestial influence.</meta>

### ### Magic is Divided into the Theoretical and the Practical

The **theoretical** <note>original: *al-ilmi* - literally "scientific" or "knowledge-based"</note> consists of knowing the positions of the fixed stars, as their positions are the locations of the "forms," and understanding how they cast their rays upon the wandering stars <note>the planets</note>, as well as the state of the celestial proportions when seeking a desired outcome. Under this category falls everything established by the ancients regarding elections <note>choosing auspicious times</note> and talismans.

Know that for whoever chooses to bind a talisman, this knowledge is a necessity. The finest type of theoretical magic is **speech**, and similarly, **gesture**. Indeed, "some speech is surely magic" <note>A famous Hadith: *Inna min al-bayani la-sihra*</note>. Among the proofs of this is the saying of the Supporter, Plato, in the *Book of Aphorisms*: "Just as a friend turns into an enemy through evil speech, so too does an enemy turn into a friend through good speech." Is this not a species of magic?

The **practical** <note>original: *al-amali*</note> refers to the subtle arts dependent on the "generated beings" <note>minerals, plants, and animals</note> and the planetary powers infused within them. These are expressed as "properties" <note><term>khawass: occult properties inherent in natural substances</term></note> by those who advocate for them, though they may not know their cause or reality, nor have the need to uncover the secrets of the ancients. Furthermore, it involves the mixing of some substances with others through labor, seeking thereby an elemental heat—this belongs to the category of **fumigations**, used to aid complete powers over deficient ones. Or, one seeks thereby a natural heat—this belongs to the category of **edibles**. In both cases, they do not exceed these limits and are only assisted by the human or animal soul.

The tricks called *Niranjat* <note><term>Niranjat: a Persian-derived term for white magic, sleight of hand, or illusions produced by natural means</term></note> are the finest types of this practical magic. Know that magic is of two kinds: that which is **acquired** and that which is **mechanical**.

*   **The Acquired:** This is what the Sage of the Lunar Cycle used to support, and it is alluded to by the words of the Almighty: *"Take four birds"* <note>A reference to Quran 2:260 regarding Abraham</note>.
*   **The Mechanical:** This is what the Sage of the Saturnian Cycle used to perform, as well as what the Sage of the Venusian Cycle performed.

The ancient Greeks used to designate *Niranjat* and "the transformation of the eye" <note>optical illusions or glamours</note> with the name *Tarjih* <note>preponderance</note>, and they called the talisman *Saljamus* <note><term>Saljamus: likely a corruption of a Greek term, possibly related to the summoning of spirits</term></note>, which is the drawing down of the powers of the higher spirits. They applied the name "Magic" to all of these. These sages had no power over this science except through the knowledge of astronomy.

### Chapter Three

Know, O observer, that the celestial sphere is a solid orb in the ultimate state of roundness. Likewise, everything within it, in all its states and at all times, is perfectly circular. Some people have imagined that states occur within the sphere that remove it from its circular shape at certain times, but it is impossible for anything of that sort to exist at all. This is because the shape of the sphere is the shape of its Cause. This is because the Soul—this is its form—is the "First Thing Sent Forth." This is because the First Thing, in which there is no corruption, possesses a perfect form. The perfect form is the **circle**, and for this reason, it is constituted from a single line, because it is a First Cause. This subject has a hidden proof.

The sphere, moreover, is an essence, and the degrees <note>of the zodiac</note> within it are merely positional. If we begin with the positional degrees, we ascend from them to the "Necessary Compulsion." The path of instruction for this matter is actually the reverse. Furthermore, the teaching of the path leads to the teaching of the states of the sphere. It is impossible for any of the bodies of the "World of Generation and Corruption" <note>the sublunary realm/Earth</note> to be in the place of the sphere, nor can any part of the sphere be in the place of the World of Generation and Corruption, unless by force.

The sphere, as we have said, is a smooth orb from all sides in the ultimate health of its roundness. The circle is formed from a single curved line, inside of which is a point. All lines extending from this point to the circumference are equal. That point is the **Center**. The reference to these lines is to the "casting of the rays" of the planets upon the World of the Center <note>Earth</note>. This is the influence of talismans; it is like a limit or a design that has been agreed upon. Since the sphere is an orb encompassing the entire world, there is neither void nor plenum beyond it.

The **Ether** is a still form, and it is that which is pointed to... those planets are within its interior, deviating from its center toward the side of the Earth's center, as has been said regarding the inclination. The nature of the sphere is a single nature.

<summary>This page defines the two branches of magic—theoretical (astrological/linguistic) and practical (natural/elemental)—and begins a cosmological explanation of the celestial spheres as perfect, circular bodies that influence the Earth through the casting of rays.</summary>
<keywords>theoretical magic, practical magic, talismans, Plato, Niranjat, celestial spheres, geometry of the universe, planetary rays, Ether, World of Generation and Corruption</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the relationship between celestial movements and earthly effects, transitioning from the general nature of the spheres to the specific requirements for astrological judgment and the construction of talismans.</meta>

The movement of physical bodies possessing natural properties follows the movement of the celestial sphere and the heat generated by it indirectly, for it illuminates the existence of everything in this world.

->(The degrees of the celestial sphere are three hundred and sixty)<-

according to the primary division. Likewise, there are three hundred and sixty forms <note>images or symbols associated with each degree</note>, and within them lies the hidden meaning of astrological judgments, because judgments follow the cause. (Some people have said) and held the view that the celestial sphere itself has no utility, and that the utility comes from the ether in the higher world, which is the stars... and the heat and the form within the degrees themselves... are merely representations of the positions of the stars joined to one another. (Others have said) that the form of the degree is the core of the science of the stars <term>astronomy/astrology: 'ilm al-nujum</term> and is the cause of the existence of all created things. As for the actions of the degrees: when a degree falls at a specific point—any point whatsoever—and a fixed star is located there, and then a wandering star <term>planet: kawkab sayyar</term> reaches that point, then you must recognize the position of that planet regarding the earthly things found here.

It is necessary to know the shape of the equatorial sphere, which is the sphere of names and is expressed as the Throne <note>al-'Arsh: a cosmological term often identifying the outermost encompassing sphere in Islamic Neoplatonism</note>, and the spheres it contains, the movement of the zodiacal sphere <term>ecliptic: falak al-buruj</term>, its physical body, and all its conditions. One must also know the natures of the twelve signs of the zodiac and their specific indications for the beings in this world; the lines belonging to the seven planets within the zone of the zodiacal sphere and related matters; the natures of the seven planets and the two nodes <note>the North and South Nodes of the Moon</note>, their positions in the sphere, and their specific indications for beings in this world; what occurs to the seven planets in themselves and in relation to one another; and the foundational proofs upon which the craft of astrological judgment <term>judicial astrology: ahkam al-nujum</term> revolves, as well as the essential predominance <term>Almuten: al-ibtizaz al-dhati, the planet with the most dignity/power</term> of the seven planets, the levels of their mastery, and the extraction of the "Lots" <note>Arabic Parts/Seham: mathematical points in a chart</note> and their positions in the zodiac. This is the necessary knowledge of the science of the spheres, and it is found in the books of the ancients, so let it be taken from them. Such knowledge reflects the traces of the First Wise One <note>likely referring to Hermes Trismegistus or a similar primordial sage</note> in his saying:

> I am the one who was raised above the seven spheres.

And similar to it. By saying "I was raised," he intended the comprehension of them through the knowledge of his intellectual power, similar to the allusion in the verse: "And We raised him to a high station" <note>Quran 19:57, referring to the Prophet Idris, who is traditionally identified with Hermes</note>.

## Chapter Four

Know, O observer—may the world be your model—that Saturn moves things characterized by coldness and dryness. Jupiter moves things characterized by coldness and moisture <note>Note: Traditionally Jupiter is hot and moist; the author may be following a specific variant tradition or emphasizing its tempering effect</note>. Mars moves things characterized by heat and dryness. Venus moves things characterized by weak heat and dominant moisture. Mercury moves things characterized by weak heat and much dryness. The Moon moves things characterized by coldness and moisture... and the fixed stars are likewise. Whenever a degree is under the influence of a planet that is purely hot but weak in its secondary qualities of dryness and moisture, and the Sun is in that place alone, you should judge according to that matter and its increase. Likewise, if it happens that they act with their combined power, growth will be greater. This is a difficult and highly obscure point among the early sages; for "obscurity" to them in speech meant to veil the literal meaning and hide the inner meaning—this is what they called obscurity. So know this.

## Chapter Five

Furthermore, when people needed to create a talisman <term>talisman: tilsam</term>, they lacked knowledge of the proportions upon which the craft of talisman-making is established, which is the "marking" <note>tashira: likely referring to the specific engraving or timing</note> for their effects. I present to you here the celestial proportions as foundations to build upon, serving as the basis for the work of talismans... for the practitioner of talismans needs to be knowledgeable in celestial balance and proportions, and must, along with this, be certain of the correctness of...

<summary>This page outlines the essential astronomical and astrological knowledge required for making predictions and talismans, emphasizing the qualities of the seven planets and the importance of the 360 degrees of the zodiac. It links the intellectual mastery of the spheres to the spiritual elevation of ancient sages like Hermes/Idris.</summary>
<keywords>zodiacal degrees, seven planets, judicial astrology, Almuten, Arabic Parts, talismans, Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Venus, Mercury, Moon, Hermes Trismegistus</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructional discourse on the practical application of astrological timing and the creation of talismans, specifically focusing on the Lunar Mansions.</meta>

What one performs should be done without any doubt entering into the work, nor any hesitation, so that the action of the *rational soul* <note>The "rational soul" (al-nafs al-natiqa) refers to the highest part of the human psyche in Aristotelian-Islamic philosophy, responsible for intellect and will.</note> may be strengthened by it. Thus, the will of the practitioner is joined with the soul of the worker so that the desired goal may be achieved. I shall also inform you of a vital condition regarding our immediate situation: do not perform any of these works until the Moon is in the degree appropriate for that specific task. For the Moon has manifest effects that are not hidden. I will provide you with a summary of its actions hereafter; for now, I am mentioning the effects of the Moon within the boundaries of its "mansions" <note>The Lunar Mansions (Manazil al-Qamar) are 28 divisions of the sky through which the moon passes each month.</note> according to what the Indians <note>original: "al-Hindiyyun"; referring to the influential Indian astrological tradition in medieval Arabic science.</note> specifically have agreed upon regarding the twenty-eight mansions.

->### The Lunar Mansions<-

**The first of them is the mansion of al-Sharatan** <note>The "Two Signs," identified with the horns of Aries (Beta and Gamma Arietis).</note> (and it extends from the beginning of Aries to twelve degrees, fifty-one minutes, and twenty-four seconds). The people of India, when the Moon is in this mansion, undertake travel and drink purgative medicine. Therefore, make it a rule for a traveler to have a <term>talisman</term> <note>original: "tilsam"; an object inscribed with astrological symbols to harness celestial power.</note> made for them here for safety during their journey. In this mansion, a talisman is also made to cause corruption between a married couple, or to cause separation and enmity between two friends. Also made in it is a talisman for the escape of a slave, or for binding a slave for whomever you wish and intend, and for ruining a partnership between partners, because it is of a **fiery, inauspicious nature**. And I mention a rule for you: always ensure in works of good that the Moon is safe and free from <term>malefics</term> <note>original: "al-nuhus"; refers to the influence of planets like Mars or Saturn.</note> and combustion <note>Combustion occurs when a planet is too close to the Sun, weakening its power.</note>. In works of evil, let it be combust; if it is inauspicious, take note of that.

->### The Second is the mansion of al-Butayn<-

(And it extends from twelve degrees, fifty-one minutes, and twenty seconds, to the completion of twenty-five degrees, forty-two minutes, and twelve seconds of Aries). In it are made talismans for digging wells and rivers, and for extracting hidden treasures and sought-after items—and talismans for the growth of crops. Also made in it is the corruption of marriage between those intended before they unite, and talismans for arousal, because it is of a **fiery, auspicious nature**. It is also used for the escape of a slave, and for tightening the bonds of a prisoner if one intends to harm him.

->### The Third is the mansion of al-Thurayya<-

(And it extends from twenty-five degrees, forty-two minutes, and twelve seconds of Aries, to eight degrees, thirty-four minutes, and two seconds of Taurus). In it is made a talisman for one who travels by water, for he shall be saved. And a talisman suitable for ruining a partnership; and a talisman for loosening the bonds of a prisoner and his release. In it is made a talisman for the successful pursuit of <term>alchemy</term> <note>original: "al-kimiya"; here referring to the practical and spiritual art of transformation involving fire.</note> and the service of fire. And a talisman for land hunting. And a talisman for affection between a married couple. And a talisman for the ruin of sheep, cattle, and slaves against their owner so that they do not remain in his possession, because it is of a **mixed, auspicious nature**; so know this.

->### The Fourth is the mansion of al-Dabaran<-

(And it extends from eight degrees, thirty-four minutes, and two seconds of Taurus, to twenty-one degrees, twenty-five minutes, and forty-four seconds of Taurus). In it is made a talisman for the ruin of a city's condition. And a talisman for a building whose survival is not hoped for and whose condition will not be good. And a talisman for the destruction of crops. And a talisman for the protection of slaves for their owner. And a talisman for ruining the relationship between a married couple and casting separation between them. And talismans for the fear of misfortune for those who dig wells and seek buried treasures. And for the destruction of whomever you wish, and for the binding of snakes and scorpions.

## ( The Fifth Mansion: Al-Haq'ah <note>The "White Spot" in Orion's head</note> — H )

It extends from twenty-one degrees, twenty-five minutes, and forty-four seconds of Taurus to four degrees, seventeen minutes, and ten seconds of Gemini. In this mansion, a talisman <term>Talisman: an object inscribed with astrological symbols to channel celestial power</term> is made for the well-being of children, the growth of their education, and their success in schools and crafts. Also, a talisman for the safety of the traveler and his good condition, and for speeding the duration of travel by water. A talisman for the repair of buildings, and a talisman for the dissolution of partnerships. It is also used for the well-being of spouses and their harmony if the Moon and the Ascendant are in a single sign depicting a human form, provided it is sound and free from malefic influences or "combustion" <note>Combustion: when a planet is too close to the sun, weakening its power</note> as we mentioned. (The signs that take the form of human beings are) Gemini, Virgo, Libra, Sagittarius, and Aquarius.

---

## ( The Sixth Mansion: Al-Han'ah <note>The "Mark" or "Touch" in Gemini</note> — W )

It extends from four degrees, seventeen minutes, and ten seconds of Gemini to seventeen degrees, eight minutes, and thirty-six seconds of Gemini. In it are made talismans for the destruction and siege of cities, for taking revenge against kings, and for inflicting harm and evil upon enemies through every means of ruin. Also, talismans for the destruction of farms, trusts, and deposits, as well as for improving the affairs of partners and improving land hunting. It is also used to spoil the effectiveness of medicines when they are taken.

---

## ( The Seventh Mansion: Al-Dhira' <note>The "Arm" or "Forearm" of the Lion</note> — Z )

It extends from seventeen degrees, eight minutes, and thirty-six seconds of Gemini to the end of Gemini. In it is made a talisman for the growth and blessing of trade, for the growth of crops, and a talisman for the good condition and safety of the traveler by water, and for reconciliation between friends and partners. In this mansion, flies are bound so that they do not enter a place. Whatever industrial work is performed during this time will be spoiled and will require being redone. A talisman is also made here to achieve a wish from a Sultan or a great man you wish to contact. Also, a talisman for the well-being of a runaway slave, and a talisman to remove land, money, a house, or the like from a person's hand.

---

## ( The Eighth Mansion: Al-Nathrah <note>The "Muzzle" or "Nose-fold" in Cancer</note> — H )

It extends from the beginning of Cancer to twelve degrees, fifty-one minutes, and twenty-six seconds thereof. In it are made talismans for reconciliation, love, and friendships between those who hate each other, and for improving the state of partners. Along with this, a talisman is made for the binding of prisoners and captives, the ruin of kingdoms, and a talisman to repel mice and also to repel bedbugs.

---

## ( The Ninth Mansion: Al-Tarfah <note>The "Glance" or eyes of the Lion</note> — T )

It extends from twelve degrees, fifty-one minutes, and twenty-six seconds of Cancer to twenty-five degrees, forty-two minutes, and fifty-one seconds thereof. In it are made talismans for the destruction of farms, the exposure of travelers on land and others intended for harm, the severing of partnerships, and the imprisonment and harming of a demanded adversary.

---

## (The Eleventh Mansion: al-Zubrah — Letter K)

It spans from eight degrees, thirty-four minutes, and eighteen seconds of Leo. <note>The text provides the starting point; the end point is implied by the start of the next mansion.</note> In it, one creates talismans for the release of prisoners and captives, talismans for besieging cities, talismans for the growth of trade, for improving the condition of the traveler, for the stability of buildings, and for improving the affairs of partners.

## (The Twelfth Mansion: al-Sarfah — Letter L)

It spans from twenty-one degrees, twenty-five minutes, and forty-four seconds of Leo to four degrees, seventeen minutes, and six seconds of Virgo <term>al-Sunbulah: literally 'the Ear of Grain', the Arabic name for the zodiac sign Virgo</term>. In it, one creates a talisman for the growth of crops and plantations, and for the destruction of a person's wealth if they are to be harmed thereby. Also, a talisman for the wrecking of ships, a talisman for improving the affairs of partners—it is also suitable for attempting the <term>Alchemical Craft: likely referring to 'al-Sina'a', the Great Work or alchemy</term>—and a talisman for the welfare of slaves or for their escape, depending on what is desired for them.

## (The Thirteenth Mansion: al-Awwa — Letter M)

It spans from four degrees, seventeen minutes, and eight seconds of Virgo to seventeen degrees, eight minutes, and thirty-six seconds of Virgo. In it, one creates talismans for the growth of trade and agriculture, for improving the condition of the traveler, for completing marriages between couples, for the release of the imprisoned, and talismans for gaining access to kings and great men.

## (The Fourteenth Mansion: al-Simak — Letter N)

It spans from seventeen degrees, eight minutes, and thirty-six seconds of Virgo to the completion of thirty degrees of it. In it, one creates a talisman for reconciling spouses, a talisman for completing a cure through medicine, and also talismans for the corruption of farms and plantations, for the destruction of safety, and for bringing fear and misfortune to the traveler. It is also used for the improvement of the servant, the welfare of those aboard ships, and the agreement of partners.

## (The Fifteenth Mansion: al-Ghafr — Letter S)

It spans from the beginning of Libra <term>al-Mizan: the Balance</term> to twelve degrees, fifty-one minutes, and twenty-six seconds of Libra. In it, one creates talismans for digging wells and finding treasures, for hindering a traveler from his journey, for causing separation between spouses, for ruining the friendship of companions, for scattering partners, for the banishment of enemies and their expulsion from their homelands, and for the destruction of estates and houses.

In it, one creates talismans for besieging cities, for triumphing over enemies, for obtaining what one seeks from others, for the destruction of a person's wealth, and for estrangement and separation. One also creates talismans for improving the circumstances of a traveler, for the growth of crops, a talisman for causing a slave to flee from his master, a talisman for the wrecking and breaking of ships, and for discord among partners. Finally, a talisman is made for the escape of the confined and the captive.

It spans from four degrees, seventeen minutes, and ten seconds of Sagittarius to seventeen degrees, eight minutes, and thirty-six seconds of Sagittarius. In it, one creates talismans for improving the condition of a beast that is difficult to train, a talisman for speed and ease of travel, for attracting whomsoever you desire and for intimacy, for tightening the confinement of a prisoner, and for ruining the affairs of partners through other means.

### (The Twenty-First Mansion: al-Baldah — Letter Sh)
<note>al-Baldah: "The City" or "The Region," an empty area of the sky in Sagittarius.</note>

It spans from seventeen degrees, eight minutes, and thirty-six seconds of Sagittarius to the completion of its thirty degrees. In it, one creates talismans for the stability of buildings, the growth of agriculture, and talismans to ensure that wealth, livestock, and beasts remain with their owners. Also created are talismans for the safety of travelers, and a talisman by which a woman is separated from her husband such that she never marries again after him.

<note>Sa'd al-Su'ud: "The Luck of Lucks," generally considered the most auspicious of the "Luck" mansions.</note>

In it, one creates talismans for the siege of cities, talismans for harming enemies, triumphing over them, and inflicting misfortune upon them. It is also used for talismans to seek out spies and capture them, for the separation of spouses, the destruction of crops, and the "binding" <note>a magical restriction of physical function</note> of the vulva and all limbs. In it, the prisoner is bound tighter, and the foundations of buildings are established and made firm through its talismans.

### (The Twenty-Sixth Mansion: The Fore-Spout of the Bucket — Letter D)
<note>al-Far' al-Muqaddam: Literally "The First Spout" of the water-bucket, located in the constellation Pegasus.</note>

In it, one creates talismans of goodness for the gathering and harmonizing of souls through affection, for achieving one's desires while traveling, and for the stability of buildings. It is used for talismans for the safety of those traveling in ships, and also talismans for causing discord between partners and for the binding and punishment of prisoners.

### (The Twenty-Seventh Mansion: The Rear-Spout of the Bucket — Letter Z)
<note>al-Far' al-Mu'akhkhar: "The Second Spout," the stars following the previous mansion.</note>

In it, one creates talismans for the growth of trade, blessing in crops, and the rapid healing of diseases. <meta>This page transitions from the specific catalog of the Lunar Mansions to the foundational principles of talismanic magic. It discusses the astrological requirements for success, the philosophical necessity of the practitioner's intent, and the physical "readiness" of materials to receive magical forms.</meta>

...talismans for the growth of trade and the flourishing of farms, for healing from diseases, for the destruction of deposits <note>likely referring to the loss of stored wealth or entrusted goods</note>, for the safety of travelers, and for reconciliation between spouses. There are also talismans for the binding of prisoners and their punishment, and for striking at ships. Know this well.

->** ( These are the images of the twenty-eight mansions ) **<-

The people of India rely on these in their pursuits and astrological elections; this is what we have found in our readings of their books on this matter, and it is the foundation of what we have presented. The core principle is that the Moon—in whatever works of "good" you attempt—must be clear of **malefics** <term>Nuhus: "unfortunate" planets like Mars or Saturn</term> and **combustion** <term>al-Ihtiraq: being too close to the Sun's heat</term>, and it should be conjoined with **benefics** <term>al-Su'ud: "fortunate" planets like Jupiter or Venus</term>. At the beginning of your works, you should ensure the Moon is moving away from one benefic and connecting to another benefic. In works of "evil," the opposite is true. Know this.

As for our previous statement that the maker of talismans must be certain of the validity of what he does, this is the **readiness** <note>original: "isti'dad"</note> of the practitioner. It is the preparation to receive the targeted, dominant forces that one desires to manifest. This specific preparation only exists within the human condition. 

As for "readiness" in all other substances, it refers to the receptivity of natural materials—such as the way wax is ready to accept the impression of a seal, or the way one suffering from a seizure is affected by the source of the fit; this is a readiness to receive the affliction due to the weakness of the limbs in resisting it. Readiness can be found in weakness as well as in strength. This type of readiness is required for the materials from which talismans are made, for not every material is capable of receiving every intended action. This is a consensus principle among those who have recorded this knowledge. 

When "readiness" and the "preparation to receive" are established, then "acceptance" must follow. When acceptance is established, "union" occurs and the intended effect appears. Union is the imprinting of a "form" <note>the magical intent or image</note> onto "matter." This occurs until the **prime matter** <term>Hyle: the Greek philosophical concept of raw, formless substance</term> and the **form** become one, just as the image of a human sticks to a mirror or water, or like the union of the **Divinity** and the **Humanity** <note>original: "al-Lahut" and "al-Nasut," terms used in Christian theology to describe the nature of Christ</note> as the Christians believe, or the union of the soul with the body. Understand this and observe it well. Know that the purpose of writing these compositions is nothing other than to reveal what the ancients have hidden; God Almighty only places this knowledge in the hands of those He approves of, for such is His way in His actions, Glory be to Him.

Let us return to our purpose. I say: Know that if your work is by day, you should place the Moon in the **Ascendant** <term>Tali': the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, and let the Ascendant be one of the **Diurnal signs**. Likewise, if it is by night, use a **Nocturnal sign**. If the Ascendant is one of the **signs of long ascension** <term>al-mustaqim al-tulu': signs that take longer to rise, generally considered more stable</term>, the work will be easy and effective. If the Ascendant is one of the **signs of short ascension** <term>al-mu'awaj: "crooked" signs that rise quickly</term>, the work will be difficult, unless its condition is rectified or corrupted by the "aspects" (gazes) of the planets—the benefics and malefics—upon it. 

If the Ascendant is of long ascension but a malefic planet aspects it, the work will be corrupted and made difficult. If it is a sign of short ascension but benefics aspect it or are present within it, they will make the work easy. The same applies to Nocturnal and Diurnal signs if they rise out of their proper time (e.g., a Diurnal sign rising at night); if benefics aspect them, they rectify the situation, but if malefics aspect them, they increase the harm. 

Therefore, the talisman practitioner must necessarily know: 
- The "crooked" and "straight" signs (short/long ascension)
- The fixed, cardinal, and mutable signs
- The diurnal and nocturnal signs
- The benefic and malefic planets
- The purity of the Moon from the "accidents" (afflictions) that strike it
- Which planets and signs are suitable for which specific talismanic works.

The practitioner's need is also dire regarding the **Lunar Eclipse**. Beware with all your effort of a Lunar Eclipse in works of "good," or the Moon being "under the sun's rays" until it is "released" from the knot. This **release** <note>original: "al-inhilal"</note> occurs when the Moon is 12 degrees before or after the exact point of the Sun. Beware also if **Mars** or **Saturn** are within these 12 degrees, or if the Moon is descending in southern latitude, or if it is approaching the **Head** or **Tail** <note>The North and South Lunar Nodes, often called the Dragon's Head and Tail</note>, or if the degree of the Sun (or the degree opposite the Sun) is within 12 degrees, for then it is deviated by the meeting of its body with the Sun's body. 

Also, beware if the Moon is "slow in motion," which is when it moves less than 12 degrees in a day, for then it resembles the slow movement of Saturn. Beware if it is in the **Burned Path** <term>al-Tariq al-Muhtaraqa: a specific dangerous area of the zodiac</term>, the harshest part of which is from 18 degrees of Libra to 3 degrees of Scorpio. Finally, avoid the very end of any sign, for that is the "term" of the malefics, or if it is "cadent" from the midheaven into the Ninth House, unless a matter surprises you and you have no choice but to act.

This page continues that technical instruction, providing specific "recipes" for various magical outcomes based on the alignment of the planets and the Houses of the horoscope.</meta>

...and if you are not able to delay the work to wait for the rectification of the Moon, then place **Jupiter** and **Venus** in the **Ascendant** <term>al-Tali: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> or the **Midheaven** <term>Wast al-Sama: the highest point of the southern sky in a chart</term>, for they will protect against the evil of that <note>Meaning the beneficent power of these two planets can compensate for a poorly positioned Moon</note>.

## Chapter Six

Regarding examples of celestial proportions used in the crafting of talismans. One such example is for the gathering of a lover with their beloved and the permanence of their affection. Craft their image during the hour of **Jupiter**, with the **Head** <note>original: "al-Ra's," referring to the North Node of the Moon, considered an increasing and auspicious point</note> in the Ascendant. Let the **Sun** and the **Moon** be with **Venus**, connected to them and in conjunction with them. The ruler of the Ninth House should be connected to the ruler of the Ascendant by a **trine** <term>Tathlith: an auspicious 120-degree aspect</term> or a **sextile** <term>Tasdis: a harmonious 60-degree aspect</term> with **reception** <term>Qabul: a cooperative relationship where one planet is in a sign ruled by the other</term>. Join the two images together in an embrace and bury them in the location of the lover. This may also be crafted for a man who has turned away from his family and whom you wish to return to them.

-> ( A talisman to destroy an enemy ) <-

If you wish to expel someone from their place, craft their image during the hour of **Mars**, while the **Moon** is in **Scorpio** <note>Scorpio is the "fall" of the Moon, where its influence is considered most harsh</note>. Afflict the Ascendant as much as you are able, and afflict its ruler as well. Afflict the **House of Death** <term>Bayt al-Maniyya: the Eighth House of the horoscope</term>. Make the ruler of the Ascendant connect to the ruler of the House of Death, or place the ruler of the Ascendant within the House of Death. Alternatively, let it be connected to a **malefic** <term>Nahs: an unlucky planet, specifically Mars or Saturn</term> in the Fourth or Seventh House. Bury it upside down outside the city.

-> ( A talisman for the ruin of a city or territory ) <-

Create an image based on the Ascendant of that city. Afflict its **House of Life** <note>The First House</note> and its House of Death, as well as the ruler of the Ascendant, the Moon, the ruler of the Moon's sign, and the ruler of the sign containing the Ascendant's ruler. Afflict the Tenth House and its ruler, then bury it in the center of the city.

-> ( A talisman to restore a city or place ) <-

Craft a talisman using a fortunate Ascendant. Fortify the Tenth House and its ruler, as well as the Second and Eighth Houses. Fortify the ruler of the Ascendant and the ruler of the sign it occupies. Fortify the Moon and its ruler, then bury it in the center of the city; you will see a wonder.

-> ( Another talisman for the ruin of a city or place ) <-

This is to be crafted during the hour of **Saturn** while it is malefic. Afflict the city’s Ascendant and the ruler of the Ascendant's ruler. Ensure the **benefics** <term>al-Su'ud: fortunate planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> are hidden from the Ascendant and its ruler. Keep the benefics away from the **triplicities** of the Ascendant and away from the **angles** <term>al-Awtad: the four most powerful houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th)</term>. Bury it in the center of the city.

-> ( A talisman for the growth of wealth and trade ) <-

Craft an image after fortifying the Ascendant, the Tenth House, their rulers, and their dispositors, as well as the Moon and its ruler. Fortify the Second House and its ruler. Ensure the ruler of the Second House is connected to the ruler of the Ascendant by a trine or sextile with reception between them. Fortify the Second House and place the **Lot of Fortune** <term>Sahm al-Sa'ada: a calculated point in a chart representing prosperity</term> in the Ascendant or the Tenth House. Let the ruler of the **Lot of Wealth** aspect the Lot of Wealth itself. Fortify the Eleventh House and its ruler. Whoever possesses this image will be among the wealthiest of creation; every trade they attempt will be made easy for them, and they shall profit in all their movements.

-> ( A talisman for a significant and high authority ) <-

Craft these talismans after fortifying the Ascendant, the Tenth House, and its ruler. Ensure the malefics are hidden from the Ascendant and its ruler. Make the ruler of the Eleventh House a fortunate planet that aspects the Ascendant and its ruler, and ensure the ruler of the Tenth House is connected to the ruler of the Ascendant.

-> ( A talisman to attain authority over whomever you wish ) <-

And for the elevation of one's status: Craft an image in his name during a most fortunate **Ascendant** <term>al-Tali: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> with a strong **Benefic** <term>al-Sa’d: a lucky planet like Jupiter or Venus</term>. Let the Benefic be neither **retrograde** <term>raji’: appearing to move backward</term>, nor **cadent** <term>saqit: in a weak house of the chart</term>, nor **combust** <term>muhtariq: too close to the Sun's heat</term>. The Ruler of the Ascendant must be powerful, in good condition, and direct in its movement. Let the Ruler of the **Tenth House** <note>The house of career, kingship, and public standing</note> be connected to the Ruler of the Ascendant by a **trine** <term>Tathlith: a harmonious 120-degree aspect</term> or a **sextile** <term>Tasdis: a harmonious 60-degree aspect</term>, and let it bring him fortune. The Ruler of the Tenth must be the one applying to the Ruler of the Ascendant with an aspect of **reception** <term>Qabul: a cooperative relationship where planets host one another in their respective signs</term>. Place the Ruler of the Ascendant in the **commanding signs** <note>The signs from Aries to Virgo</note> and the Ruler of the Tenth in the **obedient signs** <note>The signs from Libra to Pisces</note>. Whoever keeps this image with himself, no person of authority shall encounter his intent without leaning toward him entirely and elevating his status.

-> ( A talisman for a servant seeking to win the favor of his master ) <-

One should craft two talismans. The first during the hour of a **superior planet** <note>The outer planets: Saturn, Jupiter, or Mars</note> while the Moon is increasing in light and brilliance, and the **Head** <note>al-Ra's: the North Node of the Moon, associated with increase</note> is in the Ascendant or one of the **Angles** <term>al-Awtad: the four most powerful houses in a chart (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th)</term>. The second talisman should be made during the hour of an **inferior planet** <note>The inner planets: Venus, Mercury, or the Moon</note>, with the Ascendant being the tenth sign from the first Ascendant, and the **Tail** <note>al-Dhanab: the South Node of the Moon, associated with decrease or release</note> in the Ascendant or one of the Angles. Then, join them in an embrace and bury them in the location of the seeker of favor; he shall return to him entirely and fulfill all his needs.

-> ( A talisman for one who desires marriage but is prevented from it ) <-

Craft two figures. One during the hour of **Jupiter** with the Ascendant in **Virgo** and the Moon increasing in light within the Angles. The second during the hour of **Venus**, with Venus looking toward Jupiter, while **Saturn** and **Mars** <note>original: "Bahram," a Persian name for Mars</note> are cadent from the Ascendant. Let the Ascendant be the **Seventh House** <note>The house of marriage and partnerships</note> of the first figure, and let its significator be connected to the significator of the first Ascendant from the place of its **triplicity** <term>Muthallatha: a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element</term>. Then join them in an embrace and bury them in the seeker's location.

-> ( A talisman to hinder a man seeking to marry another woman ) <-

To prevent a man from taking a second wife against his current wife: Craft two talismans, one with **Leo** as the Ascendant during the hour of the **Sun**, and the other with **Cancer** as the Ascendant during the hour of the **Moon**. Let the Moon be increasing in light, free from **malefics** <note>Difficult planets like Saturn or Mars</note>, and swift in its course. Bury it in the Tenth House of the city’s chart, facing the image toward the house of the **Imam** <note>The religious or community leader who would perform the marriage</note>.

-> ( A talisman to release a prisoner ) <-

Craft this during the hour of the **Moon** while it is increasing in light, free from malefics, and swift in its course.

-> ( A talisman for victory over an enemy whose destruction you seek ) <-

Craft two images. One with **Leo** as the Ascendant during the hour of the **Sun** while the Moon is cadent. The other with **Cancer** as the Ascendant during the hour of **Mars** <note>original: "Bahram"</note> while he is cadent and the Moon is in his triplicity. Make the first image take revenge upon the second with whatever type of vengeance you desire. Bury them during the hour of **Mars** with the Ascendant in **Aries**, in its first **face** <term>Wajh: a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign, also known as a decan</term>. Then seek his destruction in any way you wish, and he shall perish within the hour.

-> ( A talisman for a governor of a land whose subjects have rebelled ) <-

So that they may become pleased with him: Craft two talismans. One during the hour of **Jupiter** with the Moon connected to the **Sun** in a favorable, sound form, free from malefics. Place the **Dragon's Head** <note>al-Jawzahar: the North Node</note> in the Ascendant or have it aspecting the Ascendant. The second talisman should be made with the Ascendant being the **Fifth House** <note>The house of joy and messengers</note> from the first, during the hour of **Venus**, and let Venus...

->( A talisman to keep whomever you wish in his city )<-

Let the **Dragon's Head** <term>al-Ra's: the North Node of the Moon, considered a point of increase</term> be in one of the **Angles** <term>al-Awtad: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a chart</term>, with the Ascendant within it. Bury it in the center of the city upside down while the Ascendant is fixed. He shall not leave that city as long as the talisman remains.

->( A talisman to expel a man from his city on that very day )<-

Craft an image during a **Movable Sign** <term>Tali' Munqalib: a Cardinal sign like Aries or Cancer, signifying change</term>. Let its **Significator** <term>Dalil: the planet representing the person in the chart</term> be **cadent** <term>za'il: located in a weak house, away from the power centers of the chart</term> from the Angles, and the Moon also cadent from the Angles. Bury it in the section of the **Burned Way** <term>al-Tariq al-Muhtariqah: a specific unlucky area of the zodiac, usually between 15° Libra and 15° Scorpio</term>. Point the face of the statue toward the direction where you wish to exile him.

->( A talisman for the meeting of two people and their harmony )<-

Craft two talismans. One is for the **Querent** <term>al-Mas'alah: the person asking for the work</term>; fortify the Ascendant and the **Tenth House** <note>The house of public standing and success</note>. Keep the Malefics away from the Ascendant and make the Ruler of the **Eleventh House** <note>The house of friends and hopes</note> a **Benefic** planet <term>Kawkab Sa'id: a lucky planet like Venus or Jupiter</term> connecting to the Ruler of the Ascendant through a **Trine** <term>Tathlith: a harmonious 120-degree angle</term> or a **Sextile** <term>Tasdis: a harmonious 60-degree angle</term> while in **Reception** <term>Qabul: a cooperative state where planets host one another</term>. 

I remind you here of what you must do: Understand that the reason a Trine or Sextile is considered an aspect of affection and harmony is that a Trine occurs between a **Fiery sign** and another Fiery sign, Earth to Earth, Air to Air, and Water to Water. Thus, it is an aspect of friendship and love <note>The author refers to the shared "triplicities" of elements</note>. A Sextile is from Fire to Air or from Earth to Water; since they agree in **active qualities** <note>Fire and Air are both "Hot"; Earth and Water are both "Cold"</note> but differ in passive ones, it is also an aspect of friendship and love. Conversely, a **Square** <term>Tarbi': a tense 90-degree angle</term> is Water to Fire or Air to Earth. Since they differ entirely in nature, the Square becomes an aspect of distance and hatred. 

(Returning to our topic, I say): Craft the second talisman. If it is for a friend, use the Ascendant of the Eleventh House; if it is for a husband or wife, use the Ascendant of the **Seventh House** <note>The house of marriage and partnership</note>. Make the Significator of the one you wish to win over connected to the Ruler of the First House with Reception between them. Bury the two talismans together in the location of the person seeking affection. They shall meet and never part.

->( A talisman for separation and enmity )<-

Craft an image during the Ascendant of the Querent. Afflict the Ruler of the Ascendant with a strong Malefic and afflict the Tenth House with a strong Malefic as well. Let the Ruler of the Ascendant and the Ruler of the Tenth be afflicted by an **Opposition** <term>Muqabalah: a 180-degree angle of conflict</term> or a Square. There should be no Reception between them. Remove the **Benefics** <note>original: al-Su'ud</note> from the Ascendant and from the Tenth House. Bury them in the residence of one of them during a Fixed Sign afflicted by the **Dragon's Tail** <term>al-Dhanab: the South Node of the Moon, associated with loss and decrease</term> or a strong Malefic. They shall separate and never reunite due to the great hatred that will arise between them.

->( A talisman for the destruction of a ruler )<-

For the one who employs it: Craft the image according to the proportions previously mentioned for this talisman. Make the Ruler of the Ascendant **separating** <term>munsarif: moving away from an aspect</term> from the Ruler of the Tenth while afflicted by it, and connected to the **Lord of Death** <note>The ruler of the Eighth House</note>, who also afflicts it through an Opposition or **Conjunction** <term>Mujama'ah: occupying the same degree</term>. Bury them likewise during a Fixed Sign that is afflicted. He will surely kill his servant in any case and for any reason.

Craft two talismans when **Venus** <note>original: al-Zuharah</note> is in the Ascendant in the first **Decan** <term>Wajh: a 10-degree subdivision of a sign</term> of **Cancer**, and the Moon is in the first Decan of **Taurus**. Let Venus be...

### ( Another talisman for permanent love )

You craft two talismans during a fortunate **Ascendant** <term>Tali' Sa'd: a rising sign ruled by beneficial planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> with the Moon in Taurus, and likewise Venus in it as well. You draw on the first image two hundred and twenty—220—as a number of *Alifs* <note>the first letter of the Arabic alphabet</note> or circles, and on the second, two hundred and eighty-four—284—likewise as a number of *Alifs* or circles. Then join them together as if embracing, and permanent love will occur, and the desire between them will strengthen. This talisman is known as the **Talisman of Amicable Numbers** <term>al-A'dad al-Mutahabbah: pairs of numbers, like 220 and 284, where the sum of the divisors of one equals the other, used in magic to symbolize perfect friendship</term>.

### ( A talisman for catching fish )

You fashion an image of a fish during the Ascendant of **Pisces** <note>original: al-Hut</note> with Jupiter within it and during the Hour of Venus. The first thing you make of it is:

-> Its head <- -> Its body <- -> Its tail <-

Then assemble them at the [astrologically appointed] time. Fashion a slender silver pillar for it that holds onto the side of the fish. Then make a lead vessel and place the pillar upright within it with the fish at its tip. Fill the vessel with water and seal it, with the water and the talisman inside. Seal the mouth of the vessel tightly so that no water escapes. Then throw the vessel to the bottom of that river, and the fish shall come from every direction toward that talisman.

### ( A talisman to repel scorpions )

Fashion the image of a scorpion out of gold while the Ascendant is one of the **Angles** <term>al-Awtad: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, which are the most powerful points in a chart</term> such as Taurus, Aquarius, or Leo. The best of these is Leo because its nature <note>a hot, dry Fire sign</note> opposes the nature of the **Scorpion** <note>a cold, moist Water sign</note>. Let the Sun be in Leo during the Hour of the Sun while **Saturn** is **retrograde** <term>raji': appearing to move backward in the sky, a state often used in magic to reverse or undo a situation</term>. You make first:

-> The tail <- -> The legs <- -> The claws <-

Finally, understand this reversal for the repelling: when you have finished, attach the left claw in the place of the right, and the right in the place of the left. Set the head in its place, as well as the tail, and place the stinger. Then place it upside down on its back.

->( A talisman for scorpion stings )<-

One engraves the image of a scorpion onto a yellow sapphire gemstone during the hour of the Moon and the Sun, with the Sun in **Scorpio** at its first degree, and the **Ascendant** <term>Tali': the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> being Leo, Taurus, or Aquarius. Set it into a gold ring and use it to stamp chewed **frankincense** <note>original: kundur</note> during the prescribed hour while the Moon is in Scorpio. Give the stamped impression to the person who was stung to drink, and they will be cured of their pain.

->( A talisman for harmony between men and women )<-

Craft a talisman in the image of a young woman from a cold, dry metal while the Ascendant is **Virgo** <note>original: al-Sunbulah</note>. Within it, **Mercury** <note>original: 'Utarid</note> should be ascending toward its apogee, holding dominion over its governance. Begin during the hour of Mercury until it concludes, and seek help from craftsmen. Then craft another in the form of a man when Mercury has returned to Virgo. Beware of a discrepancy in the Ascendant, lest Mercury be in Virgo while the Ascendant is Gemini, or Mercury be in Gemini while the Ascendant is Virgo. Embrace the necks of the two figures and place each image’s hand upon its companion. The work should be done in the hour of Mercury, with the Ascendant being either Gemini or Virgo, and the other sign being of the same nature. Bury them in the busiest road in the city, for women and men will then grow fond of one another. Similarly, you may do this for one specific person with another and bury it in a place they both pass over.

We have mentioned to you wondrous matters regarding celestial ratios. We have deposited in this treatise those things which, if you reflect upon them, would allow you to perform every talisman ever made in the world. (The substances of the talismans we mentioned) should be similar to what is required for the intended work, whether for good or evil. Following this, I will present to you what has been apportioned to the planets—including minerals, animals, plants, incenses, foods, and sacrifices. All of these are matters used for assistance, just as a physician utilizes a multitude of materials like food and medicine, and as the patient accepts the treatment to complete his success and reach what he hoped for through his efforts. The core of the matter rests upon the accuracy of **astronomical observation** <term>al-rasd: the precise tracking of celestial bodies</term.

(And) the sages of Greece used to observe the planets when they were with **Venus** <note>original: al-Zuharah</note>. They would place it at the degree of the Midheaven, burn its incense, recite its invocation, and offer its sacrifice for the thing they desired, casting it forth so their request would succeed.

(Likewise) they claimed they would do the same if it were with the **Tail of the Dragon** <term>Dhanab al-Jawzahr: the descending lunar node, often associated with spiritual or transformative effects</term>, for it has no latitude then, especially if the planet has a strong testimony in the nativity <note>the birth chart</note> of the requester; for then it is more manifest in influence and more readily accepted. The degree of this work is noble, though seekers of it in our time are few, blind to its pursuit and the methods of achieving it. (And) we had alluded in our book titled ***The Rank of the Sage*** <note>original: Rutbat al-Hakim, an alchemical work also attributed to the author of the Picatrix</note> that encompassing the results of the "artificial" <note>magical or alchemical works</note> is higher and more noble than encompassing this result. (Know) that we only pointed that out regarding natural science and its attainment according to the nature of the sciences, without estimating what comes before or what is delayed... as for what the matter itself and complete philosophy require, (it is) if we were to take two men, one a practitioner knowledgeable in this result and the other a practitioner knowledgeable in the other result, and both were devoid of [other] sciences but had acquired them through imitation, then the one knowledgeable in the magical result is not...

This is self-evident to the intellect of anyone who contemplates it, and it is a matter already concluded. However, you should know that the hierarchy [of science] exists only because of the desperate need to use the sciences according to their true realities and to direct them according to their natural properties.

## (Chapter Seven)

Know that wisdom is exceedingly noble, and attaining it is even nobler. Wisdom exists in degrees, one serving as a ladder to the next. The **Perfect Man** <term>al-Insan al-Kamil: the philosophical ideal of a human who has realized all spiritual and intellectual potentials</term> is the one who encompasses the fruits of wisdom by acquiring it, for he is the true lover of it. Truly, what did they find in philosophy when they defined its limits by its nobility, stating that it is the "preference for wisdom"? Anyone who falls short of this rank is not truly counted as a human, even if they are human by name <note>literally "by allusion" or "metaphor"</note>. This is because such a person does not look into the reality of their own existence: that they are a **Microcosm** <term>al-alam al-asghar: "Small World"</term> corresponding to the **Macrocosm** <term>al-alam al-akbar: "Large World"</term>.

The reality of Man is that he is a complete being possessing three souls: the **rational**, the **vegetative**, and the **animal**. He alone possesses all three, distinguished from all other animals by the "rational" soul. In this context, "rational" <note>original: natiqah</note> means "discerning," for it is this soul that facilitates the arts and industries, brings the absent to mind, composes images, and perceives what it has not even willed. It brings existing things and distant lands into the mind; it binds heard things within its faculties and essence, and sees in sleep what exists in wakefulness. 

Man is the "Small World" contained within the "Large World," harmonizing with it because his form is connected to its forms. All things are found within him. He shares traits with all animals but differs from them through sciences and clever stratagems. He possesses six directions of movement, and his spine and thighs align in a straight line. He dies by nature but lives by accidental means. He has divided fingers and shoulders, a luminous head, and nails. He is proportioned, capable of learning sciences and writing, and an inventor of industries. His gait mimics animals, yet they cannot mimic him. He is a being that laughs and cries—using tears to express grief. 

In him is the power of form and civil politics. He is an **idol** <note>original: sanam; here meaning the physical vessel or statue</note> whose interior is light; thus, his body is his statue and the dwelling of his soul. He is straight in his design, discerning what harms him and what benefits him. He acts with intention when he manages affairs and understands with purpose; he exercises theoretical and intellectual restraint. He invents subtle industries and wondrous, miraculous **talismans** <term>tilasmat</term>, and he preserves the forms of the sciences. 

He is forbidden from being purely sensual, for God has made him the treasurer of His wisdom and the interpreter of Himself and all His creations. He is the recipient of His revelation and the vessel for His sciences. He is the "offspring" of the Macrocosm, a copy that gathers all meanings within his creation and composition. Other creatures are scattered, but he gathers them; he is not gathered by them. He subdues them, but they do not subdue him. He imitates their sounds and represents their forms with his hand and describes them with his tongue, explaining their natures. No animal can change itself or shift from its innate creation to imitate another; a rooster does not cease its crowing, nor a dog its barking, nor a lion its roaring. Yet Man changes his voice and his character, taking on whatever likeness he wishes, whispering to himself and others. 

He is the possessor of a dense body and a subtle spirit. Part of him is subtle and part is dense; part is subtle and alive, and part is dense and dead. Half of him is motion and half is stillness; half is "engraved" <note>perhaps meaning "formed" or "featured"</note> and half is void. Half of him is night and half is day; half is darkness and half is light. Half is manifest and half is hidden; half is sensible and half is inverted. Half is the carrier and half is the carried. He feels shame at the base and is embarrassed; he understands what he wills and feels regret. 

He is composed of the subtle and the coarse of essence. In him is the coarseness of Earth, the subtlety of Air, the fineness of Light <note>original: al-nahar, literally "day" or "daylight"</note>, and the coldness of Water. Therefore, he became balanced through movement, which is the spirit of life. He knows the heat of fire through the "fire-nature" potentially within him; he knows the coldness of water through his own coldness, and likewise for the other elements. Furthermore, his head is like the celestial sphere in its shape, its roundness, and the gathering of subtleties and lights within it—sight, hearing, smell, taste, and speech. His two eyes are like the two luminaries <note>the Sun and Moon</note>, his nostrils like the two winds, and his ears like the East...

<summary>This page introduces Chapter Seven, focusing on the philosophical concept of Man as the "Microcosm" (Small World). It details how human beings uniquely combine the rational, vegetative, and animal souls, serving as a mirror to the entire universe and a vessel for divine wisdom.</summary>
