<meta>This page continues the detailed exploration of the human being as a "microcosm," a small-scale reflection of the entire universe. While the previous page established the philosophical necessity of this view, this page provides a specific anatomical and functional mapping, linking body parts to celestial bodies, numbers, and metaphysical powers.</meta>

...and the West. His feet are like the day, and his back is like the night. His walking is like the movement of the stars, his sitting is like their stationary state, his speed is like their descent, and his death is like their burning out. His internal organs <note>lit. "inner limbs"</note> are seven, like the **seven planets** <term>al-kawakib al-sab'a: the classical planets—Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn—which were believed to govern different aspects of life</term>. In his head, the most great features are seven, like the number of the seven days. In his back are twenty-four vertebrae, corresponding to the number of hours in the day and night. He has twenty-eight joints, matching the number of the **mansions of the moon** <term>manazil al-qamar: the 28 divisions of the sky through which the moon passes during its monthly cycle</term> and the letters of the alphabet. In his belly, there are shrines numbering the days of the lunar cycles. Within him are 360 pulsing veins <note>referring to the arteries</note> and an equal number of resting veins, matching the number of days and nights in a year and the degrees of the celestial sphere. Within him are temperaments numbering the seasons of the year. 

His eyes are the spies of the **Rational Faculty** <term>al-quwwa al-natiqah: the uniquely human power of speech and logical thought</term>, which acts as a King. His ears are the gatherers of its news, and the tongue is its translator. The heart is the administrative bureau of its work, while the stomach is its treasury—it is the cooking pot of the body. The liver is its scribe, and the gall bladder is its patience <note>The Arabic word "sabruhu" can mean "his patience," but also implies "bitterness," referring to the bitter bile that regulates the humors</note>, preventing the flesh from bursting; it is the salt of the body and its medicine. The lung is its fan, the hands are its chamberlains, and the legs are its mount. The flesh of his body is like soil, his bones like mountains, his hair like plants, his veins like valleys, and his internal organs like minerals. 

<insert>And his body</insert> is composed of nine essences built upon nine circles, nested one within the other. The surrounding sphere is a wall for them, which consists of: flesh, bone, muscle, nerve, marrow, skin, hair, and nail. 

The marrow lies within the bones; its function is to preserve the faculties and to soften the dryness of the bone. The function of the bones is to hold the flesh and provide it stability. The function of the muscles is to bind the joints and move the limbs. The function of the flesh is to fill the gaps of the body and protect the bones so they do not separate or break. The function of the veins is to gather blood and carry it to the extremities of the body. The function of the blood is to hold heat, regulate life, balance the **temperament** <term>mizaj: the medical balance of the four humors</term>, and generate movement. The function of the skin is to surround the entire body and everything within it, like a defensive wall. The function of the nails is to protect and hold the tips [of the fingers] so they do not spread or break. 

In his structure are twelve openings, mirroring the twelve signs of the zodiac. Just as six of the signs are southern and six are northern, there are six openings on the right side of a human and six on the left side, matching them in both quantity and quality. And just as there are seven wandering planets in the heavens through which the decrees of the spheres and created beings are enacted, and by which the order of all existence is maintained, <insert>likewise</insert> there are seven active forces in the human body emanating from the human soul, through which the well-being of the body is achieved. 

Just as these planets have souls, bodies, and spiritual actions that influence visible existence—minerals, animals, and plants—likewise there are physical forces found in the human body that ensure its survival and well-being. These are supported by seven other psychological forces: the Retentive, the Attractive, the Digestive, the Expulsive, the Nutritive, the Growth-oriented, and the Formative. There are also seven spiritual forces matching the spiritualities of the seven planets; these are the sensory faculties through which human perfection and the completion of actions are achieved, just as the seven planets are the adornment of the sphere, its foundation, and the order of the world. These are the powers of Sight, Hearing, Smell, Taste, Touch, Speech, and Reason. 

<insert>Among these powers,</insert> two resemble the Sun and the Moon. The moon takes its light from the sun in its twenty-eight mansions; likewise, the Faculty of Speech takes meanings of existence from the Faculty of Reason, then expresses them through the twenty-eight letters of the alphabet. And just as there are two "nodes" in the heavens—the **Head and the Tail** <term>al-ra's wa al-dhanab: the North and South lunar nodes where eclipses occur; often personified as a dragon in medieval astrology</term>—which are hidden in essence but manifest in their actions and effects, likewise two things of a similar nature are found in the human body: the sickness and the health of the temperament. 

Furthermore, if the Soul inclines toward its [celestial] world, its actions become sound, it is purified from the murkiness of nature, and it finds guidance. But if it inclines toward [material] nature, its actions become disordered and its flaws are revealed. Just as the Sun and Moon are obscured by the "Tail" node <note>original: 'uqdat al-dhanab; referring to a lunar eclipse</note>, so too do difficult matters and destruction arise from a poor temperament. Through a sound temperament, the Rational Faculty is perfected. When the structure of the body is healthy and follows the natural order, the soul becomes pure, radiant, and illuminated. 

And since the Sun and the Moon are the lamps of the heavens, the two eyes are found in the body as its lamps. Through them, the soul perceives the forms of existing things, colors, and visible objects via the light of the Sun and Moon. This is the same for all animals. And just as the circle of the heavens and its zodiac signs have boundaries, faces, and degrees...

<meta>This page continues the analogy between the human body and the cosmos, shifting from a physical mapping of organs to a functional and metaphysical comparison. It explores how the human soul and will mirror the Universal Soul and Divine Will, while distinguishing between the "particular" physical human and the "universal" eternal human.</meta>

Likewise, there exist in the joints of the body and the limbs of the frame veins of various descriptions. And just as the powers of the **Universal Soul** <term>al-nafs al-kulliyya: in Neoplatonic philosophy, the soul that animates the entire cosmos</term> emanate through the seven planets and the twelve signs of the zodiac as spiritual forces with specific actions for each planet and sign—descending to the world at every moment, hour, and minute of time's movements—so too does the human soul have works and actions in its body and joints. These actions appear and emerge with every movement, every glance, and every breath, persisting as long as the person exists in essence until the moment the soul departs the body. Similarly, the Universal Soul is connected to the particular souls, and their existence lasts for a measured duration according to a governing wisdom.

(**Among the wonders of man**) is that he is a **manager** <term>mudabbir: an administrator or governor who organizes affairs through reason</term> by nature, so that he may govern himself, his household, his attendants, his family, and the people of his kingdom. A single word can please him so much that he might even abdicate his throne, or it can enrage him until he fights and rushes into danger. He wears clothing, treats himself with medicine, and consumes drugs and herbs to balance his temperament and mend himself. He may display friendship while concealing enmity, and treat his enemy as a brother. He is a creature that dives into the air <note>perhaps referring to the breath or intellectual heights</note> yet remains bound to the earth. 

This is the description of the **particular man** <term>al-insan al-juz'i: the specific, individual human being we see and touch</term> who is sensible and fleeting in his parts, but not in his whole. He has become a particular human because of the "accidents," changes, and temporal states that have affected him—and these are what perish. (**As for**) the **Universal Man** <term>al-insan al-kulli: the archetypal or ideal human being existing in the realm of the intellect</term>, he remains through the existence of his intellect, whether the particular man is knowledgeable or ignorant. He is like the **element** <term>al-istiqas: derived from the Greek "stoicheion," meaning a fundamental building block of matter</term> which perishes in its part but not in its whole. For example, when water changes, it may turn into air, then it decays and returns to its natural state; it perishes due to an "accident" <note>an external quality or change</note> but remains in its essential nature. Likewise, the particular man is nullified by dissolution but returns to his universality. Therefore, the "Whole" exists through the intellect, not through the senses. The "Intelligible" aspect of him is one; it does not change in its essence nor transform, even if the "accidents" attached to it change as it divides toward sensory perfection.

I will provide an example for you to understand this meaning, for it is a strange saying: each of the **elements** <term>al-istiqassat: the primary elements (earth, water, air, fire)</term> became an element because each was arranged in its proper place and according to the accidents that befall it within its specific rank. Water, for instance, acquired this cold nature because of its place and the attributes that follow it in the order of creation. However, its primary existence, which preceded its status as a physical element, remains in its universality. These universals exist in their own essence whether man understands and finds them or not, for existence is essential to them. The same is true for the Universal Man: existence is essential to him. The proof of the truth of what I have told you is that in water there is fire, and in fire there is water, because the elements are smelted and inseparable from one another. Were it not so, nothing would be hidden or stored within them.

Likewise, the attachment of human wills and volitions to the **Primary Wills** <term>al-mashi'at al-ula: the divine or cosmic creative will</term> is like the attachment of created beings to existence. Created things follow them, even if this is not apparent to the particular human. Even if a person's will and volition are not carried out in all his affairs and movements, they are carried out in some. Their influence on the "Whole" is not apparent to him unless he is one of those who has training and expertise in the **theoretical sciences** <term>al-ulum al-nazariyya: abstract or philosophical sciences like metaphysics and mathematics</term>; only then does it become clear to him.

(**Know**) that if it were possible for man to find fire in its pure state, stripped of accidents, conditions, and everything that attaches to it when it mingles with its "sisters" <note>the other elements</note>, he would find it to be non-burning. This is because its "particular" action—burning—is shared with accidents, conditions, and location. Do you not see that we find fire outside of its natural place when it is attached to an object? When it leaves the body or the wood, it returns to its element and its place above the air. (**As for its simple, universal action**), it is beyond description, beyond being grasped by any power or spoken by any tongue, because it is a supreme and magnificent force. It is above simplicity and above quality. The same is true for all other elements.

Man follows this same course. This is the man who took the **covenant in eternity** <note>original: "al-ahd fi al-azaliyya"; a reference to the "Day of Alast" where souls pledged loyalty to God before the creation of the world</note>, and around him the composite man revolves. It is his mixing and blending with all the subsequent things that attached to him by way of "accidents"—in various states and locations from which he cannot be separated—that changed his form, altered him, and made him share traits with animals, plants, and elements. Because of this, he became similar to all things.

It uses the nature of elements like fire and water to explain how the human soul and will are connected to the broader governing forces of the cosmos.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the physiological and psychological mapping of the human being to a metaphysical hierarchy. It establishes the "Universal Human" as the spiritual archetype of which the physical person is merely a shell. This framework is presented as the essential theoretical foundation for the practice of talismanic magic, citing Plato’s Timaeus and Euclid as precedents for concealing such deep truths from the uninitiated.</meta>

When this capacity is understood, and one follows the path of governance <term>tadbir: the skillful management of one's faculties and the soul's affairs</term> that leads to the lights of the **First Principle** <term>al-mabda' al-awwal: the Neoplatonic source of all existence, often equated with the Divine One</term>—which is his specific order—

then the essential nature which is his origin allows his humanity to be distinct from others in proportion to his liberation <note>referring to the soul's liberation from material constraints</note>. All of this is achieved through the practice of virtues according to his ability.

Therefore, the particular, living, physical, corporeal, dense human—composed of a soul, a particular intellect, and a body—is the shared, perishable image in this lower world. It is but an **effigy** and a **husk** for that **Universal Human**: the spiritual, noble, subtle, simple, and primordial Intellect. That is the pure Form <note>original: "al-sura al-mahda"</note> that does not reside in a body and remains eternally in that upper world.

(**The form of the Universal Human**) has its inward reality within the form of the particular human, serving as its simple essence and its primary matter. (**The form of the particular human**) has its inward reality within the physical body, serving as its simple essence. 

The image of the body is a composite effigy and a husk for the image of the particular human. 
The image of the particular human is a composite effigy and a husk for the image of the Universal Human. 
The image of the Universal Human is an effigy and a husk for the image of the **Universal Soul**. 
The Universal Soul is an effigy and a husk for the **Universal Intellect**. 
The Universal Intellect is an effigy and a husk for the **Light** from which the Intellect originated. 

Light is the primary matter <term>hayula: the fundamental, formless substance of reality</term> for the Universal Intellect. Likewise, everything below the highest is always the primary matter and the simple essence for what is beneath it in relation to it. That which is below is always a form for that which is above it and a composite in relation to it. Truly, the human being is the composite psychic form that makes use of the physical bodies united by nature. 

Whoever wishes to know this in truth should be virtuous, pure in mind and body from all defilements. If he is so, he shall see this and witness it with a true witnessing. We have not strayed from our intended purpose with this discourse; rather, it is the remaining foundation for the subject of this book, as it is the **basis for the science of talismans** <term>al-talsamat: the study of creating magical objects that harness celestial powers</term>. If you have trained in the sciences and verified this, you will have known it and realized that it is **manifest magic**.

Upon this foundation, that man who excelled in the sciences and was foremost in virtue—**Plato**—built his book called *Timaeus*. In it, he spoke at great length regarding "Form," clarifying this intended meaning. However, he locked his discourse and imbued it with obscurity, as is the habit of the Sages to do with their wisdom—to preserve and protect it from the ignorant. 

**Euclid** did the same. To these scholars, **obscurity** in the sciences refers to the precision of meanings so that they become hidden and faint. To extract that obscurity requires thought and the deliberate use of perception until one can distinguish it from the manifest matters it is mixed with. 

For the sciences are of two types:
1. That which is manifest and apparent, which is the well-known.
2. That which is hidden and inward. 

The hidden, inward type is the "obscure." That obscure meaning either requires an analogy <note>original: "qiyas"</note> and premises to unlock it, or it requires contemplation, inference, thought, and vision until that meaning dawns upon the seeker and the intent is explained to him. Then, what was locked is opened to him, and he attains his goal. 

**Inference** <term>istidlal: the process of using evidence to reach a conclusion</term> occurs through many means, including:
- Judging the absent by the witnessed.
- Returning a branch to its root through a unifying principle.
- Constructing one’s view upon accepted and approved sayings from a single authority or a group of authorities, and deriving a conclusion from them. 

From these, the sought-after meaning appears. In short: one must find paths to knowledge. From whichever direction his contemplation is directed and his vision passes through that path, he will acquire the meanings of all existents and their various ranks will become clear to him.

It also defends the use of obscure language by philosophers like Plato and Euclid as a necessary means to protect esoteric knowledge from the unready.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the specific discussion of the "Universal Human" to a broader cosmological mapping. It situates the human being within a vast hierarchy of existence, moving from the Divine Creator through the celestial spheres down to the physical elements, then back up through the biological kingdoms to the "Rational Animal" who possesses wisdom.</meta>

## ( CHAPTER )

Know that all existing things <term>al-mawjudat: everything that has being, from God to physical matter</term> possess specific ranks. The existence of the **Creator**—exalted is He—is the most excellent existence and the most perfect rank. Following Him in the rank of existence is the **Intellect**. From the Intellect comes the existence of the **Soul**. Then comes **Prime Matter** <term>al-hayula: from the Greek 'hyle,' referring to the underlying 'stuff' of the universe before it takes a specific shape</term>. All of these are immovable and cannot be described by spatial movement.

Then, after these, comes the **Sphere of Nature**, which is the beginning of movement and rest; from it begins the process of generation and corruption <note>the Aristotelian cycle of birth, decay, and transformation in the physical world</note> within this world. Then, following the Sphere of Nature, are the existence of the celestial spheres reaching down to the **Sphere of the Moon**. Here, a "common matter" exists which has a sensory rank. (The meaning of this) is that things within it exist in **potentiality**, not yet in **actuality**.

Next are the **Primary Elements** <term>al-istaqisat: from the Greek 'stoicheia,' referring to the four basic elements of earth, water, air, and fire</term> which operate within that common matter. After the elements come the **Minerals**, then **Plants**, then **Animals**, and finally the **Rational Animal** <note>the human being</note>.

The existence of this final rank is different from the existence of the first rank. The first rank, beginning with the Intellect, is a noble rank; it descends through the levels until it ends at the Sphere of the Moon. However, that which follows the Sphere of the Moon begins from the lowest point and ascends toward the most noble, which is the **Rational Animal**, because within him is **Wisdom**, and in him **Will** is completed in actuality, not just potentiality. Know this, O seeker of wisdom, and you shall attain a great portion of success and the ultimate happiness. Let the purpose and intent be the acceptance of this speaker’s words, for he has done well:

-> I have not seen among the faults of men a fault * like the shortcoming of those capable of reaching perfection. <-
<note>original: "wa-lam ara fi 'uyubi al-nasi 'ayban...". This is a famous verse by the poet al-Mutanabbi (10th century), suggesting that the greatest tragedy is a person who has the potential for greatness but settles for less.</note>

I will now provide a classification for the ranks of existing things to help you train your mind and illuminate your insight; so listen and be guided:
1. The **Principle** <note>the starting point</note>
2. The **Element**
3. The **Primary Element**
4. **Prime Matter** (Hyle)
5. **Form**
6. **Nature**
7. **Body**
8. **The Dyad**
9. **Animal**
10. **Human**
11. **Man**
12. **A specific man (like Zayd) confused with another**
13. **The known, individual Zayd**

The **Principle** is more general than the **Element** and acts as its genus, for it applies to both **Substance** and **Accident** <term>Substance (jawhar) is what exists in itself; Accident ('arad) is a property like color or size that needs a substance to exist</term>. The **Element** is a substance without quality. If it accepts quality, it becomes a **Primary Element**.

The **Primary Element** is more general than **Prime Matter**, because it is a single substance before it takes on a quality. **Prime Matter** (Hyle) is the gathering of elements to receive a **Form**. Prime Matter is more general than Form because, before it accepts a shape, it is "simple" <note>undifferentiated</note>; once it accepts a shape, it becomes a Form—just as copper is the matter for a jug, or wood is the matter for the form of a chair.

If it accepts movement, rest, and various forces, it is called **Nature**. When these natures gather and accept color, growth, or decrease, it becomes a **Body**. The **Body** is the eighth rank. The **Dyad** <note>the first step into multiplicity</note> can be animal or non-animal. The **Animal** can be human or non-human. The **Human** can be a man or not a man. The **Man** can be "Zayd" or not Zayd. "Zayd" can be confused or not confused—and the one who is not confused is the "Known Individual."

The **Prime Matter** (Hyle), which is the gathering of elements to receive forms, is of two types:
1. **Personal Matter:** It only accepts a single form. it is composed of the combined elements—Earth, Water, Fire, and Air—and it transforms from one substance to another.
2. **Universal Matter:** It is capable of receiving all forms. It is composed of the "simple elements," which are: **Heat**, **Cold**, **Dryness**, and **Moisture**. It does not transition from one substance to another.

The sage **Aristotle** <note>original: "Arstalis"</note> spoke excellently when he defined Prime Matter from the perspective of the **Instructional Sciences** <note>mathematics/logic</note> as "the power capable of receiving different forms." From the perspective of the **Natural Sciences**, he defined it as "the body that constitutes the essence of all particular things."

We have mentioned these meanings to "impregnate the mind" and sharpen the intellect. These ideas, and others like them, are the **"Spiritual Slaps"** <note>an idiomatic expression for sudden intellectual awakenings or shocks that stir the soul</note> and the "words" that Adam received from his Lord, which none but the learned truly understand. For whoever achieves the breakthrough of "Universal Connection" and raises their perception will find the content of this discourse to be self-evident. Here I conclude this section, and I shall provide in what follows the discourse we previously promised.

It provides technical definitions for matter, form, and substance, citing Aristotle to bridge the gap between abstract logic and physical nature.</summary>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page's discussion on the hierarchy of existence from the Divine to the physical elements, this page moves into the practical application of celestial influences through the creation of talismans. It bridges the gap between theoretical cosmology and the hermetic arts, specifically attributing certain practices to the scholar al-Khwarizmi.</meta>

At the opening of this book, I ask God for help in achieving the goal and the wish in what we have intended.

->( A Talisman for Catching Fish )<-

I also found this attributed to Muhammad ibn Musa al-Khwarizmi <note>original: "Muhammad ibn Musa al-Khwarizmi," the 9th-century polymath famous for his work in algebra, who also wrote on geography and the calendar</note> in a treatise where he mentioned it and claimed to have tested it. You are to craft the image of a fish.

This must be done during the rising of the first face <term>wajh: a "face" or decan is a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term> of Pisces, with the Moon and Mercury within it, and during the hour of the Moon. It should be held at the time of fishing, for it provides great assistance in the endeavor.

<image-desc>A simple ink drawing of a fish with visible scales and fins, oriented horizontally and facing left.</image-desc>

->( A Talisman to Draw People to a Place of Healing )<-

This talisman is intended for physicians to make. Engrave upon a tin plate the image of a seated man with medical instruments before him, and people standing with water flasks <note>qawarir: glass vessels used for uroscopy, the primary diagnostic method of the time</note> seeking his counsel. The entire engraving should be made when the ascendant <term>tali': the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> is one of the two houses of Venus <note>Taurus or Libra</note> while Mars is within it, and the Dragon’s Head <term>ras al-jawzahar: the North Node of the Moon, considered a point of increase and power</term> is at the Midheaven <term>wust al-sama': the highest point in the sky at that moment</term>. Keep this plate permanently in the required location, and you shall see a marvel.

->( A Talisman for Planting and Tilling )<-

Depict upon a silver plate a man seated among crops and plantings, while the ascendant is Taurus and the Moon is in the ascendant, moving away from the Sun and connecting with Saturn. Bury it in the desired location, and everything planted there will thrive and produce quickly. No vermin, hail, birds, or other harmful things will approach it.

->( A Talisman for Trade )<-

Depict the image of a man holding a balance scale in a yellow plate <note>safihah safra: likely referring to brass or a gold-colored alloy</note> while the ascendant is one of the two houses of Mars <note>Aries or Scorpio</note> and the Moon is within it. Whoever holds this shall see much goodness in their trade.

->( A Talisman for the Stone )<-

Engrave upon a gold plate the image of a lion with a stone <note>referring to a kidney or bladder stone</note> before him, as if he is playing with it. This should be done during the hour of the Sun, with the ascendant being the beginning of the middle face <term>the second decan</term> of Leo, and the Sun positioned at that exact degree. When the one suffering from the stone holds this, the pain of the stone shall be removed from them.

<image-desc>An ink drawing of a lion standing, facing right but looking back toward the viewer. A large, patterned sphere (representing the stone/حصاة) is positioned beneath its front paws.</image-desc>

<meta>The previous page concluded with instructions for a talisman to draw people to a place of healing, specifically detailing the astrological timing involving Venus and the Dragon’s Head. This page continues the practical manual of talismans, transitioning from medical applications to the broader theory of how celestial spirits animate these objects, citing the authority of Plato and Aristotle.</meta>

### (A Talisman for Melancholic Diseases)

This talisman can bolster the health of those seeking it until they reach a state of excellent, stable wellness, and it repels the harm of hostile influences. It should be crafted during the hour of Venus and the Moon, while the ascendant is in one of the cardines <term>watad: one of the four "pivotal" houses of a birth chart—the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th—considered the strongest points of influence</term>, with the ascendant making a connection to Venus. The ruler of the Sixth House <note>the house traditionally associated with illness and service</note> should be in a trine aspect with a benefic planet <term>sa'd: a "lucky" planet, usually Jupiter or Venus</term> or in opposition to a benefic. The ruler of the Eighth House <note>the house associated with death and transformation</note> should be in a square aspect with Mercury, provided that Mercury is not retrograde, combust <term>muhtaraq: a planet "burnt" by being too close to the Sun, weakening its power</term>, or looking toward a malefic planet. It should be made during the final hour of Sunday, governed by the ruler of the Tenth House from the ascendant. It must be crafted from pure silver, for it will then repel all the ailments we have mentioned.

->(Talismans)<-

The meaning of talismans is that they are "dead" bodies awaiting celestial personalities; as long as this is the case, no one remains ignorant of them. Among the requirements: do not craft any talismans for love or intimacy unless the Moon is joined with the benefic planets, and the days are likewise favorable. Do not craft any of them while the Moon is with malefic planets or while the Moon is "void of course" <note>original: "farighan," meaning the Moon makes no further aspects before changing signs, indicating a lack of result</note>. 

For example: craft talismans for affection, love, and meeting kings on the day of the Moon when it is full and located in Sagittarius, Taurus, Cancer, or Pisces. If the Dragon’s Head <term>al-jawzahar: the North Node of the Moon, a point of increase</term> is with it, the effect is strengthened. Observe the Moon always: wait for its arrival in a "fortunate" mansion <note>referring to the 28 Lunar Mansions of the zodiac</note> and avoid it when it is in an "unfortunate" one. Also, craft for love when the Moon is with Venus, or during the hour of Jupiter while it is in Pisces, Sagittarius, or Cancer with the Moon present. Craft for enmity and harm when the Moon is in an unfortunate mansion with malefic stars, or in a square or opposition aspect to them, and let it be with the Dragon's Tail <term>al-dhanab: the South Node of the Moon, associated with decrease and loss</term>, for then you will succeed. Works performed at night are better than those performed during the day.

(Among its conditions), without which nothing is completed, is that the maker must focus their entire spiritual energy on the work and refine their intention so that the powers of the soul may be linked to the powers of the heavens. Plato spoke well in his *Book of Aphorisms* <note>likely the *Liber de Fructu* or similar Pseudo-Platonic hermetic texts translated into Arabic</note> when he said: "If the speech matches the intention of the speaker, it moves the intention of the listener" by purposeful aim. This is the foundation upon which the union of intention, prayer, and humility before the First Origin is built when seeking what is hoped for from the Lords of Prayer. 

Another condition is that the work be kept secret from people, hidden from their sight, and away from the rising and light of the sun’s eye. No one should look upon it except one of firm resolve and trusted companionship—not one who is dismissive or contemptuous of anything issued by the "spiritualities of the spheres" <term>ruhaniyyat al-falak: the angelic or spiritual intelligences governing the planets</term> through these nine brilliant and overpowering works. Know this well. The realization of what is explained here is found in abundance in his discourse on talismans, which is the most sublime branch of astrology. He claimed that there is no life for a body that has no spirit. By this, he meant talismans crafted without their intended celestial proportions; such objects cannot receive the diffusion of the planetary spiritualities and are therefore like dead bodies without souls. However, if they receive the celestial spiritualities by being placed according to the true celestial proportions corresponding to the desired goal, they become living bodies that perform miraculous deeds.

### (Aristotle Also Said) in his treatise:

The best way a talisman can be made is through the action of the seven wandering stars <note>the seven visible planets of antiquity</note>. The most noble and enduring is that which is faced by a benefic planet that assists in bringing down a "spirituality" from the heavens to the Earth. He then said: It may be that there are Divine Names which, if used to summon the spiritualities, cause them to descend and fall low. Yet, they might overwhelm the summoner if he is not knowledgeable in the nature of the celestial spiritualities being brought down. 

This is the secret indicated by the masters of the Sufis regarding the "Greatest Name of God" <term>al-Ism al-A'zam: a hidden name of God in Islamic tradition believed to grant the power to perform miracles</term>, which, according to them, changes things from their natural state and leaves an impact. He refused to speak of the distant effects it has on the world. The majority of the Sufis—I mean the common folk among them—hold a belief contrary to this. We have already written a treatise on this matter in which we brought forth what specifically pertains to that. Returning to the words of Aristotle, he then said:

<meta>This page continues the discussion of talismanic theory from page 24. While the previous page focused on the astrological timing for health-related charms, this page explains the "animation" of talismans through speech and intention, citing the authority of Timaeus. It transitions from theory to providing specific templates for incantations regarding love, enmity, and protection.</meta>

In the art of **magical incantations** <term>ruqā: spoken spells or charms used to invoke supernatural forces</term>, it is understood that they do not exceed the terrestrial sphere in their capacity to draw down what is being summoned. However, incantations do not achieve their full effect unless one's hope is tethered to the **Greatest Graces** <note>likely referring to the "Greatest Names" of God or supreme celestial powers</note>. For by the movement of these Graces, the **primordial matter** <term>al-hayūlā: from the Greek 'hyle', referring to the underlying 'stuff' of the universe that takes on different forms</term> is commanded and returned, and the practitioner directs it toward a specific point on the Earth. I shall provide the full details of this in the fourth part of this book of ours.

All experts in this field have reached a consensus regarding the speech used during the creation of talismans for a specific purpose, alongside the invocation—or in another manuscript, "alongside the planetary faces" <note>original: "al-wujūh," referring to decans or specific faces of the zodiac signs</note>—to ensure the influence is accepted. Among their teachings is what **Timaeus the Sage** <note>Refers to the character in Plato’s dialogues, who in the medieval Islamic tradition was considered a master of natural philosophy and the occult</note> said: "The speech used over talismans serves as the soul does for the body." It acts as the mover of spiritual forces, especially when these forces are gathered together and the practitioner speaks with a sincere intention. This is the noble purpose through which all talismans are completed. The meaning of "speech" here is to speak words that align with the intended goal and according to the specific power the maker has prepared within his own mind and which flows through his consciousness.

->(Example of this)<-

For talismans of **love and intimacy**, it should be said:

"I have united So-and-so and So-and-so just as Fire, Air, Water, and Earth are united. I have moved the spiritual essence of So-and-so just as the sun’s rays move the light and powers of the world. I have beautified So-and-so in the eyes of So-and-so just as the sky is beautified by its stars and the plants by their flowers. I have made the spiritual essence of So-and-so rise above the essence of So-and-so, just as fire rises above air, and water above the earth. May So-and-so not eat, nor drink, nor find pleasure or joy, except in the presence of So-and-so."

->And if you are crafting for enmity and separation, say:<-

"I have severed and separated So-and-so from So-and-so by the power of these spiritual beings, just as light is separated from darkness. I have placed between them enmity and hatred, like the enmity between water and fire."

->And if you are crafting a 'binding of desire' and its movement, say:<-

"I have bound the spiritual essence of the desire of So-and-so from So-and-so—or from all women—and I have seized it by the power of these spiritual beings, like the binding of mountains and their rocks."

->And if you are crafting a 'release,' say:<-

"I have released and dissolved the binding of the spiritual essence of desire that was bound for So-and-so from So-and-so—and from all women—by the power of these spiritual beings, just as fire dissolves flesh <note>or "renders fat"</note>, and as the shining sun releases the darkness of the world and its spirits, and as snow melts before the sun."

->And if you are crafting something to silence the tongues of people against you<-

Or against someone else, then say: "I have shielded So-and-so with the veil of the shining light, and I have cut off the tongues from him, and I have lowered a spiritual veil over their eyes to repel their evil gazes and sever their harmful tongues and intentions." Use words similar to this, tailored to the specific aim, with a sincere intention and a strong will.

<meta>This page concludes the first treatise, moving from the theory of incantations discussed on page 25 to a specific practical example of a "binding" or "exposing" spell. ->And if you work to expose a person’s secrets, say:<-

"The exposing of the secrets of [So-and-so] by the power of these spiritual beings is like the sun's rays piercing the thickness of the clouds. I have disgraced him and made him a target for the tongues of spiritual beings, just as arrows strike the bodies frequented by archers."

And do not speak while you are working with any words outside of the desired goal, except for speech that strengthens the work and executes it. This is an example I have mentioned to you of the astronomical proportions—wondrous matters indeed. In this treatise, we have deposited knowledge such that if you contemplate it, you would be able to perform the work of every **talisman** <term>talsam: a physical object imbued with celestial power through specific timing and inscriptions</term> that has been or will be made in the world. 

The substances of the talismans we mentioned must be similar to the requirements of the desired goal, whether it be for good or for evil. I will provide you later with the planetary divisions of the **Three Procreations** <term>al-mawālid al-thalātha: the three kingdoms of nature—minerals, plants, and animals</term>, including their specific incenses, offerings, and materials. All these matters are tools to be utilized, just as a physician utilizes a multitude of materials from foods and medicines; if the patient accepts the treatment, the success is completed and the effort reaches its hope. The entire matter revolves around the perfection of the description. 

The Greek sages used to observe a planet when it was with its **Node** <term>jawzahar: the points where a planet’s orbit crosses the ecliptic; in astrology, these are points of intensified power</term>. They would place it at the degree of the "Midheaven" <note>the highest point in the sky at a given time</note>, perform a sacrifice, burn incense, and offer their oblation. They would then call upon it with its specific invocation for the thing they desired, and their goal would succeed. Likewise, they claimed to do this when a planet is with its node because it has no latitude <note>it is exactly on the ecliptic path</note> at that time, especially if the planet holds a strong "testimony" <note>a technical term for a planet's strength or influence</note> in the birth chart of the practitioner. In such cases, its influence is more manifest and more readily accepted. 

The status of this world—may God wake you from the sleep of heedlessness and the slumber of ignorance—is noble. Yet, seekers in our time are few. Be among that small group, and you shall triumph in wisdom, if God Most High wills.

**The first treatise is complete. It is followed by the second treatise concerning celestial images, their actions, and the clarification of the secrets hidden by the sages.**

# « The Second Treatise »
## From the Book "The Goal of the Wise"

This treatise concerns the **celestial forms** and their actions, the disclosure of the secrets regarding them that the sages have obscured, and examples of how **magic** <term>sihr: the art of directing celestial energies</term> is borrowed into this world—called the **World of Generation and Corruption** <term>al-kawn wa-l-fasād: the physical, sublunary realm where things are born and decay</term>—from the **World of the Aether** <term>al-athīr: the celestial, unchanging realm above the moon</term>, which is the home of spiritual powers.

---

### ( Chapter One )

The "universal intellects" have never ceased aspiring to know the secrets of the sages and the wondrous matters they hid in their books through symbols and riddles; thus, they achieved their desire and purpose. However, those with "deficient intellects" suffer an inability to reach their goals—may God not deprive us or you of our intellects. Among the motivations that moved me to research the secrets of **talismans** <term>al-talsamāt: physical objects constructed at specific astrological moments to produce effects</term> is that in my youth, I saw the virtuous **Ptolemy** <note>original: "Batlamyus," the famous Greco-Egyptian astronomer and mathematician</note> say in his book known as **The Fruit** <note>original: "al-Thamara," known in Latin as the *Centiloquium*, a collection of 100 astrological aphorisms</note>: "The forms in the **World of Composition** <term>al-tarkīb: the physical world where elements are combined</term> are obedient to the celestial forms."

The words of the sages testify by consensus that the stars possess powers that are naturally dominant according to the laws of habit. For this reason, the makers of talismans engraved them when the planets resided within them to achieve the works they desired, and they reached their goals in the artful composition of these secrets. I also saw in the commentary of this chapter by **Ahmad ibn Yusuf the Scribe** <note>a 9th-century mathematician and secretary in Egypt</note> a story that occurred in the days of **Ibn Ahmad ibn Tulun** <note>likely referring to the Tulunid dynasty in Egypt</note>, as narrated by a Roman sage who had settled there. It is necessary that noble souls do not withhold their gifts in such matters. This is the text of what he recounted. He said:

"It happened that we were with him one day when we heard a cry from the side of the house. When he asked about it, he was told a servant boy had been stung by a scorpion. He called for a cloth containing seals that smelled of **frankincense** <note>original: "al-kunder"</note> and took one of the seals from it. He ordered that the whole thing be crushed and given to the boy to drink. When that was done, the boy’s crying subsided and his ailment vanished upon drinking it. I examined the scroll and found each [seal] to be the image of a scorpion. I asked him what he had used to stamp the seals, and he produced a gold ring with a yellow gemstone featuring the image of a scorpion. I asked him about the secret of the ring and how it was made. He mentioned that it is engraved while the **Moon is in Scorpio** in its **first face** <term>the first decan (10 degrees) of the zodiac sign Scorpio</term>. So, I made one and began stamping it for those who were stung, and I would change what I stamped it on <note>i.e., using materials other than frankincense</note> for fear that the effect was specifically due to the frankincense. I saw the most wondrous wonders from it."

Here ends his words. Because of what I had appreciated of this in my youth, I took it upon myself to observe this astrological proportion alongside those I trust from the people of the **mathematical sciences** <term>al-ʿulūm al-taʿlīmiyya: literally 'educational sciences,' referring to the Quadrivium: arithmetic, geometry, astronomy, and music</term> and others. I engraved the image at the mentioned time and tested the power of this talisman, finding its effects in this regard to be so frequent that it led to utter wonder. Everyone who witnessed those wondrous actions was amazed by it. This was the reason that prompted the research into this science—my sensation and perception of this degree [of power]. For sensation is the perception of the soul, and its definition is not established except after knowing the **sense** <term>al-hiss</term>, which is the change in the temperament of the qualities of the senses through direct contact with the objects of perception. Sensation is the perception of those sensitive powers regarding the change in the temperaments of the senses. **For example**: the **visual power** <term>al-quwwa al-bāṣira</term> has its channel in this eye, and it is derived from the pupils in the organs of the eye, and likewise for the rest of the senses.

<summary>This page introduces the second treatise of the Picatrix, focusing on how celestial forms influence the physical world and recounting a famous anecdote of a scorpion-healing talisman used in Tulunid Egypt.</summary>
<keywords>Celestial forms, World of Generation and Corruption, Aether, Ptolemy, Centiloquium, Ahmad ibn Yusuf, Ibn Tulun, Scorpio talisman, Sensation and Perception, visual power</keywords>

--- Page 29 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to the Second Treatise. While page 27 introduced the anecdotal evidence of talismans (specifically the scorpion), page 28 outlines the rigorous philosophical and scientific curriculum required to master the art of magic, emphasizing that it is a discipline for the learned philosopher rather than the uneducated practitioner.</meta>

Sensation is the perception of those powers and the occurrence of knowledge regarding them. Since **demonstration** <term>al-burhān: a logical proof derived from certain premises to reach an undeniable conclusion</term> is a scientific scale through which every true objective is perfectly reached—either through matters specific to that objective or essential, eternal premises—I present them so that each category of knowledge may be obtained. These are of two types: **assent** <term>taṣdīq: the act of judging a proposition as true</term> and **conception** <term>taṣawwur: the mental grasping of an essence without judgment</term>. 

What I first saw of the words of **Ptolemy** <note>original: "Baṭlamyūs"</note> was of this demonstrative nature; then, I later came across an experiment that clarified for me the action of this **talisman** <note>referring to the scorpion talisman mentioned in the First Treatise</note> whose construction I mentioned in the first treatise. I did not leave a single researcher's question or inquiry throughout all the books of the sages on this matter until I stood upon that which brought me to my desired goal. 

Furthermore, I say: no one can reach an understanding of how the **Higher World** <note>the celestial spheres and stars</note> influences this **Lower World** <note>the Earthly realm of change</note> except after mastering all the sciences of philosophy. By this, I mean: **Mathematics**, **Physics**, and **Metaphysics**. 

Whoever falls short in these will not reach the reality of his goal, because the principles of his quest are taken from these three sciences. As for **Mathematics** <note>original: "al-riyāḍah," referring to the mathematical arts</note>, one who lacks knowledge of arithmetic will fail. He will also lack knowledge of the movements of the "high individuals" <note>the planets and stars</note> and the ways by which one attains the **Science of the Configuration** <term>‘ilm al-hay’ah: the study of the positions and movements of celestial bodies</term>, for it can only be grasped through the arts of arithmetic and geometry. Without these, one lacks celestial astronomy, as neither observations nor astronomical measurements—the principles of which are taken from demonstration and geometry—can exist without them. 

Furthermore, without the **Art of Composition** <term>ṣinā‘at al-ta’līf: the science of harmony and musical proportions</term>, through which harmony and discord are known, one cannot know which celestial things most resemble earthly things, nor which action of the former is most like the action of the latter in this lower realm. If one does not know this measure, how can he use likenesses to draw inferences about other likenesses? 

Whoever does not know **Physics** does not know the likenesses of **generation and corruption** <term>al-kawn wa-l-fasād: the cycle of birth and decay in the physical world</term> or the conjunctions. Whoever does not know that does not know that the "high individuals" <note>the stars</note> influence the "low individuals" <note>earthly things</note>. 

Whoever does not know **Metaphysics** <note>original: "mā ba‘d al-ṭabī‘ah"</note> does not know in which earthly beings the traces of the celestial bodies occur, where these effects attach to them, and where they do not. 

Inevitably, no one can know this art in its reality except one who knows its principles. Its principles are not found except in a **philosopher**. Therefore, it is necessary that no one knows it except the philosopher—so know this.

## « Chapter on the Great Figures »

### ( Section )

<image-desc>A line drawing of a standing man with a mustache and a focused expression, wearing a simple robe or tunic. His arms are crossed over his stomach. Beside his legs, the word "Proportionality" is handwritten.</image-desc>

<margin>Proportionality</margin>
Discussion regarding the **figures** <note>referring to the constellations and zodiacal images</note> is very difficult, because the ancients sought to hide it to the best of their ability. However, I shall explain to you what they veiled. Whoever wishes to dive deep into this subject should consult the *Great Book of Figures* by **Rusuwas** <note>original: "Rūsuwas," likely Teucer of Babylon, a 1st-century astrologer known for his work on the extra-zodiacal decans and constellations</note>, for he exhausted and fully explored this matter. 

As for what appears in the figures of the zodiac, my brother, it is of two types. One type appears in the zodiac from the constellations and their forty-eight parts, which comprise the sphere. These are the "imaginary figures" formed by the outlines of the stars; they are what you see by imagining the gathering of the fixed stars and their radiance, such as the figures of the zodiac signs. 

The other type of figures are those like the Dog, the Bear, the Cat, the Hen, and others. These figures shift and move from sign to sign. Thus, they are not an inherent nature in the celestial sphere, especially in the zodiacal belt, for they move out of a "face" <term>wajh: a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term> every thousand years. As for those figures near the poles, their movement is not apparent over many years, or they are closer to being fixed because 

<summary>This page establishes that the study of magic (specifically talismans and celestial influence) requires a complete education in the three branches of philosophy: mathematics, physics, and metaphysics. It begins a new chapter on the "Great Figures," distinguishing between fixed zodiac constellations and moving extra-zodiacal constellations.</summary>
<keywords>Philosophy, Mathematics, Physics, Metaphysics, Astronomy, Ptolemy, Talismans, Rusuwas (Teucer), Zodiac, Constellations, Generation and Corruption</keywords>

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the principles of astrology and talismanic magic, transitioning from the philosophical requirements of the practitioner to the specific symbolic interpretations of the zodiacal decans, specifically focusing on Aries.</meta>

...the course of its circle becomes very narrow. This is one aspect. As for the second aspect, it consists of the **imaginary forms** <term>al-ṣuwar al-wahmiyya: symbolic figures visualized by ancient astrologers to represent specific celestial influences</term> mentioned by the Indians. They said: in the first **decan** <term>wajh: literally "face," a 10-degree division of a zodiac sign</term> of **Aries** <note>original: "al-Ḥamal"</note>, there rises a man with red eyes and a large body, stout-hearted and full of pride, wearing a white garment fastened at his waist with a rope; he is angry, standing on one leg, and acts as a guard and protector.

->[Image Description: An illustration of a figure representing the second decan of Aries, depicted with the features of a woman. She has only one leg, and from behind her emerges what appears to be the body or tail of a horse. She is raising her left hand.]<-

In the second **decan**, there rises a woman wearing a cloak and red clothes. She has only one leg, and her form resembles that of a horse. In her mind is the desire to go out and seek clothing, jewelry, and children.

In the third **decan**, there rises a man of reddish complexion with red hair, angry, coming forth with a sword in one hand and a wooden staff in the other. He wears thin red clothes and is skilled in ironwork. He desires to do good but is unable to do so. The same logic applies to the rest of the signs of the Zodiac. Know that they only imagined these things based on the natures of the planets and the signs. 

They spoke of a man with red eyes and a stout heart in the first decan because it is the **domicile** <note>original: "bayt"</note> and the decan of **Mars** <note>original: "al-Mirrīkh"</note>. Mars is the indicator of redness, courage, and boldness. The large white garment comes from the influence of the **exaltation of the Sun** <note>The Sun is traditionally "exalted" or at its most powerful in Aries</note> and its rays. His anger stems from the way Mars mixes with the Sun in this place, producing wrath. His standing on one leg and his guarding are characteristic of the servants of powerful tyrants. 

In the second decan, they placed a woman instead of a man because it is the **term** <term>ḥadd: a further subdivision of a sign assigned to a specific planet</term> of **Mercury** <note>original: "‘Uṭārid"</note>. Mercury is considered feminine for most of its cycle. Within this decan are also two degrees belonging to the term of **Venus** <note>original: "al-Zuharah"</note>. The red clothing comes from the nature of Mars. Her horse-like form is also from the nature of Mars, except for the blackness, which indicates beasts of burden due to its proximity to the **Sixth House** <note>In astrology, the Sixth House relates to small animals and labor</note>. Among beasts are the "beasts of war." Her seeking of jewelry and clothes is due to the portion of the Sun, because the Sun's rays act as a veil for her. "Children" are mentioned because the **Midheaven** <note>original: "wasṭ al-samā’"</note> is the house of light and the indicator of the beginning of existence, as its exit from it is through the two sharp points of Mars. 

As for the man <note>in the third decan</note>, they are actually two reddish males—their color being between the redness of Mars and the leaden grey of **Saturn** <note>original: "Zuḥal"</note>. Red hair belongs to Mars. Anger is of the nature of Mars, as is stubbornness. In his hand is a sword because weaponry is of the works of Mars, while wood is of the nature of Saturn. Similarly, the ironwork <note>comes from Mars</note>. He desires to do good and evil due to the decan of Venus, but Mars prevents him through the domicile, and Saturn prevents him through the term.

->[Image Description: An illustration of a figure representing the third decan of Aries, showing a bearded man wearing a long robe and a turban. He holds a curved sword (scimitar) in his right hand and what looks like a rod or staff in his left hand.]<-

**By this example**, all that they symbolized in this second aspect is understood; this was the logic they followed. I have given you, O student, a scale by which you may measure and work. This was the approach of **Abu Bakr ibn Wahshiyya** <note>A 9th-century scholar famous for his "Nabataean Agriculture" and works on symbols</note> in his book translated from the Nabataean, which he titled *Tiqana*, when he spoke of the **triplicities** <term>muthallathāt: groups of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, Water)</term> and mentioned the northern figures within the Watery Triplicity.

<summary>This page explains the symbolic imagery (decans) of the sign of Aries, detailing how the physical descriptions of these "imaginary forms" are derived from the astrological influences of Mars, the Sun, Venus, and Saturn.</summary>
<keywords>Aries, Decans, Mars, Mercury, Saturn, Ibn Wahshiyya, Astrological symbolism, Nabataean science, Triplicities</keywords>

--- Page 31 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of astrological influences, moving from the visual descriptions of the zodiacal decans on the previous page to the mathematical and symbolic logic used to interpret planetary combinations and elemental associations.</meta>

-> - 30 - <-

...and southern. Then he said: whenever you see the mention of water, a river, a sea, a well, a watercourse, or a swamp, and whether the mention is masculine or feminine, know that it exerts an influence over water—especially aquatic matters. Likewise for the other **triplicities** <term>muthallathāt: groups of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element</term>—be they fire, earth, air, or water—apply this same knowledge. 

As for the ancients' naming of the degrees and the depiction of their forms in their respective places, all of that is a symbolic code for the placement of influences—so understand this. For example, their saying "a severed head that speaks," or a "paralyzed or severed limb," and similar depictions of death, its image, poison, weeping, and the signals from one planet to another... these are merely symbols to recognize their effects and the wonderful actions gifted by the planets according to their influences. 

It is through this approach that they encoded the science of **talismans** <term>al-ṭalasmāt: objects inscribed with astrological symbols to harness celestial power</term>. As for "sleep," it refers to reports on the current hidden or absent state. As for "death," it refers to reports on distant absent things that have passed, and their retrieval if accompanied by the signal of the pointer to the dead. Indeed, it provides information on what will be in the future and over long periods of time, and other such things.

This is what **Ibn Hayyan al-Sufi** <note>Likely referring to the famous alchemist Jabir ibn Hayyan, often called "al-Sufi" in medieval traditions</note> mentioned in the book *Explanation of the Forms of the Zodiac and their Influences*. In this book, he enumerated all the perceptible stars of the firmament and assigned the action of each one to a specific craft, as a substitute for what **Aristotle** <note>original: "Arisṭū"</note> composed in his book titled *Serulogia* <note>original: "Sīrūlūjiyā," likely a corruption of the Greek "Meteorologica" or a reference to a lost pseudo-Aristotelian work</note>. This book was among those burned, and no copy of it remains. The same applies to his book on plants.

**Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of the occult sciences</note> mentioned in his book titled *The Construction of Moving Spatial Images of Translocative Motion* that the actions of the planets are vast and cannot be fully contained. He said: every planet has an influence in every degree of the firmament when it aligns with it or resides within it. The sum of these actions is derived from multiplying 360 by 7, which equals 2,520. This configuration produces specific traces and actions. Then, multiplying 360 by 6 represents the conjunction of any two planets in a single degree. This results in six combinations, as division necessitates this. For instance, Saturn and Jupiter may meet:

| In a single degree... | which makes two... | and Saturn and the Sun may meet |
|-----------------------|--------------------|---------------------------------|
| In a single degree... | which makes three...| and Saturn and Venus may meet |
| In a single degree... | which makes four... | and Saturn and Mercury may meet |
| In a single degree... | which makes five... | and Saturn and the Moon may meet |
| In a single degree... | which makes six... | This total is reached by multiplying... |

...six by 360, which are the degrees of the circle, resulting in 2,160. These too are forms that produce wonderful traces and effects in this science. Then you multiply 360 by 5, which is the conjunction of any three planets in a single degree, totaling 1,800. These also produce strange effects. Then multiply 360 by 4, the conjunction of any four planets in a single degree, totaling 1,440. These forms also have influences and actions in a single degree. Then multiply that by 3, the conjunction of any five planets in a single degree, totaling 1,080. These forms also have influences and actions. Then multiply that by 2, the conjunction of any six planets in a single degree, totaling 720. These forms also have actions and influences. Then multiply that by 1, which is the conjunction of all seven planets in a single degree. This is the method mentioned by the **First Sage** <note>likely referring to Hermes or Aristotle</note> regarding the forms indicating the judgments of the degrees; their total number is 10,080, possessing wonderful actions and influences. Then the Sage...

It provides a mathematical breakdown of how the seven planets combine across the 360 degrees of the zodiac to create over 10,000 unique celestial influences.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological calculations, transitioning from the symbolic interpretation of zodiacal degrees to the mathematical distribution of planetary influences. It then shifts focus to the specific lunar cycles and the differing traditions of ancient astrological schools regarding the Moon's power.</meta>

31

First: He said that you must do this if you place one of the planets in the [first] degree... and the other planet in the second degree. You then divide this among the **seven planets** <term>al-kawākib al-sabʿa: the Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</term>. Then, apply this same calculation to every interval of two degrees throughout. Continue this for all 360 degrees of the **celestial sphere** <term>al-falak</term>. This method is longer than the first, but it is far more beneficial. What then, O observer, do you think of grasping the essence of the actions of these images and their influences upon the world? 

When you have finished all of that, return to the conjunction of the **seven wandering planets** <term>al-kawākib al-mutaḥayyira: the planets that move across the sky</term> within the degrees, alongside the **fixed stars** <term>al-kawākib al-thābita</term>, both individually and in combination. By "combination," I mean the shared influence of the wandering planets; by "individually," I mean the independent influence of the fixed stars. Know this, understand it clearly, and be stingy with it <note>This is a common medieval instruction to keep "secret" or "elite" knowledge hidden from the uninitiated</note>. Do not reveal these secrets to those who are not worthy of them, lest you wrong yourself and the recipient.

-> (A Section: On the Views of a Certain Group) <-

A certain group has argued that the action of the celestial sphere is due to an increase or decrease in heat, as they were not privy to these wonderful effects and hidden sciences. They claimed that these resulting actions only stem from the two luminaries, the Sun and the Moon, while the rest of the planets merely assist and strengthen them in their actions. (They said) that by the movements of the Sun, the movements of all planets in their degrees are measured, and by the conditions of the Sun, all conditions of the **horoscopes** <term>al-mawālīd: literally "the births" or natal charts</term> are measured. (They also agreed) that the Moon has specific states by which its influences are measured. 

The first of its states is its distance from the Sun after it separates from its **conjunction** <term>al-ijtimāʿ: the New Moon, when the Sun and Moon are at the same degree</term>. Until it reaches its **first quadrature** <term>tarbīʿ: a 90-degree angle, or the First Quarter Moon</term>, it is strongest at stirring moisture; here, moisture is greater and heat is less. Its effect is most apparent in the growth, development, and expansion of all plants, and this is most evident in things that spread across the earth. 

From the first quadrature until the time it reaches its full light—which is the time of its **opposition** <term>istiqbāl: the Full Moon, 180 degrees from the Sun</term>—its stirring of heat and moisture is balanced. During this period, its movement is slowest, and the growth of all plants is most visibly active in everything it does, including the stirring of heat and moisture in animal bodies, plant structures, and minerals.

From the opposition until the time of its second decline in light <note>The Third Quarter Moon</note>, it continues to stir moisture, though the heat is slightly less than the moisture, or the heat is slightly more <note>The text notes a slight shift in dominance between the two qualities</note>. Its effect on animal bodies, plants, and minerals is that it expands and grows them, moving them toward swelling, spreading, and unfolding; however, its expansion through heat is greater than through moisture.

(From the middle of the second [half of the cycle]) until it is hidden by the Sun's rays, its action and stirring of heat is very slight—much less than in the three preceding phases. It is even said that, by comparison, it dries slightly and cools significantly. This is because its stirring of moisture is reduced, which is why we said it is permissible to say that it cools significantly and dries slightly in this fourth phase.

When it meets the Sun at the exact same minute, this is its fifth state. Among the **Chaldeans** <note>The ancient Babylonians, famous for their foundational role in astrology</note>, this is its best state and the one where its action is most powerful. (Among the **Indians**), it is its most corrupted state, where its actions and powers are at their weakest. Among the **Persians**, its strength, weakness, and the increase or decrease of its action depend on the **Zodiac sign** <term>al-burj</term> in which the conjunction with the Sun occurs. 

(As for) the **Greeks** and the **Egyptians**, they believe that its conjunction with the Sun makes it most powerful, as we mentioned. They do not say, as some do, that its best state is when it is full of light; rather, they hold that joining with the Sun makes it uniquely powerful. This is because it is its finest state and the most potent for its action. They have all agreed since antiquity that the Moon's best states are defined by its shape and its distance or proximity to the Sun. When it joins the Sun at the same minute, this fifth state is governed by different rules than the other four. It is its most sublime and powerful state because it "empties" itself into the conjunction with the Sun. Just as a traveler is most effective when returning from a journey to his homeland, even if he had previously moved through various regions, in the conjunction, he completes what was lacking and increases what was diminished. They also say that it becomes capable of actions similar to those of the Sun—and this is a great matter and a significant state.

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the influences of the celestial bodies, specifically focusing on the relationship between the Sun and the Moon. It bridges the theoretical arguments of "a certain group" mentioned at the end of the previous page with a detailed explanation of how the Moon acts as a cosmic mirror, manifesting the Sun’s hidden powers into the physical world of humors, seasons, and life cycles.</meta>

And they said that it is at this point that specific properties manifest in all **composite bodies** <term>al-ajsām al-murakkaba: physical objects formed by the mixing of the four elements (fire, air, water, and earth)</term>. One should not understand here that the Moon creates these properties and others on its own; rather, all these actions belong to the **Sun** <term>al-shams</term>. The role of the **Moon** <term>al-qamar</term> is merely to manifest those effects produced by the Sun, bringing them forth from their hidden places and reigniting them after they have been extinguished. Or, we might say more generally: the Moon reveals what was previously hidden. And how can this be said when the Sun is the source from which all things emerge, bringing them from non-existence into being? 

These five states that we have described regarding the Moon’s relationship to the Sun are all similar to the states of animals, plants, and minerals. Likewise, the Moon's phases relative to the Sun correspond to the life stages of all animals: from infancy, to youth, to maturity, to old age, and finally to decrepitude. It also corresponds to the four seasons of the year, as well as the four directions called the **angles of the world** <term>zawāyā al-ʿālam: the cardinal points of the compass</term>... which are the East... the West... the Right (South)... and the North. (And)... from these four directions, the four winds blow. All of these further correspond to the **four humors** <term>al-akhlāṭ al-arbaʿa: blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile</term> within the human body.

All of these meanings and these specific aspects—even if their origin lies in the essence of the stars and the **two luminaries** <term>al-nayyirayn: the Sun and the Moon</term>—are strengthened by their movements. After they take this form, they receive from the powers of the planets' movements things that are transmitted to them. Thus, they possess states that necessitate forms different from their original ones. If this is the case, then all composite bodies are in a state of constant change due to what they receive from the powers of the planets' movements and their alignments with one another. These changes are what are called "constant partial changes." Those first changes mentioned previously—which are the pillars and foundations of things—are called "constant universal changes"; they do not cease or flip, for if they were to flip, the forms of all things would be destroyed. These are the fixed universals. 

The result of what we have presented is that all things—in their states, their beginnings, and their outcomes—follow the state of the Moon relative to the Sun. This is because the cause of accidents befalling vineyards and all plants, and all bodies composed of the stars, comes specifically from the Moon, and is completed by some of the planets. Those subsequent "afflictions" occur due to **eclipses** <term>kusūf</term> of the Moon and eclipses of the planets. Thus, they receive constant change from the two luminaries and the planets. They receive "soundness" from favorable conditions and "corruption" from unfavorable conditions. An eclipse is an accident occurring to the luminaries and planets that resembles the corruption of a composite body. Let no one think that the two luminaries possess any inherent corruption in themselves—neither in their accidental qualities nor in their essences. Our calling these "heavenly afflictions" has a specific meaning: the reason for the occurrence of every affliction that happens to animals, plants, and other bodies composed of the elements is the eclipse of the Moon and the eclipses of the planets.

Let us return to our subject and say: (The duty in all practical works is to look to the position of the Moon, which is the most beneficial of things, the most truthful in testimony, and the greatest indicator of what occurs in the **world of generation and corruption** <term>ʿālam al-kawn wa-l-fasād: the earthly realm where things are born and die</term>). This is because the Moon is the most specifically assigned to its governance. Look to its health alone, ensuring it is away from the **Combust Way** <term>al-ṭarīqa al-muḥtariqa: a specific dangerous area of the zodiac, usually late Libra and early Scorpio</term>. For every undertaking whose beginning is marked by the Moon's safety and rectitude will have a praised outcome and complete benefit. Its duration and stability will depend on the slowness or speed of the motion indicated. 

If the Moon is connected to the **malefics** <term>al-nuḥūs: typically Mars and Saturn</term>, descending toward the South, or in the very last or very first degree of a zodiac sign, it is bad. Or, if it is in its "fall," or "void" from the ruler of its house (meaning the ruler does not "aspect" or see it), or if it is "falling" from a **pivot** <term>al-watad: the four most powerful houses in a chart</term>, or if it is with the **Dragon** <term>al-jawzahar: the lunar nodes</term>, then that beginning will have no stability. The planet from which the Moon is separating and the planet to which the Moon is connecting should be in a pivot or succeeding a pivot, rather than falling, because if the Moon is "falling," there is no good in it unless the position is fixed from the **Ascendant** <term>al-ṭāliʿ</term>. This is true even if the ruler of its house is also falling. For if you find the house of the Moon's ruler in the pivot of the Ascendant, or in the Midheaven, or the eleventh or fifth houses, and it is "oriental" (rising before the sun) and direct in motion, then this is favorable for the matter being started. For example: **Venus** <term>al-Zuhara</term> for matters of women, joy, and agreement; **Jupiter** <term>al-Mushtarī</term> for wealth, religion, and men; and the agreement of **Mercury** <term>ʿUṭārid</term> for writing and messengers.
