<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological timing, transitioning from the theoretical relationship between the Sun and Moon to the practical application of these celestial cycles in magic, alchemy, and daily affairs. It emphasizes the Moon’s role as the intermediary that "delivers" planetary influences to the earthly realm.</meta>

٣٣
(Book 3 — *The Goal of the Wise*) <note>Original: *Ghāyat al-Ḥakīm*, also known in the Latin West as the *Picatrix*.</note>

You should consider, in every work you begin, the Sun and the Moon and the masters of their **exaltations** <term>sharaf: a specific degree in a zodiac sign where a planet’s influence is most powerful</term> and their **bounds** <term>ḥudūd: a division of a zodiac sign governed by a specific planet</term>. Then, look to the **Midheaven** <term>wasaṭ al-samāʾ: the highest point of the sky at the moment of the observation, the tenth astrological house</term>, because whenever you find these two positions clear of **malefics** <term>nuḥūs: planetary influences considered challenging or harmful, usually Saturn and Mars</term>, and their masters—I mean the master of the exaltation or the master of the **Ascendant** <term>al-ṭāliʿ: the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>—are in a good position, then the beginning will be praised, complete, and most excellent. This is especially true if the bright **benefics** <term>al-suʿūd: planets considered helpful or lucky, usually Jupiter and Venus</term> are positioned to the right. 

And if the master of the Ascendant is **oriental** <term>sharīf: here meaning rising before the Sun</term>, it is better, because the rising of the planets indicates overcoming, victory, completion, and speed in achieving one's needs. Conversely, the setting of the planets—even if they are in an **angle** <term>watad: the four most powerful points of a chart: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term>—indicates delay, transfer, and prolongation. 

(And) if you find the Moon in a good position, the work will be completed according to how its master sought its completion and stability. This is especially true if the master of the Ascendant is in an angle and is a benefic. Even if it is a malefic, its position may still be favorable. The most beneficial thing along with that is for **Jupiter** <term>al-mushtarī</term> and **Venus** <term>al-zuhara</term> to be in the Ascendant or aspecting the Ascendant, for that indicates the completion of the work, the best outcome, the hastening of benefit, and the general blessing of the endeavor. This is particularly so if the Moon is **applying** <term>muttaṣil: moving toward an astrological aspect</term> to a benefic, and that benefic is not **decreasing** <term>nāqiṣ: losing light or speed</term> or **retrograde** <term>rājiʿ: appearing to move backward in the sky</term>; such a configuration is suitable for every work. The only exception is if a slave intends to flee from his master or take what does not belong to him <note>The author suggests that "good" astrology shouldn't be used for "bad" social acts like theft or escape</note>.

The Moon is, by precedence, the most worthy of the planets to govern what lies beneath it in the **world of generation and corruption** <term>ʿālam al-kawn wa-l-fasād: the physical, earthly realm where things are born, change, and die</term>. It is the intermediary because it receives the actions of the planets and delivers them to the world of generation and corruption. For this reason, you need to look at what I mentioned first regarding its fortune or misfortune. 

Know its increase from its beginning: from the time it departs from the Sun, it begins to gain strength. Then it changes at its **sextile** <term>tasdīs: an aspect of 60 degrees</term> for several days. Likewise at its **square** <term>tarbīʿ: 90 degrees</term>, its **trine** <term>tathlīth: 120 degrees</term>, and its **opposition** <term>muqābala: 180 degrees</term> to the Sun. Its strength will be in accordance with the planets it connects with at that time, and the quality of the sign in which that square, trine, sextile, or opposition occurs. 

If you find the Moon increasing in its power, that is best for works where an "increase" is desired. If it is decreasing in its light, that is best for works where one desires to diminish or hide things. Thus, from the moment the Moon separates from the Sun until it reaches the **left square** <note>The first quarter phase</note> and then the opposition (Full Moon), it is good for buying, selling, litigation, arguments, and debating matters. As for the period between the opposition and the **right square** <note>The last quarter phase</note>, it is suitable for those seeking justice in disputes and debts, until it reaches its conjunction with the Sun, where it becomes suitable for those who work with knowledge and the pursuit of truth.

(And know) that the best of the lucky Ascendants and planets is when a benefic is in the sign preceding it and a benefic is in the eighth sign from it. Furthermore, the **cardinal signs** <term>al-burūj al-munqaliba: Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn</term> are suitable for every matter involving competition or haste, especially Capricorn and Aries. The **double-bodied signs** <term>dhawāt al-jasadayn: Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces, also known as mutable signs</term> are for those who work with **magic** <term>siḥr</term>, **talismans of illusion** <term>nayranjāt: natural magic or sleight-of-hand</term>, and mental projections. 

(The **fixed signs** are for those making contracts, bindings, the installation of talismans, and whatever the practitioner intends to remain stable). Whenever you want to perform a work that endures and remains, such as matters of the **alchemical craft** <term>al-ṣināʿa al-kīmiyāʾiyya</term>, or a work to bind a spiritual entity, let the Ascendant be a double-bodied sign and the Moon be in a cardinal sign. Whenever you want a work whose stability and strength endure, let the Ascendant be a fixed sign or double-bodied, and the Moon be in a fixed sign, connecting to the master of its house who towers over it, shares with it, and mixes with it in its actions. This produces very elegant and strange effects. 

Likewise, when the Moon is at its closest point to Earth, it performs the actions of the planet beneath it—understand this! In the same way, the sphere of the **fixed stars** acts like the most luminous and perfect sphere, and the sphere of the Moon performs the actions of the **elements** <term>ṭabāʾiʿ: literally "natures," referring to the four elements</term>, especially Fire and Air due to their subtleness. The same applies to **Saturn** <term>zuḥal</term>; it always performs the actions of the fixed stars when it is at its farthest distance from Earth, and it performs the actions of **Jupiter** when it is at its nearest distance and in its specific dignities. This is the case for every planet in relation to what is above it and what is beneath it. This is among the grandest secrets of the work of **talismans** <term>al-ṭalāsim</term> and among the hidden teachings of the masters of this craft, which they do not speak of openly. If we were to hide this from the seekers of this path, it would be a sign of stinginess and greed.

(And know) that the planets that move more slowly have a stronger effect and more wondrous traces, while the faster planets are slightly weaker.

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on planetary cycles, moving into the theoretical physics of planetary "action" and "weight." It establishes the hierarchy of influence between the outer planets—specifically Saturn—and the inner planets like the Moon, explaining how celestial power descends to the Earth.</meta>

٢٤

The planets with moderate movement have a moderate influence in their actions, but this statement is not absolute. Rather, it is a matter of relativity. This principle can be reversed, so that the heaviest planet has the least action, while the fastest has the most and strongest action; this is relative to the relationship between the **Sphere of the Aether** <term>al-falak al-athīr: the highest region of the universe, above the air, composed of a pure, indestructible element</term> and the **Fixed Stars** <term>al-kawākib al-thābitah: the stars that appear to stay in the same position relative to one another, unlike the "wandering" planets</term>, and the movements of earthly beings. As for the relationship to earthly things, whatever is heavier is stronger in action, and whatever is lighter is weaker in action. But regarding the sphere of the Fixed Stars, whatever is lighter is stronger in action, and whatever is heavier is weaker in action, because it is a relationship of similarity. 

A great deal of disagreement has occurred among scholars on this point. Among the ancients, some argued the opposite, making the similarity and relationship based on the Aetheric Sphere and the sphere of the Fixed Stars. According to this view, whichever planet is slowest in its movement must necessarily be closer in similarity to the Aetheric Sphere in its stillness and the "weight" <note>Original: *thaqal*, referring here to the density or gravity of influence rather than physical mass</note> of the stars, because it is slow-moving. This is because simple essences only resemble one another in the simplest conditions they share, as they are of the same nature. Thus, the slow-moving is like the slow-moving, and the fast-moving is like the fast-moving in this context. Those who came before left behind much discourse and difficult symbols on this, and I shall remind you in this place of a secret from the secrets of the ancients who were devoted to **Talismans** <term>al-ṭilsamāt: magical objects or images constructed under specific astrological conditions to capture celestial power</term>.

( Know that the Moon ) when it is with **Saturn** <note>Original: *Zuḥal*</note>, the action belongs to Saturn, because the action of Saturn is much stronger than the action of the Moon. Every planet that conjoins Saturn must fall under its influence. This is because its power is stronger than the powers of all the specialized planets <note>Original: *al-kawākib al-mutaḥayyizah*, likely referring to the planets with specific directional or elemental assignments</note> due to the conditions we mentioned. This is either because of its high altitude, its proximity to the two great spheres of the Whole—meaning the Aether and the sphere of the Fixed Stars—or because of its slow movement and its strong similarity to the Aetheric sphere, and other similar reasons mentioned in such cases.

( And the Conjunctions ) are such that **Jupiter** <note>Original: *al-Mushtarī*</note>, the **Sun**, or **Venus** <note>Original: *al-Zuhrah*</note>, when they mix with the actions of Saturn, they change them completely and improve them to some degree. The same is true for the Fixed Stars in their relationship to Jupiter. This influence continues to flow in a single, consistent, and unchanging order down to the Moon. Thus, the Moon's actions manifest in the elements of Fire and Air, and the actions of Fire and Air manifest in Water and Earth. The actions of these four elements then become effective in the secondary physical existence derived from them. 

It is possible for every two planets in **conjunction** <term>iqtirān: when two celestial bodies appear in the same part of the sky</term> to have three states: excess, deficiency, or a mean between the two. For example, it is possible for the Moon to be with Saturn and for its action to be subsumed under the action (meaning the power) of Saturn. This occurs when they are in **alignment** <term>al-musāmatat: a state where planets share the same zenith or celestial path</term>, in **exaltation** <term>al-sharaf: a planet's most powerful position in the zodiac</term>, or at **apogee** <term>al-awj: the point in a planet's orbit furthest from the Earth</term>. In such places, and nowhere else, if the Moon and Saturn follow the same path, this is the case. This also applies to Saturn's state in all other positions and for all planets. 

However, it is also possible for the action and power of the Moon to be much greater than the power of Saturn if the Moon is in those dominant positions while Saturn is in their opposites. If the Moon and Saturn are both in the opposites of those noble positions equally, the action of the Moon would still follow the action of Saturn, as in the first case. When Saturn is in those high positions and the Moon is in its own specific places, the Moon's action is entirely weak. When Saturn is in **direct motion** or **retrograde** <term>al-istiqāmah wa al-rujūʿ: the apparent forward or backward movement of a planet across the sky</term> and similar changes from the balanced path, the two actions become almost equal, especially if the Moon is at its highest point. This is because the Moon's maximum height does not parallel Saturn's lowest point, let alone its point of direct motion, but that is its condition. 

As for the relationship between Saturn and Jupiter, it is not like that of Saturn and the Moon; rather, they are nearly equal in most cases. If Saturn is high and Jupiter is also high, the action is stronger for Jupiter—unless Jupiter is high while Saturn is in its middle path, or in its exaltation or direct motion, in which case the action does not belong to Jupiter alone. If the conditions differ from that, the action belongs to Saturn, and Jupiter's action is nullified for less than that. Likewise, in the case of **Mars** <note>Original: *al-Mirrīkh*</note> and Saturn, if they are together, the actions are more apparent for the higher Mars, following the conditions previously mentioned. As for Venus and **Mercury** <note>Original: *ʿUṭārid*</note>, they have a relationship and similarity to every planet, but the Moon is not like them.

<summary>This page discusses the hierarchy of planetary influence, explaining how the "weight" and speed of planets like Saturn determine their dominance over others, especially the Moon. It details how celestial power flows from the highest spheres down through the elements of Fire, Air, Water, and Earth to affect the physical world.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theoretical discussion of planetary influences, transitioning from the concept of planetary "weight" and movement to the metaphysical foundations of how celestial forces interact with matter. ٣٥

Because it lacks a direct ratio or likeness to the higher planets, this principle serves as a foundation upon which things are built. Such concepts are necessary for these incidental correspondences, and the need for them is paramount in the science of talismans.

(Know) that the actions of the planets belong to their very essences because they are **simple essences** <term>basāʾit: substances not composed of smaller parts, making them indestructible in medieval physics</term>. Whatever is simple is not subject to corruption. Likewise, if a simple thing were to be corrupted, it would cease to have existence. Corruption only occurs in **composites** <term>murakkabāt: things made of different elements or parts, like earthly objects</term> which are composed of various elements.

(Know) that the action of the **Universal Sphere** <term>falak al-kull: also known as the Primum Mobile, the outermost sphere that moves all others</term> of the East is the absolute, general action for all spheres and all planets. The reason for this is that it is the cause of their movement. In truth, it is the absolute "Dweller" <note>Original: *al-musākin*. This likely refers to the "Unmoved Mover" concept—the entity that initiates all motion while remaining still itself</note>, for it is the mover of all spheres. Therefore, it is called the "Mover of All," yet it is not itself moving, as that would be an impossibility. As for the sphere of the **Fixed Stars**, although its movement is general, it is specific in relation to the movement of the Universal Sphere. The same applies to the rest of the spheres. It will be shown to you that the movement of every sphere towards the planets within it is general to those planets, the spheres, and all their other movements. Furthermore, the cause of the planets' actions and their essential deeds is the movement of their respective spheres. This is because planets do not move by themselves incidentally. Thus, the action of the sphere is twofold: **motion** by essence, and **heat** by accident, as heat is something that arises as a consequence of motion. Motion, as we have said, is the very "being" of the sphere. The ultimate goal in talismans is to set a receptive object into motion or to bring it from **potentiality into actuality** <note>This refers to the Aristotelian transition from *quwwa* (the power to be something) to *fiʿl* (the state of being it)</note>. This is because the "Form" contains the likeness of everything, while "Matter" contains the potential to receive everything. Every piece of matter has a "Form"—that is, a temperament—which it carries. For example: a small fire grows and becomes a great fire because the small fire draws out the fire that exists in the **Hyle** <term>al-hayūlā: prime matter; the formless "stuff" that has the potential to become any physical thing</term> through potentiality until it exists by its own essence and multiplies as it becomes actual fire.

(Know) that a "contrary" does not enter into what is already **actual** to corrupt it. Rather, it enters into what is **potential** to corrupt it. Whatever follows the natural course is balanced, and whatever goes against the natural course is imbalanced. Because of this, that which is actually hot naturally moves that which is potentially hot. When a hot thing provides heat, it does so through the principle and cause within it that has the power to heat. Just as that which is actually hot moves that which is potentially hot, that which is potentially hot is naturally moved by that which is actually hot; for just as the former has the principle of action within itself, the latter has the principle of "being acted upon" within itself. Know this well.

# Section

From what we have mentioned, one can understand the nature of **reception** and **gifts** <note>Terms used to describe how an earthly object "receives" celestial influence and how the planet "gives" its power</note> in the actions and works of talismans, and the manner of their correspondence and variation. **Similarity** is a category of "relation" in the work of talismans. Specifically, it is the similarity that exists through the work between the action of the planets and the action of the **stone** from which the talisman is made, as well as the time, the place, and the setting of the talisman. It is by the combination of these elements that a talisman exists, and by their absence, the talisman is nullified.

"Relation" (or Addition) is one of the great foundations in the existence of a talisman. This is also true for **Quantity**. For knowledge, as is said in the **Mathematical Sciences** <term>al-taʿālīm: specifically the Quadrivium (arithmetic, geometry, music, and astronomy)</term>, is divided into two major parts: the **continuous** and the **discrete**.
The continuous part is divided into five types: the **Line**, the **Surface**, the **Body**, **Time**, and **Place**.
The discrete part is divided into **Speech** and **Number**.
All of these elements may be required in the construction of talismans. The **Line** is required for the **Alignment** <term>al-musāmatha: the geometric line of sight or path of influence between a star and an object</term> that exists between the planets and the talisman, and for the alignment between the talisman and the object intended to be affected by the talisman's action through **Likeness** and **Opposition**. These two divisions of the "Line" regarding alignment are what are described in the Mathematical Sciences as the "Straight Line," which is their saying...

[The text continues] within it: that the **straight line** is that which is placed in opposition, where any points are aligned one with another. This is the most complete definition given regarding the nature of the **talisman** <term>tilasm: an object inscribed with astronomical signs and used to channel celestial power</term>. This is because the straight line is the means by which the process of the desired action is completed in a single passage, from the starting point of the origin to the points of contact with the object where it terminates. This is similar to saying "So-and-so is facing so-and-so" or "is in their line of sight," and other such descriptions—provided that nothing obstructs what travels along that straight line. An example of this is the falling of the points of the **planetary rays** <term>shuʿāʿāt: the influential light or energy emitted by stars and planets</term>, for it is a single point that falls upon the physical surface prepared to receive the ray. Know this and understand it clearly.

The reason why this statement is the most complete of all sayings regarding the nature of the talisman is that the **celestial line** aimed at for the talisman's action is also a "ray-casting" specifically for the essence of the action of that stone or the talisman placed for the work in terms of opposition and similarity. Thus, it is a necessary duty that the line extending from the planets to the talisman be **straight** so that the "gift" <note>The author uses the word "gift" ('atā') to describe the flow of spiritual or physical influence from a planet to an object.</note> may be complete. If the line deviates from being straight in the manner we have described, that gift will not be complete.

As for the **Surface**, it is by necessity the "image of the gift." This is because the surface represents the spreading of the talisman's action in that location. When the action spreads, it must necessarily become a surface. The reason for this is that the air itself is transformed by that action, just as it is transformed by heat, cold, scent, light, colors, and the like. The thing upon which the lines extending from the planets to the talisman—and from the talisman to the location—are completed is—[know and understand these hidden secrets]. The scholars of the past were not generous in revealing the cause of these things.

As for **Time**, it follows the movement of the celestial body. It is intended to clarify the matter of the talisman itself. In the works of talismans, time is divided into several parts:
*   The time of **observing the planet** <term>rasad: astronomical observation for the purpose of calculation</term> at the position where its gift of action is at its peak or its lowest, based on the degrees of planetary deviation.
*   The time required for observing the **degree** <note>In astrology, specific degrees of the zodiac can hold more power than the planet itself.</note> which possesses an action truly greater than the action of the planets themselves.
*   The time of observing the planet when it is necessary for it to be in **conjunction** with another planet, whether in the same degree, or in a position of **opposition**, **trine**, **square**, or similar placements <note>These are "aspects," or the geometric angles between planets that determine their influence.</note> required of the planetary positions. There, they grant their actions—either complete or otherwise—according to the modes of action such as being "direct," "in fall," or "in exaltation."

This includes the "gifts" and the "cutoff of gifts," such as **auspicious and inauspicious** <term>suʿūd and nuhūs: lucky or unlucky planetary influences</term>, the arrival of light, traces, voluntary action, and all these matters spoken of in this science. This is the **Great Secret** within the secret of the talisman, following that path that attracts or repels to carry what is said regarding similarity and opposition. In the science of **Elections** <term>al-ikhtiyārāt: the branch of astrology concerned with choosing the best time to start an endeavor</term>, this is the pillar of the craft of talismans and its key. For this reason, the **Lawgiver** <note>Referring to religious authority or the Prophet, who prohibited astrology in certain contexts.</note> forbade diving into the science of the stars, because knowledge of it leads to the science of talismans. In the revealing of this, there is much for those who contemplate what they intended.

This is why the Sage **Aristotle** intended to say to **Alexander**:
> "O Alexander, if you are able to make no movement among your movements except by the likeness and agreement of a heavenly movement, then do so. You shall find no way to do this except through **Election**; then you shall reach your desire and attain your hope."

How excellent is the Sage's advice! For this is the difference between the pursuit of the learned and the pursuit of the ignorant, who *know only the outward appearance of the life of this world, and are heedless of the hereafter.* <note>The author concludes by quoting the Quran (Surah Ar-Rum 30:7) to contrast the deep, hidden knowledge of the "wise" with the superficial knowledge of the masses.</note>

### Chapter

Know that regarding the art of **talismans**, the ancients believed that the quarters of the celestial sphere move **eight** <note>original: ح (Hā), the Abjad numerical equivalent of 8</note> degrees forward and eight degrees backward. This has been neglected by some practitioners of **astronomical tables** <term>Zij: a set of mathematical tables used to calculate the positions of the sun, moon, and planets</term> in their calculations. Yet, it is within this movement that the true benefit of this art—meaning talismans—is found.

The method to achieve knowledge of this, as has been verified, is to learn the date of the reign of **Augustus** <note>original: Ighatatish</note>. The recession of those eight degrees ended during his time, and then their advancement began thereafter. Then, add to these years three hundred and thirteen years (313), which is the time from the beginning of the reign of **Augustus** to the beginning of the reign of **Diocletian** <note>original: Dighlatiyash</note>. To this, we add the years of **Diocletian** and calculate one degree for every eighty years, for the sphere moves one degree every eighty years. Whatever total we gather, we subtract from eight hundred, and whatever remains is added to the calculation of the Sun, the Moon, and the **five wandering stars** <term>the five planets visible to the naked eye: Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</term>.

By this method, one reaches the works they seek in this art. Do not neglect the matter of the "advancing" and "receding" of the sphere, for it is the foundation of talismans for the sake of the **forms** <note>The 'forms' or 'images' refer to the specific shapes engraved on a talisman to capture planetary power.</note>; know this. This is among their most hidden secrets. These eight degrees of advancing and receding are completed in six hundred and forty-six years (646). I have already informed you where the recession ended, and by that, you may know the times of advancing and receding. This is also urgently needed for the art of **astral influence**. This advancing and receding is caused by the movement of the pole of the **ecliptic** <term>falk al-buruj: the path the sun appears to follow across the sky</term>. Movement occurs from East to West or West to East; no other way is valid. When it begins its advancement, it indicates events that will occur in the world, and likewise, when it begins its recession, it indicates other events caused by that recession. Know, moreover, that these movements belong to the **zodiacal sphere** and not the **straight sphere** <note>The 'straight sphere' refers to the celestial equator's alignment.</note>.

### ( Chapter )

I have seen wonders regarding this subject from a leader among the predecessors in this art. I thought I should recount them. He divided this science into three branches:

1.  **The Science of Talismans**: He stated that the people most concerned with it and most successful in it are the **Nabateans** of the **Chaldeans** <note>Ancient Mesopotamian peoples renowned in the Middle Ages for their expertise in magic and astrology.</note>.
2.  **The Science of the Stars**: Dealing with them through offerings, incense, and rituals. The **Greeks** specialized in this second type, and they are also superior in the foundation of both branches, which is the **Science of the Stars**—the primary and decisive science.
3.  **The Science of Conjurations** <term>Qalfatriyyat: a term for specific incantations or magical operations</term>: and charms using pure, sincere intent, and the knowledge of the spirits who are subject to them, and the relation of those words through oral transmission. This science is one they uniquely specialized in and cared for. Each of these sciences has scientific and practical foundations and principles.

It is famously reported of the **People of India** that they possess effective charms that serve as cures for lethal poisons. They have words by which they strike people down, mesmerizing the mind upon hearing them and distracting it through their movement. They possess "musical signs" played on an instrument they call the **Kankala** <note>likely the Kinkila or Ektara, an ancient one-stringed instrument</note>. It has a single string on a single body, with which they produce whatever types of sounds and fine melodies they desire.

(And they have wonders regarding **conception**)... among them is that a woman may conceive without a man touching her, through certain movements they direct toward her. They possess a drink that prevents aging, stooping, and natural decay; this is specific to them alone. They are the most capable of people in **magic and illusion**. They are the ones who believe that beyond the **equator** to the south, there is civilization. They are those who are referred to by the titles of **Jinn and Demons**, being a subtle world that is not seen, yet they reproduce and die; and the Religious Law has agreed with them on this.

They claim that their guide in the heavens is **Saturn** and the **Tail** <note>original: al-dhanab; referring to the Descending Lunar Node, often associated with endings or malefic influence</note>. According to the history of one of their sages—the one they refer to as **Adam**—it was the beginning of their celestial progression for the sake of his expulsion. This leader in the craft claimed that every form in the world of **generation and corruption** <term>the sublunary world where things are born and decay</term> originates from the configuration of the **fixed stars**, which are formed by that specific type of arrangement in the heavens. He also claimed that in the heavens, there are forms that do not exist on Earth. These are used by scholars of **spiritual arts** <term>Ruhaniya: a branch of occult science dealing with spirits and celestial energies</term> and **talismans** by renewing known ratios and calling them, by convention, **seals**.

<image-desc>Five small circles arranged in two rows: three in the top row and two in the bottom row.</image-desc> Like this image, after connecting them with lines; those lines extend from one to another until their specific shapes are achieved. This occurs in the **eighth sphere** <note>In traditional cosmology, the sphere containing the fixed stars, located beyond the seven planetary spheres</note>, and thus this form becomes:

<image-desc>A complex talismanic sigil or diagram composed of intersecting lines with small circles at the ends, resembling a stylized astrological or magical character.</image-desc> People of observation and the spiritual arts recognize it. For this reason, it is what the **exorcists**, **healers**, and **illusionists** recount, perfect, and depict. However, these are not the actual stars in the sky formed by the fixed stars. This scholar claimed that among the forms of the spheres are **illusory forms** that are not actually depicted, but rather their significance comes from combined degrees. This wondrous approach was only discovered by this man through the **books of India**, who are deeply immersed in this science. They believe in using them according to those known ratios at specific renewed times and known **ascendants** <term>the sign of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment</term>. They are the ones who use **omens** and **augury by birds** <term>Zajr: the practice of divining the future by observing the flight or sounds of birds</term>, as well as divination to strengthen their certainty regarding questions, secrets, and the uncovering of buried treasures. They seek help from these just as they seek help from **halos**, **parhelia**, **rainbows**, **meteors**, **comets**, and the **dimming of the sun** to understand celestial omens. All of these provide aid and strength for what is intended. 

They said: in the sphere, there are beautiful and ugly forms, which are the configuration of the fixed stars. If a being is found and the ascendant is a beautiful form, it indicates **happiness** for that being. If a child is born while the ascendant is an ugly form, and the **Luminaries** <note>The Sun and the Moon</note> are in an ugly form, it indicates **misfortune** for that newborn. The same applies to **revolutions**, **horary questions**, and **syzygies**. They also claim that among the configurations are the **confused stars** <note>original: nujum adghath al-nawm; a play on the Quranic phrase for "confused dreams," referring here to stars whose influences are too mixed to be interpreted</note> which signify nothing because of their muddled nature; one must be cautious and wait for others. This is often taken into account in nativities, questions, and revolutions. 

They claim that the **science of dreams** comes from the power of the soul and its connection to the world of the spheres. There, the soul sees the forms of things that will happen on Earth, and they are imprinted upon it. This is the **true vision**, and it is a companion to the science of the stars, a possessor of it, and similar to it. Thus, the soul seeks help through it, and it is of a **Mercurial power**. This is because **Mercury** indicates the power of vision in nativities when it is strong. Dreams may also arise from the **inner dialogue** of the soul and the **bodily humors**.

Know, may God honor you, that the reality of the dream is the representation of individual things stripped of their physical carrier. It occurs when the soul ceases using the external senses and employs **intellection**. This is for the **imaginative faculty** to assemble the traces of sensory things it finds within itself and to distinguish some from others. It has three functions, one of which is **mimesis** <term>Muhaka'a: the mind's ability to imitate or represent reality through symbols</term. If the **rational soul** is at its perfection, the dreamer sees the thing and it occurs exactly as it was seen. If the imaginative faculty is more dominant, the dream comes out as a symbolic meaning; for example, in a perfect soul, if one sees a dog or a predator fighting him, it manifests exactly as it is. But if the imaginative faculty has perfection, it manifests as a **policeman** or a **thief**, which is the symbolic meaning. 

If the nature is strong and the soul is preoccupied with what nourishes it—such as sexual intercourse, clothing, and everything it is busy with while awake—it appears in the dream as a preoccupation with pleasures and women. For example, if a person is "congested" and does not have intercourse despite the fullness of his body, he will have a wet dream. (If his body) is full of **moisture**, he sees water and rivers in his sleep. If his temperament is dominated by **cold and dryness** <note>the Melancholic humor</note>, he sees nonsensical, frightening things. (And if his temperament is **hot yellow bile**) <note>the Choleric humor</note>, he sees fires, ovens, and the like; so know that. Just as the reality of **augury** <term>Zajr</term> is **intuition**, it occurs after reflecting on the matter being divined—either by an image one looks at or by what one hears during reflection—and by releasing the imaginative powers to look into it.

-> - 39 - <-

What he desires of knowledge, if his imaginative faculty is strong, portrays it for him like someone looking into a reflective mirror. He seeks aid from what he has seen and heard; when he hears, it leads him to a certain type of knowledge, just as the imaginative faculties differentiate when the senses are at rest <note>Referring to the state of sleep or deep meditation where external sensory input ceases</note>. It mediates between the sensible things seen in his wakefulness and combines them with speech, and from this, a vision is formed. If it is in proper proportion, and the body and its **humors** <note>original: al-akhlat; the four bodily fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—believed to govern health and temperament</note> are in balance, it is a "true vision." If it is otherwise, it may be "muddled dreams" <note>A Quranic term for confused or meaningless nightmares</note>.

You should know that **divination** <note>original: al-kahana</note> is an effect of the **Fifth Essence** <note>The Ether or celestial substance that makes up the heavens, distinct from the four earthly elements</note> called **revelation**. It is a power among the soul’s imaginative faculties that penetrates into specific details until it encompasses them and reflects them. One then reports them, either in sleep or wakefulness. This is because when the imaginative faculty is at its perfection, specific details are present before it. These details become, as we said, like a mirror; they are reflected, he sees them in their perfection, and he reports them. However, he does not exceed being perfect in **particulars** <note>Specific, individual things or events in the physical world</note>. Therefore, he does not reach perfection in **intellectual universals** <note>Purely abstract or philosophical concepts</note>. If he is unique in universals and penetrates them, he is a **sage** <note>original: hakim; a philosopher or wise man</note>. But if he penetrates both these and those, he is a **prophet**. This only occurs in rare individuals to whom revelation comes in its perfection from the **First Creator**. The Creator reveals to him through the mediation of the **Active Intellect** <term>the bridge between the divine mind and human reason</term>, so that what flows from the First Creator to the Active Intellect then flows from the Active Intellect to the **passive intellect** through the mediation of the **acquired intellect**, and into his imaginative faculty. Thus, by what flows into his passive intellect, he is a sage; and by what flows into his imaginative faculty, he is a prophet. This person is at the most perfect rank of humanity and the highest degree of **happiness** <note>original: sa'ada; referring to Eudaimonia or supreme flourishing</note>. It is for this reason that every good is sought, and in it, every good ends. We only seek virtues to be happy, and we use as means for that the refinement of character, the management of the household, and the management of the nation—uniting them upon a single word that leads them to happiness and the knowledge of existing things. This is the "theoretical part": how things are, why they are, and for what purpose they exist, beginning from sensible things and ascending from them to divine matters according to human capacity. This is the truly happy person and the **Perfect Human**. Happiness is the good sought for its own sake; it is never sought at any time to attain something else, for there is nothing more perfect beyond happiness. To this, **Abu Nasr al-Farabi** <note>A famous 10th-century philosopher known as the "Second Teacher"</note> alluded in his treatise on the <unclear>Snoriya</unclear> result:

"The best of matters may be causes for the action of their greatest parts. The actions by which happiness is attained are the beautiful actions, the exercise of kingship, and the volitions from which good things are portrayed. He who reaches them delights in an eternity with no passing, a joy with no grief, a knowledge with no ignorance, and a wealth with no poverty..."

Returning to our purpose, we say: The people of India claim that a **spiritual being** <note>original: ruhaniya; a celestial or spiritual entity associated with a planet or star</note> may appear to a spiritual scholar as a person, speaking to him and teaching him whatever matters he wishes. It may endear him to kings and rulers, and bind or loosen whatever actions he desires. These actions appear in the <term>Barabi</term> <note>Ancient Egyptian or "pagan" temples, often viewed as sites of preserved ancient magic</note> which their ancestors built in various forms, answering the caller in whatever he desires. They claim that <term>talismans</term> <note>Magical objects created by combining celestial influences with physical materials</note> are more effective than mere **astrological elections** because they are aided by their "universal nature." They are like **miracles** because they summon **natural properties** <note>original: al-khawass; the hidden, occult qualities of substances</note>. Indeed, these properties can perform wonders on their own, such as the <term>Red Ruby</term> <note>original: yaqut ahmar</note> protecting its wearer from diseases like the plague and others. A talisman can do this because it combines the "choice" <note>The specific astrological timing</note> and the "property"—such as repelling mosquitoes, fleas, and flies through the celestial ratio and the material of the bodies it is made from. In the existence of a thing, its command is a natural property. The purpose here in mentioning "property" is so that you may know the true essence of a talisman, and know that everything made in this world that seeks a specific effect is, in a way, a talisman. You shall see this.

It establishes a philosophical link between natural biological generation and the artificial creation of magical objects.</meta>

## Section

The purpose of mentioning **occult properties** <term>khawass: specific, often hidden qualities of a substance that produce effects through their nature rather than through obvious physical mechanics</term> is for you to know that a thing acting by its natural temperament may change and its efficacy may diminish. However, if a thing’s natural temperament acts in such a way that the action is a specific property of that thing, its action is more complete and powerful, and the result is more apparent and superior. An example of this is **scammony** <note>original: al-saqmunya; a plant resin used historically as a potent purgative</note> attracting **yellow bile** <note>original: al-safra; one of the four humors or bodily fluids in medieval medicine</note>. It attracts it through both similarity and its specific property. The cause for this is that scammony is hot and dry, and it is simultaneously similar to the nature of yellow bile.

Know that when the acting medicine has a temperament specific to the task, the action resulting from it is stronger and the **talisman** <note>original: al-tilsam; an object inscribed with symbols to harness celestial power</note> is more magnificent. This is evident from the "image of bestowal" from the planets and also from the perspective of "reception," as the bestowal becomes more perfect.

**An example of this** is to intend the "image of reception" at the very beginning of the talisman’s creation. If its form receives the gift from the planets with full reception, the purpose of the talisman is achieved, its effect persists, and its **spirituality** <term>ruhaniyya: the subtle spiritual essence or planetary energy that inhabits a physical object</term> spreads. An example of this, so you may see it clearly, is in the gift of the planets regarding the chosen matter and the image of reception from them. People are making talismans at all times, yet they do not realize it. For if you wish to generate an animal, compose a plant, or form something from stones, you first intend to gather the parts of that thing. You collect their constituent parts first, then bury, grind, or mix them—or other types of labor—until your action upon them is complete. Meanwhile, nature does not cease its work, and the planets govern and complete it until the thing reaches the final end intended for it.

This is like the production of many dyes, the generation of animals—specifically the production of bees, snakes, and scorpions, and all that is mentioned in this book—likewise the case in cooking, in mixtures, and in the composition of medicines. A similar case is the ripening in the wombs of various types of semen until they reach the ends where nature and the planets perform their actions gradually. The same applies to the **mineral mine** <note>original: al-ma'dan</note>. Water, in the generation of stones, is at first like semen; then winds continue to strike the interior of the mine until it becomes foam. Then the "cooker" of the mine and the surrounding natural elements remain devoted to it until it solidifies into its perfect and intended form. The case is exactly the same for plants and their formation.

This is the example of the "first change" and its return to the state of moist matter, which is the subject of "becoming" through the reception of likenesses and forms. Then, when it reaches that state, the formation of the likeness begins, through the "renewed reception" it attains. This is different from the first reception; it only gains this reception through the dew and moisture it acquires at that time. In this same way, everything generated by craft must lead to these types of **metamorphoses** <note>original: istihalat; the process of one substance changing into another</note> and "becoming." Whoever does not undergo "becoming" cannot sustain the forms placed within it at all.

Every maker and worker who produces something through generation, formation, and similar labors intends first to prepare the parts from which that generated thing is made, until those parts become a "primary subject matter" ready to receive a second form. Thus you find the maker of **starch** <note>original: al-nasha'</note> from food, and **vermicelli** <note>original: al-itriyya; a traditional type of pasta or dough</note> from starch, and items made from types of sweets. Similarly, the maker of *kamakh* <note>a savory fermented condiment</note>, cheese, and butter from milk. Likewise the spinner of thread from cotton and all other such works. The cause for this is that no matter possessing a form can accept another form unless that first matter is corrupted <note>meaning its previous form is broken down</note>, so it gains a "second reception" and can then accept another form. For when it accepts any form whatsoever, it becomes void of all other remaining forms. This is said only of the **fluid matter** <term>al-madda al-sayyala: the transient, changeable matter of the physical world below the moon, as opposed to the eternal matter of the heavens</term> found in this world.

<summary>This page explains that the effectiveness of talismans and medicines depends on matching the "occult properties" of materials with celestial influences. It argues that all generation—whether natural (animals, minerals) or artificial (cooking, crafts)—follows a universal process of breaking down old forms to allow matter to receive new ones.</summary>
<keywords>occult properties (khawass), talismans, planetary bestowal, yellow bile, scammony, generation and corruption, mineralogy, alchemy of food, fluid matter</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the general theory of occult properties to the specific application of these principles in the construction of talismans. It establishes a hierarchy between the "Higher World" (the celestial spheres) and the "Lower World" (material existence), using analogies from medicine and physics to explain how celestial power is captured and concentrated.</meta>

->### As for the Higher World ###<-

It is either matter in actuality, making it an eternal form, or it is a singular form without matter. Now that the intent of our goal has been clarified through these examples, let us understand that similar principles apply to the **talisman** <note>original: al-tilsam</note> and the manner of its creation. The maker of a talisman seeks, through its location or the material from which it is fashioned, to create a single point of focus that possesses perfection in its ability to receive a specific "form." 

This is similar to the observable phenomenon where **laurel berries** <note>original: habb al-ghar; the fruit of the bay laurel tree</note> are uniquely effective against snake venom, or how **saffron** <note>original: al-za'faran</note> alone repels geckos, or how the wasp flees from sour and bitter things yet seeks out rose water and is drawn to the scent of **aromatic herbs** <note>original: al-hasha</note> and attracted by its incense. Another example is the way **chickpeas** <note>original: al-himmis</note> and similar items increase semen. 

The "body" of the talisman, which is the physical object, is often composed of several materials joined together so that through their combination, they achieve the capacity to receive the specific influence being sought. This is the same principle behind the medicines that physicians prescribe to treat illnesses and pains. These medicines are either "simples" <term>simples: medicinal herbs or minerals used on their own without being mixed</term>, which are used individually—this is the method of the philosopher and the perfect physician. **Ibn Masawayh** <note>Yuhanna ibn Masawayh (d. 857 AD), a famous Nestorian Christian physician in Baghdad</note> alluded to this when he said: "As long as you are able to treat with a simple medicine, do not treat with a compound one." 

However, medicines may also be compounded from a group of ingredients to achieve a specific goal. Consider the visible effects produced by simple medicines, such as those found in **electuaries** <term>ma'junat: medicinal pastes made by mixing powders with honey or syrup</term>. It was for this reason that the **Great Theriac** <note>original: al-tiryaq al-kabir; a legendary "universal" antidote and cure-all used for centuries</note> was formulated. This logic also applies to foods: some are simple, like fruits, while others are compound, like various types of sweets and prepared dishes.

Know that a single star can produce different effects. This is like fire used to heat honey: if the heat is moderate, it gives the honey a delicious taste, but if the heat increases, it scorches the honey, ruining its consistency and turning the taste bitter and loathsome. The actions of the stars are similar, depending on whether they are located in the "luminous" or "dark" **degrees** <term>daraj: the 360 divisions of the zodiacal circle</term>. 

The **Celestial Sphere** <note>original: al-falak; the rotating heavens</note> has two primary actions: motion by its very essence, and heat by accident. Heat is an occurrence that follows motion; thus, motion generates heat, and motion is the very state of the Sphere’s existence. This is a description pertaining to the senses. As for that which pertains to the Intellect, motion is the existence of the **Ether** <term>al-athir: the "fifth element" or quintessence that makes up the heavens</term> in the sphere of the fixed stars, driven by a longing—a desire for virtue and absolute goodness. Thus, heat is necessarily a follower of motion, and motion is a follower of the "First Perfection," which nothing precedes.

Know that all parts of the Sphere, the sum of its movements, and the total heat arising from it follow this state. Following the actions of the Sphere are the actions of the planets. This is because the Sphere acts *through* the planets; the planets do not act upon the Sphere, as the celestial bodies themselves are stationary in relation to the Sphere and have no independent motion of their own. Within the planets, alongside these brilliant actions and wondrous gifts, there are qualities that are not found in the Sphere itself...

The **Trace** <term>al-athar: the residual impact or spiritual imprint left by a celestial force</term> is the "First Nature" which truly acts. The gift of the planets, in addition to this, is merely a "dyeing" or a "clothing," similar to the way we use dyes and garments, though the celestial version is far more excellent and magnificent.

The **degrees** <note>original: al-daraj</note> in the Sphere have no physical reality in themselves; they are established merely by convention, agreement, and conceptualization. This is because there is no part of the Sphere that is "active" and another that is "inactive"; rather, in its essence and nature, it is a single unity. There is no difference whatsoever between its parts and its whole, in any state or at any time...

Yet, a degree is never truly empty of the actions and relationships of the **fixed stars** <note>original: al-kawakib al-thabita</note>, for the sphere of the fixed stars is filled with them. It is only said that a degree is "empty" in relation to the observations of the "moving planets," because it is not within the power <unclear>of human observation to account for all</unclear>

It details the relationship between the movement of the celestial spheres, the generation of heat, and the specific "gifts" or properties bestowed by planets and fixed stars.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 41 regarding the practical application of celestial influences. While the previous page focused on the physical composition of talismans using materials like laurel and saffron, this page shifts to the precise astrological timing and planetary conditions required for these objects—and actions in general—to be effective.</meta>

Comprehending all the stars in the heavens is entirely beyond human perception and sensation because they cannot be reached, and their positions are fixed in such a remote place. 

The placement of the **planets** <note>original: al-kawakib; in this context, the seven traditional wandering stars of antiquity</note> in a specific location is not a simple matter. One cannot merely say a planet is in its degree, its exaltation, its "face" <term>face: a subdivision of a zodiac sign, also known as a decan</term>, its fall, its retrograde motion, or its direct motion. Rather, it may be on the path toward any of these states. Furthermore, it might not have a compositional relationship through the "aspects" of the planets—neither in opposition nor in agreement. Even if these positions are active, they are as if inactive when compared to the fixed positions of the planets' primary actions. This is because planets do not remain stationary in the sky; thus, they would be inactive. This is a subtle and hidden point, so contemplate it well... for even if they are active in essence, they perform two types of actions: **general** and **specific**. The general action is that which is directed toward the "direction."

An example of this is what is called "giving," "friendship," "relationship," and "composition," which is a single reception and a favor. 

Another example is the state of food in the stomach. When the **liver** attracts it, the food is always transformed into the likeness of blood within the veins. When the liver then distributes it from the organs, it is always transformed into the substance of those various organs; it is as if what was blood becomes blood, and what was bone becomes bone. In the same way, the planets specify those compositional relationships, and the planets also have a cause for performing their **noble works**.

This occurs when a planet is at its peak, which is called the **apogee** <note>original: al-awj</note>. It performs the work of the planets through a **trine** <term>trine: an aspect where planets are 120 degrees apart, considered very harmonious</term> or a **sextile** <term>sextile: an aspect of 60 degrees, considered favorable</term>, provided the "lord of its house" is free from **malefics** <term>malefics: the planets Saturn and Mars, traditionally associated with hardship</term>, **combustion** <term>combustion: a state where a planet is so close to the Sun that its influence is weakened or "burnt up"</term>, and retrograde motion. If that is not possible, then let the **Moon** be connected to the **benefics** <term>benefics: the planets Jupiter and Venus, associated with good fortune</term>, and let those benefics look upon the "lord of the Ascendant" <note>original: sahib al-tali'; the planet that rules the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> through a trine or sextile. 

Beware of the **opposition** <note>180 degrees apart</note> and the **square** <note>90 degrees apart</note>, for the strongest gaze of the benefics is from the trine or sextile, while their weakest gaze is from the square and opposition. If the Moon connects to the lord of its house out of friendship and it is a malefic, it may still be suitable for needs and all that is being worked upon. If it is a benefic and looks upon the **Ascendant** <note>original: al-tali'</note> with favor, it is the best and most sought-after state. 

In all things, beware of the Moon's position with the **Tail** <note>original: al-dhanab; the South Node of the Moon, often viewed as a point of depletion or misfortune</note> and the gaze of the malefics through square, opposition, or conjunction. In all matters and works, also beware of the Moon when it indicates patience, toil, and the lengthening of work and hardship; this occurs during its waning, especially if its decrease is in the three types: light, travel, and calculation. Its best state is to be increasing in all of these. **Mars** should not look upon the Moon with any aspect, for the gaze of Mars toward the Moon while it is increasing is a great misfortune. Likewise, the gaze of **Saturn** toward the Moon when it is decreasing is unfavorable. The Moon is strongest at night when it is above the earth, and the Ascendant is strongest during the day when the Moon is below the earth.

Know that one of the best things is for the Moon and the Ascendant to be in signs of **straight ascension**. If they are so, they indicate speed in fulfilling a need and success, especially if they are in a **fixed sign** or **double-bodied signs** <term>double-bodied signs: also known as mutable signs—Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces</term>.

Know that **Aries** is the fastest of the "turning" <note>cardinal</note> signs to change, **Cancer** is the most changeable, **Capricorn** is the most striving, and **Libra** is the strongest and most balanced. 

Know that the **Angles** <note>original: al-awtad; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope</note> are the fastest in completing a work and finishing it. The **succedent** houses <note>original: ma yali al-awtad; houses following the angles</note> are slower, and the **cadent** houses <note>original: al-saqitah; the "falling" houses</note> are slow, weak, and failing. The fastest way for a work to be completed is for a benefic to be in the Ascendant or with the Moon in direct motion, or for the Moon itself to be direct.

Know that knowledge of the outcomes of actions is only known through the lord of the Moon's trine and the lord of the Ascendant, the assessment of their positions and states, and the gaze of the planets toward them. Thus, the judgment on the consequences of matters is based on what is revealed through that.

( **Dorotheus** <note>original: "Dhūruthiyūs"; referring to Dorotheus of Sidon, a 1st-century astrologer whose work was foundational to the Islamic astrological tradition</note> ), the leader of the **astrological art** <term>astrological art: literally "the art of judgment" (al-ṣināʿa al-iḥkāmiyya), which involves making predictions and choosing auspicious times</term>, said regarding the commencement of works: one should rectify the **Ascendant** <term>Ascendant: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment</term>, its ruler, the Moon, and the lord of the Moon's house. One must also beware of the Moon being in a poor state at the start, of which there are ten varieties.

Rectify the Moon as much as possible and do not allow it to be weakened in the Ascendant. Even if the ruler of the Ascendant or the Moon are **malefics** <term>malefics: "unlucky" planets, usually Mars or Saturn, thought to cause difficulty</term>, if they aspect the Moon from an **angle** <term>angle: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a chart, considered the most powerful positions</term>, you should place the Moon in one of the Ascendant's angles. Do not neglect the **Part of Fortune** <note>original: "sahm al-saʿāda"; a calculated point in a chart representing prosperity and well-being</note> in any commencement or inquiry involving the Moon’s aspects and conjunctions. Do not worry about the ruler of the Part, and do not mind if the Part itself is **cadent** <term>cadent: "falling away" from the angles; positions (like the 6th or 12th houses) considered weak or hidden</term> from the Ascendant, provided the Part aspects the Ascendant and the Moon. Strive to place the ruler of the Ascendant with the Part, for that is more conducive to the matter's success and increases its excellence. Never place the Moon in the third, sixth, eighth, or twelfth [house] from the Part, as that is disliked. In all commencements, always ensure the Ascendant and the Moon are in signs of **straight ascension** <note>original: "burūj mustaqīmat al-ṭulūʿ"; signs that take longer to rise over the horizon (in the northern hemisphere: Cancer through Sagittarius), often associated with stability</note>.

Know that the Ascendant, its signs, the **fourth house** <note>original: "al-rābiʿ"; representing the end of matters or the "root" of the situation</note> from it, and its ruler, all indicate the general state of the undertaking.

If you see that the Moon is corrupted <note>afflicted by bad aspects or position</note> but a matter is forced upon you that cannot be delayed, do not give the Moon any share in the Ascendant; remove it from the Ascendant's influence and place a **benefic** <term>benefic: "lucky" planets, usually Jupiter or Venus, thought to bring success</term> in the Ascendant. Strengthen the Ascendant and its ruler. This is the teaching of ( **Dorotheus** ).

-> { Let us return to the subject at hand and say } <-

The rectification of the Ascendant and its ruler is achieved through "benefic configurations." As for the configuration, the Ascendant should resemble the nature of the need in both quality and meaning. Regarding quality: for things whose completion requires speed, intense movement, authority, might, or even harm, we use the **Fire signs** <note>Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius</note>. Regarding meaning: for wars, we use the signs of **Mars** <note>Aries and Scorpio</note>. You must also rectify the "Place of the Need," the ruler of the need, and the ruler of the ruler of the need. The Place of the Need indicates something inherent in the matter, the ruler indicates its middle stage, and the ruler of the ruler indicates its outcome. Similarly, the Ascendant indicates the beginning of the seeker’s quest, the ruler of the Ascendant indicates the middle of their affair, and the ruler of the ruler of the Ascendant indicates the final outcome. In the same way, seek testimony from the **Part of the Need** <note>original: "sahm al-ḥāja"; a specific calculated point for a desired outcome</note> regarding the state of the matter, as well as its ruler and the ruler of its ruler. Rectify all of these as I have described, and support them with the **benefics** belonging to them by placement, aspect, and applying conjunction. Remove the malefics from these positions.

Beware of the **retrogradation** <term>retrogradation: when a planet appears to move backward in the sky, symbolizing delays or reversals</term> of the ruler of the Ascendant or the ruler of the need, for it signifies twisting, prevention, and prolongation. Even if all positions and conditions indicate that the matter will come to be, there will first occur despair and obstruction through intervening obstacles, and the matter will only be completed after great difficulty. Beware of the **Tail** <note>original: "al-dhanab"; the South Node of the Moon (Cauda Draconis), considered a point of depletion or "drainage"</note> being with either of the **two Luminaries** <note>The Sun and the Moon</note> when they are in conjunction or opposition. Even if they are not in conjunction or opposition, beware of it being in the Ascendant, the Place of the Need, or with the Part of the Need, for it corrupts the matter and overwhelms it with baseness and hardship. Strive to place the benefics in the Ascendant and the Places of the Need within the angles.

As for the **Great Benefic** <note>Jupiter</note>, it is powerful in every matter the seeker pursues and for strengthening purposes.

As for the **Lesser Benefic** <note>Venus</note>, it is powerful in matters of amusement, pleasures, women, adornment, affections, and the like.

Beware of the Moon’s presence in the Ascendant for any matter, as it is contrary to it; it is the "enemy" of the Ascendant. As for the **Sun**, it does not oppose the Ascendant, but it reveals matters, publicizes them, and disperses what has been gathered.

Beware of the malefics with the utmost caution in the Ascendant and the angles, especially if they are the rulers of **moist places**... for when the Sun

## 44

If the ruler of the **Eighth House** <term>Eighth House: traditionally associated with death, inheritance, and shared resources</term> indicates corruption, it manifests through death, restriction, and major prisons. If it is the ruler of the **Sixth House** <term>Sixth House: associated with illness, servants, and small livestock</term>, the corruption comes from enemies, slaves, diseases, thefts, small prisons, and four-legged animals. If it is the ruler of the **Twelfth House** <term>Twelfth House: associated with hidden enemies, sorrow, and confinement</term>, it indicates that corruption stems from slanderers, despair, enemies, and medium-sized prisons. If it is the ruler of the **Second House** <term>Second House: the house of personal wealth and possessions</term>, it indicates harm caused by money, assistants, eating, or drinking. Everything I mention to you is of great benefit in the **Grand Science of Talismans** <note>original: "al-jalīl min ʿilm al-ṭalasmāt"</note>. Be extremely cautious and strive to ensure that the **Ascendant** <term>Ascendant: the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> is during the day in diurnal signs, or at night in nocturnal signs, and that it is "straight" <note>original: "mustaqīman"; likely referring to signs of long ascension which rise more "directly" or "straight" in the sky</note> rather than crooked in its rising. Do the same for the **Luminaries** <term>Luminaries: the Sun and the Moon</term> if you are able, and ensure these rulers are strong so that they may gather the strength we have mentioned. Observe the state of the planets and what meanings they signify; then you shall achieve your desire in this matter.

Whenever you desire affection, friendship, or social interaction, place the Moon in a state of **reception** <term>reception: a condition where a planet is in a sign or dignity of another planet, which "receives" it like a guest</term> from Venus via a **Trine** <term>Trine: an auspicious aspect where planets are 120 degrees apart</term>. The Trine is a gathering of an equilateral triangle, where each of its sides is one hundred and twenty (120) degrees of the celestial sphere. The **Sextile** <term>Sextile: a harmonious aspect of 60 degrees</term> is a gathering of an equilateral hexagon, where each of its sides is sixty (60) degrees of the celestial sphere. The **Square** <term>Square: a challenging aspect of 90 degrees</term> is a gathering of an equilateral square, where each of its sides is ninety (90) degrees of the celestial sphere. The counterpart to the **Opposition** <term>Opposition: planets 180 degrees apart, usually considered difficult</term> is the Square...

Let us return to our purpose and say: if circumstances occur where it is not possible for Venus to be the receiver, then let it be in a Trine with the Moon, and let the Moon be received by Jupiter or by the ruler of its own house—be it by Trine, Sextile, or **Conjunction** <term>Conjunction: when two planets are in the same degree of the zodiac</term>. If that is difficult and cannot be arranged, let the Moon be in one of the bounds of Venus, made auspicious by Jupiter and safe from the **Malefics** <note>original: "al-nuḥūs"; the "unlucky" planets, Saturn and Mars</note>. If the affection is for friendship and companionship only, let the Ascendant be in the bounds of Venus.

If the friendship is for benefit regarding real estate, it should be in the **Fourth House**. If it is for a benefit regarding religion, it should be in the **Ninth House**. If the matters hoped for are in the **Eleventh House**, then make the **Part of Fortune** <note>original: "sahm al-saʿāda"</note> auspicious in a strong and received position, as well as its ruler. If the benefit concerns matters of war and its affairs, make Mars receive the ruler of the Ascendant or the Moon, helping them through an aspect of friendship. If it concerns an act of fraud or deception, place the reception with Saturn instead of Mars. If it concerns accounting, writing, or scholars, place the reception with Mercury. If it concerns kings, place the reception with the Sun. If it concerns judges, religious people, and nobles, place the reception with Jupiter. Likewise, if the seeker desires only one specific type of thing, place the reception with its corresponding ruler—I mean the reception of the Moon, the Ascendant, the Part of Fortune, and the **Part of Wealth** <note>original: "sahm al-māl"</note>. Let the Moon be accompanied, as we mentioned, by the ruler of the Ascendant and the ruler of the Fourth House, pure from the Malefics and strong, for they are the indicators of the final outcome.

Whenever you wish to seek a favor, make the ruler of the Ascendant and the Moon received by the ruler of the person from whom the favor is sought, or the planet that has authority over the question. Place the Moon or the ruler of the Ascendant in the "place of the need" <note>i.e., the astrological house associated with that specific request</note>.
*   If the need is from elders or those who cultivate the land, let the ruler of the need be **Saturn**.
*   If it is from judges, the religious, the generous, noble people, or ministers, let the ruler of the need be **Jupiter**.
*   If it is from army commanders, soldiers, or those who work with fire and iron, let the ruler of the need be **Mars**.
*   If it is from the Great King or the Sultan, let its ruler be the **Sun**.
*   If it is from women, entertainers, beauticians, or makers of beautiful things like perfume, dyes, embroidery, brocade, and the like, let the ruler of the need be **Venus**.
*   If the need is from accountants, scribes, people of wisdom and ingenuity, merchants, or craftsmen of delicacy and precision, let the ruler of the need be **Mercury**.
*   If the need is from couriers, messengers, servants of the king, transporters, or spies, let the ruler of the need be the **Moon**.

Make the ruler of the Ascendant and the Moon auspicious in every need, and place them in **mutable signs** <note>original: "munqalibayn"; literally "turning" signs, which indicate change and movement</note>. If the need is for money, rectify the Part of Fortune by ensuring its prosperity.

...and its temperament, and place it in aspect to the Moon. Likewise, treat the **ruler of the Fourth House** <term>Fourth House: associated with the home, roots, and the final outcome of matters</term> with beneficence and favorable advancement. Be most cautious of a strong **malefic** <term>malefic: a planet that brings difficulty or obstruction, traditionally Mars or Saturn</term> cutting between the ruler of the Ascendant and the Moon, or between the petitioner. Be careful lest the petitioner be restricted, or the Moon and the ruler of the Ascendant be afflicted, having no connection to the matter at hand. Let the "reception" I have described be from a **trine** <term>trine: an aspect of 120 degrees, considered very harmonious</term> or a **sextile** <term>sextile: an aspect of 60 degrees, considered favorable</term> among benefics and malefics, or a conjunction with benefics. Similarly, beware of the falling of the petitioner, the **Luminaries** <term>Luminaries: the Sun and the Moon</term>, and the ruler of the Ascendant from the **angles** <term>angles: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which are the most powerful points in a chart</term>. 

If these conditions are difficult to meet, then look to the "Choice" based on the **five Hylegs** <term>Hylegs: the five points in a chart that determine the vitality and lifespan of a person or project</term> and connect it to the meaning of the specific request. Ensure these two are in **mutable signs** <note>original: "munqalibayn," literally turning or changing; in this context, it refers to signs that allow for the transition or completion of a task</note> and are auspiciously placed. Beware also of the affliction of the Moon's ruler, the ruler of the Fourth House, and the Ascendant, for in that lies the affliction of the request's final outcome and its corruption. Whenever you wish to attempt any of the established works, or their healing, or whatever returns to the hand of the artisan in a refined way, let that be while the Moon is in a **double-bodied sign** <term>double-bodied sign: Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces; signs that facilitate adaptation and change</term>, pure of malefic influences. Let the Ascendant be similarly rectified. If the attempt involves gold, then strengthen the **Sun** and its ruler at the beginning of your work, and do the same for all its other states.

(We have mentioned to you beautiful matters that will accompany you in many of your works, so be stingy with them <note>The author advises the reader to keep this knowledge secret or to treat it as a precious, scarce resource.</note> and apply analogy to the specific details of these matters. Some of these concern the timing of observations, which is the act of "giving," and among them is the time for installing the **talisman** <term>talisman: an object inscribed with astrological symbols intended to draw down celestial power</term> in its place, and similar conditions of Time. As for **Place**, it is the ultimate goal of this science regarding "Quantity"—that is, the place of observation and making it easy for the practitioner to perceive it at the desired time. This also includes the location of the talisman, the position of its installation—whether in the air or the earth—its revelation or its concealment, and such things. Furthermore, it includes the place where the talisman is crafted and the place where its material is extracted; for this is needed in the works of talismans for actions whose gifts and effects seem like lies when heard.

By my life, O seeker, these acts are truly within the realm of miracles. Among them is the action of the talisman and its effects on non-living things, such as the art of moving sands, moving heavy stones, repelling plagues, warding off rain and clouds, dispersing winds, and other such things that provide for one's needs. This is not the place to detail them, for knowledge of this rank should not be heard at all <note>The author suggests that such powerful knowledge is too dangerous or sacred for general consumption.</note>. As for speech and number—the "Discrete Quantities"—both are needed in eloquence, transactions, **divination** <term>divination: the practice of seeking knowledge of the future or the unknown by supernatural means</term>, **physiognomy** <term>physiognomy: the art of judging character or predicting the future from physical features</term>, and similar sciences.

As for the **Specific Number**, it enters into these works because it is specific to **Time**, for Time is the number of the motion of the celestial sphere. When we say "at such a time," we are only gesturing toward a certain number which, when it reaches your ear, is concluded. If we say that rest lasts from a specific hour for a duration of 42 days, we have only recounted a number.

Know, O seeker, that **Proportion** <note>original: "nisba"; can also mean relationship or ratio</note> is involved in this branch of science because it is the form of the talisman. The place where the work is done contains the receptive traces, to the point that they are said to be active within the talisman in the place where it is set. That state also falls under the category of Proportion and similar conditions. As for **Quality**, it is the true cause of the talisman. This is because the location for the talisman’s action must have full power in its resemblance to the "quality" it carries, so that the action may spread from it. This is the **binding of the higher natures to the lower natures** <note>This is the core principle of Hermetic magic: "As above, so below."</note>. That is, the place must resemble the "gift" of the planets and the reception of the influence within it so that the desired goal may be achieved. For we have said many times that the planets are specific in their actions to some things and not others—just as planets are specific to certain countries, types of animals, plants, stones, and so on. Thus, if the stone used, or any other material, is receptive to the action...
