"And of the jinn were those who worked before him by the permission of his Lord... [until] the Grateful." <note>A reference to Quran 34:12-13, describing spirits who served King Solomon.</note>

### (The Fourth Procedure)

Regarding the treatment of a woman struck by spiritual afflictions <term>Awarid: Literally "accidents" or "occurrences," referring here to possessing entities or the symptoms they cause.</term> in her chest, such that her stomach swells and she is prevented from eating. This is a severe condition that may cause some of her limbs to fail. The treatment is the same as you used for similar cases, except that you add this verse to the oils: "And [remember, O Muhammad], when We directed to you a group of the jinn... [until] manifest." <note>Quran 46:29-32, describing Jinn listening to the Quran.</note> The incense is increased with mastic <note>original: "alak al-batn"</note> and *tamur ghant* <note>An obscure medicinal herb or resin, possibly a local variety of tamarisk or a specific aromatic root.</note>. Continue the recitation of the conjuration.

### (The Fifth Procedure)

If you induce a trance <note>original: "tasra‘" — to cause a state of spiritual possession or seizure for the purpose of interrogation.</note> in the afflicted woman but the possessing spirit does not answer you, then change the times of the treatment to: before sunrise, at midday, at sunset, at the fading of the twilight, and at dawn. You must eventually gain mastery over it at one of these times. This spirit is a "Flyer" <term>Jinni Tayyar: A class of Jinn believed to fly through the air and inhabit the clouds, often considered more difficult to capture or exorcise.</term> that flies with the clouds. Burn for it an incense of asafoetida <note>original: "haltit" — a pungent resin used to repel malevolent spirits.</note>, garlic, "Barqi" tar <note>A high-quality tar, likely from the Barqa region.</note>, and radish. Do as you did with the others, but add Surah Al-Tariq <note>Quran Chapter 86, "The Nightcomer."</note> to the incantation.

### (The Sixth Procedure)

Regarding the treatment of one who strikes a woman on her head or her private parts so that she cannot bear her husband: This is an *Ifrit* <note>A powerful, often rebellious type of spirit.</note> of the waters. Perform your ritual for her as previously described, but add to the incantation the entirety of Surah Al-Muzzammil <note>Quran Chapter 73, "The Enwrapped One."</note>. Use basil in the oil, and for incense, use the germander seed <term>Shandaqurah: *Teucrium*, a bitter herb used in folk medicine and magic to treat ailments of the head and reproductive system.</term>.

### (The Seventh Procedure)

Regarding the treatment of one who "touches" <note>Refers to spiritual "touching" or possession.</note> a woman and nearly chokes her. The signs are that she strikes out with her hands and legs, and perhaps she strips off her clothes and throws them away. The treatment follows the previous description, except that you must grasp the forelock <note>The hair at the front of the head.</note> of the afflicted woman during the recitation, and the spirit will depart. Add to the Dahrushiyah oath the words of the Almighty: "O you who have believed, remember Allah with much remembrance... [until] the Light." <note>Quran 33:41-43.</note> The incense used is coriander.

### (The Eighth Procedure)

Regarding the treatment of the affliction that lingers in the human body for more than six hours, where at times she eats no food except water, and at other times she does not drink at all. When it enters her, it stays for more than six hours, and perhaps it remains for twelve hours. Its treatment follows the previous description, except she must bathe with a mixture of basil water, rose, and spikenard. Begin the recitation of the conjuration after following it with Surah Al-Mulk <note>Quran Chapter 67, "The Sovereignty."</note> until its condition becomes clear to you.

### (The Ninth Procedure)

Regarding the treatment of the water-dwellers, who are the "Children of the Red" <note>original: "Awlad al-Ahmar" — likely subordinates of Al-Ahmar, the Jinn King associated with Tuesday and the planet Mars.</note>. They strike the woman and remain in her for more than six <unclear>periods</unclear>, entering her body and altering it. Sometimes she spits upon her own form <note>Likely referring to her reflection or her physical appearance.</note> until it is thought that nothing is wrong with her. Treat this with the conjuration, the magical seals, and the "Names of the Moon" <note>A series of seven or twenty-eight names used in lunar magic.</note> seventy times in the oils.

### (The Tenth Procedure)
In treating the children of al-Qamaqim <note>original: "Bani al-Qamaqim," a specific tribe of spirits often associated with vessels or depths</note>. They manifest to certain women to frighten them and keep them away from their husbands. Their treatment is as previously described, except that the Opening Chapter <note>The Fatiha, the first chapter of the Quran</note> and the concluding verses of the Chapter of the Cow <note>Surah al-Baqarah, verses 285-286, which are traditionally used for protection</note> shall serve as the protective amulet <term>hijab: A written charm or spiritual barrier worn on the body</term>. You shall hang it upon her. Then the practitioner will gain mastery over that demon <term>ifrit: A powerful and often rebellious class of spirit</term> and expel it from her, whether it be willing or unwilling.

### (The Eleventh Procedure)
In treating the children of al-Abyad <note>original: "Awlad al-Abyad," likely referring to the offspring of the "White King" of the Jinn</note>. They strike a man, causing his mind to become confused. Their treatment involves writing on a vessel for drinking <term>al-saqi: The practice of drinking water in which sacred text or symbols have been dissolved</term> and anointing with oils prepared with the Chapter of the Jinn <note>Surah al-Jinn, Chapter 72</note> and the Invocation <note>original: "al-da'wah," the specific ritual conjuration used throughout this manuscript</note>. The patient must not eat any food containing a soul <note>meat</note> nor anything produced from one <note>dairy or eggs</note> for a period of 40 days; then he shall be cured.

### (The Twelfth Procedure)
In treating the children of Maymun <note>original: "Awlad Maymun." In occult tradition, Maymun is the spirit king of Saturday and the planet Saturn</note>. They strike young children on their heads. Their treatment consists of drinking the Invocation from a red copper vessel and hanging the Chapter of Sovereignty <note>Surah al-Mulk, Chapter 67</note> upon the child.

### (The Thirteenth Procedure)
In treating the children of al-Nu'man <note>original: "Bani al-Nu'man"</note>. They strike a virgin girl, causing her mind to become confused at times; she starts in her sleep and desires to sit with men. Dismiss her resident spirits <term>ammar: Referring to the 'Ammar al-Makan,' the spiritual inhabitants of a home who must be cleared before a ritual</term> and add the Chapter of the Beneficent <note>Surah al-Rahman, Chapter 55</note> to the Invocation. Write the chapter for her to wear, and have her drink what was written in the vessel. Strike her on her stomach with a rod of sour pomegranate wood upon which the names of the Moon are written; she shall be cured by the permission of God Almighty.

### (The Fourteenth Procedure)
In treating the inhabitants of refuse heaps <note>original: "Sukkan al-Mazabil." In folk belief, unclean places like garbage dumps are common haunts for lower spirits</note>. They strike a woman at the time of childbirth; some of them cause her blood to flow excessively and it does not stop. Her treatment involves writing on a Tuesday or a Saturday during the Hour of Mars <note>In astrological magic, Mars governs Tuesday and is associated with blood and iron</note>; then she shall be cured. The treatment with the Invocation is as previously described for those afflicted by epilepsy <term>sar'a: Here used as a general term for spiritual possession or fits</term>.

### (The Fifteenth Procedure)

They strike a woman at childbirth so that she remains ill, and her stomach may swell. Its treatment is on Sunday during the Hour of the Sun, just as you treated those with epilepsy. Likewise, the Qaythan spirits strike a woman during her cycle <note>original: "al-hilla," likely referring to menstruation or the period following childbirth</note>. Their treatment is with the exorcisms for epilepsy during the Hour of Jupiter on Thursday—according to the aforementioned conditions—or on Saturday.

### (The Sixteenth Procedure)
In treating the children of Dahman <note>original: "Bani Dahman"</note>. They strike a virgin on her head, and she leaps toward the latrine and wants to throw off her clothes. Her treatment is on Monday at dawn and Wednesday night after the sun has set. Persist in her treatment with the Invocation and oils; she shall be cured by the permission of God Almighty.

### (The Seventeenth Procedure)
In treating those who strike a man during his ritual washing <note>original: "al-ightisal"</note>; they enter his body between the skin and the flesh like ants, and into the joints. It is most active at night; it moves up like ants, the stomach swells, and the pain in the joints, back, and heart intensifies, and his health fails. Its treatment is as previously mentioned for epilepsy, with a surplus of incense and oils; he shall be cured by the permission of God Almighty. ### (The Eighteenth Procedure) 
Regarding the treatment of one who strikes a woman on her head and body and remains within her for more than a year. ### (The Twentieth Procedure) 
In treating stubborn spirits <note>original: "dahan," likely a specific class of recalcitrant jinn</note> that you cannot control except through repeated ritual work. Use incense of donkey dung and recite the Dahroushiya Oath repeatedly, and the patient will be healed by the will of God Almighty.

### (The Twenty-Second Procedure) 
In treating a type of jinn which, if it strikes someone, they do not recover until their death—may God protect us from it.

### (The Twenty-Third Procedure)
Regarding the treatment of **House Dwellers**. These spirits strike a person's vision so that they can see nothing at night. The patient should eat one piece each night. Write the Dahroushiya oath and hang it upon them; they will be healed by the will of God Almighty.

### (The Twenty-Four Procedure)
Regarding the treatment of **Swarms** <note>original: 'Arhat,' referring to groups or tribes of spirits</note> who strike a woman upon her navel, causing a chronic illness, swelling, and hemorrhaging during the summer. ### (The Twenty-Fifth Procedure)

They cause a person frequent ill health and sores. Let it be known that this happens because the person went out at night and stepped on the children of that tribe, breaking some of their limbs. The remedy for this is to use a ritual cleansing <term>nashra: A healing ritual involving washing or sprinkling with water over which prayers have been recited</term> of this kind. Then, read the Dahroushiya and say: "Take your due from us, O Bani Qay'an." Place the remedy in a piece of broken pottery and throw it toward a tree in the direction of the *Qibla* <note>The direction of prayer toward Mecca</note> or at the head of a water spring. Then write the Dahroushiya for the afflicted person to wear; they will be healed by the will of God Almighty.

**Regarding the treatment of boils and sores:** If they are numerous in children, know that they went out at night and were followed by the daughters of the Jinn. The medicine is to write the Dahroushiya and hang it upon the child; they will be healed by the will of God Almighty.

### (The Twenty-Sixth Procedure)
Regarding the treatment of a tribe of Jinn who strike children upon their stomachs, causing their condition to deteriorate. Write the Dahroushiya for them and name the tribe at the end: "**The Erring Shahashama**." **Regarding the treatment of a type of Jinn** who strike women when they are with their husbands: The remedy is to begin the conjuration and add **Surah al-Buruj** <note>Chapter 85 of the Quran, 'The Constellations'</note> to the protective amulet; she will be healed by the will of God Almighty.

**If the afflicted person complains of the pain of fever, stomach ache, or headache,** and feels something moving upwards through their body: The treatment is the Dahroushiya oath. The **Seven Seals** <note>A series of seven esoteric symbols often attributed to Solomon, used as a powerful talisman in Islamic magic</note> should be written on a piece of blue linen along with the name of the **Angel in charge of the day**, and hung around the neck; the person will be healed by the will of God Almighty and His power.

### { Section }

Regarding the treatment of one whose limbs have been rendered useless, such as the legs or one of the hands. ### { Section }

Regarding the treatment for those complaining of joint pain, lack of appetite, excessive thirst, and general bodily weakness, to the point that one might think it is "the companion's disease" <note>likely referring to a chronic or wasting condition like consumption/tuberculosis</note>.

Once ground, weigh the mixture and add an equal weight of clarified bee honey (skimmed of its foam and prepared without smoke). Instruct the patient to eat a portion of this every day before sunrise. Begin on the tenth day of the lunar month and continue for forty days; they shall be healed by the will of God Almighty.

## { This is the Dahroushiya Invocation }

In the name of God: **I am that I am** <note>original: "أهيا شراهيا" (Ahya Sharahiya), the Arabic transliteration of the Hebrew 'Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh'</note>. O Dahmush, High and Exalted in His height! Where are the powerful hosts? Where are the Shamhamariya? <note>A class of powerful spiritual servants</note> Where is Kardin? Where is Dardam? Where is 'Asab? Where is the Lord of the Mountain of Smoke? Where is the one who rides the elephant and is turbaned with a serpent? Answer and appear, by the right of the Hebrew Oaths and by *Barhamuta* and *Shaybamutha* <note>Magical names intended to compel spirits</note>. Answer, in obedience to God!

<meta>This page continues the instructional manual for occult practices and spiritual sciences. ->{ **Know** }<-

Know that this invocation <term>Dawa: Literally "a call" or "prayer," here referring to a specific ritual formula used to summon spiritual forces</term> possesses the power to perform wonders. Its definition and the preparation of the student for all such invocations and oaths were previously mentioned at the beginning.

(**If you wish to be hidden from the eyes of both jinn and humans**) then draw a circle upon the ground and stand in its center while looking toward the sky. This must be done at the midpoint of the hollow <note>likely referring to the celestial "hollow" or a specific astrological alignment</note> when the moon has settled within it, and the hour corresponds to the letter *Khā* <note>In the Abjad system of letter-numerology, the letter Khā (خ) carries the value of 600; here it likely refers to a specific planetary hour or symbolic timing</note>. Recite the invocation according to its numerical value for each direction.

For example: While facing the *Qibla* <note>the direction of Mecca</note>, you say it five times. Then recite that same number toward the East (the place of rising), then toward the South while facing it, and five times toward the West. The incense must be rising during the time of the work, and it is *al-qistal* <term>Qistal: Likely costus root, a common aromatic used in historical fumigation and medicine</term>. If you do this, proceed in the name of the "Helper" (*al-ʿAwn*), who is the chief over the spiritual assistants assigned to the southern region; their names will be mentioned later.

**{ Section on Sinking Waters }**
Recite the *Dahroushiyyah* <term>Dahroushiyyah: A famous occult invocation frequently found in medieval Arabic grimoires like the 'Sun of Knowledge' (Shams al-Ma'arif)</term> and at its end, recite this verse: *"Indeed in that is a reminder for whoever has a heart or who listens while he is present as a witness"* [Surat Qaf 50:37] until the end of the chapter. If the water is flowing down from the West, call upon the name of the Helper and travel wherever you wish. Your shadow will not be seen, nor will your footsteps be heard, as long as the moon remains in that station. This matter is among the curiosities of the science.

(**If you desire a more subtle method**) of invisibility: Take a number of frogs equal to the value of the letter *Ṭā* <note>The letter Ṭā (ط) has a numerical value of 9; thus, nine frogs are required</note> during the month of the equinox at the time of the sun's meridian. Sacrifice them with a knife that has never been used before, while reciting the entirety of the Chapter of Power (*Surat al-Qadr*). Then, tan their skins using Isfahani kohl <note>a fine powdered mineral from Isfahan used for eyes, here used as a drying agent</note> and salt. Once the skins are tanned, fashion them into a *qalansuwa* <term>Qalansuwa: A conical or close-fitting cap worn historically in the Islamic world</term> to fit your head. Sew it with black silk thread and write upon each skin the complete "Triangle of Al-Ghazali" <note>The famous 3x3 magic square where every row, column, and diagonal adds up to 15</note>. Around the outside of the shape, write one of these nine verses—writing one on each of the skins:

> **First:** From *Al-An'am*: "And among them are those who listen to you..." [6:25] to the word "ancients."

> **Second:** "Those are the ones whose hearts God has sealed..." [16:108] to the word "heedless."

> **Third:** "And who is more unjust than one who is reminded of the verses of his Lord but turns away from them..." [18:57] to the word "heaviness."

> **Fourth:** "And when you recite the Quran, We put between you and those [who do not believe]..." [17:45] to the word "heaviness."

> **Fifth:** "Did you then think that We created you in vain..." [23:115] to the phrase "he has no proof thereof." [23:117]

> **Sixth:** "And We have set a barrier before them and a barrier behind them..." [36:9] (the full verse).

> **Seventh:** "O assembly of jinn and humans, if you are able..." [55:33] to the word "pass."

> **Eighth:** "Fear not, for I am with you both; I hear and I see." [20:46]

> **الثامنة — والله من ورائهم محيط .**
> **Ninth:** "And God encompasses them from behind." [85:20]

Then recite the *Dahroushiyyah* while standing in the sun and persist in the incantation until your shadow disappears. (This matter also utilizes the *Berhatiah* <term>Berhatiah: An ancient 'oath' or series of names believed to be in Syriac or Hebrew, used to command spiritual entities</term> incantation until its end). Other scholars have substituted these nine verses with different ones; every scholar has a path they have followed, and God Almighty knows best.

The spiritual servant will then inform you in your ear of everything you have set your mind upon regarding the names of the hours.

## (Conclusion of the Dahroushiyyah)

Know, O seeker, that the service of the <term>Jinn: Supernatural spirits in Islamic culture who can be invoked for assistance.</term> has conditions: Wearing what is permissible (*halal*) and eating what is permissible. The cleanliness of the place. The incense. That you do not speak with anyone during the days of the service. That you do not sleep except when overcome [by exhaustion]. The renewal of <term>ablution: Ritual washing ("wudu") performed before prayer.</term> whenever it is nullified. Ritual bathing (*ghusl*). Frequent prayers upon the Prophet (peace be upon him) and facing the <term>Qibla: The direction of the Kaaba in Mecca.</term>. Not entering one conjuration upon another. Sitting as one sits for prayer. An intention that matches the work. And that the work be done with certainty, for God is the Guide.

## (The Famous Discourse on the Uses of the Names of the Moon)

(**And it is if you wish**) for the desired person to fall in love with the seeker. The work must be in the hour of Venus and the ascendant must be the sign of Taurus, on a Wednesday. At the head of every hundred recitations, you shall say:

->O [Name], answer [Name] with the love of God, a love like the love of Zulaykha for Joseph, peace be upon him; and I have cast upon you a love from Me and a longing.<-

## A Useful Treatise

Regarding the knowledge of the birth-ascendant <term>tali‘: The sign of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</term> for a person whose birth time is unknown. If you desire this, then determine the ascendant as accurately as possible. Identify which degree of its sign the ruler of the ascendant's degree occupies. Multiply the degrees of the ascendant by the degrees of the ruler of the "limit" <term>hadd: An Egyptian subdivision of a zodiac sign, where different planets rule specific sets of degrees.</term> and divide the result by the distance the Moon has traveled in its sign. The result is called the "Indicator" <term>al-dalil: A mathematical factor used to point toward a specific astrological outcome.</term>. Then, determine how many degrees there are from the beginning of Aries to the degree of the Sun, and divide that by the Indicator you have set aside. Then cast it out <gloss>twelve by twelve</gloss> <note>The process of modular arithmetic, dividing by 12 and taking the remainder.</note>. Whatever remains, identify it: if it is from 1 to 3, they were born in the spring; up to 6, in the summer; up to 9, in the autumn; and up to 12, in the winter.

(If you wish) to know how many degrees have passed in the known season, multiply the minutes the Sun moves in a day by sixty and divide it by the Indicator. This yields the minutes; convert them to degrees. The result is the number of days in the month and the number of degrees traveled in that sign.

(If you wish) to know whether they were born by day or by night, look at the hour the question is asked. If you find the Moon below the earth <note>The houses of the chart that are below the horizon (1st through 6th houses).</note>, then they were born at night, and vice versa.

(If you wish) to know in which part of the night or day: if you see the Moon from Aries to Cancer, it is in the first quarter; to Libra, the second; to Capricorn, the third; and to Pisces, the fourth.

(And if you wish) to know in which hour they were born: if the Moon is in the first image, it is the first hour; in the second image, the second; in the third image, the third. If the Moon is in a masculine sign <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, Libra, Sagittarius, or Aquarius.</note>, then they were born in an odd-numbered hour; otherwise, in an even-numbered hour, even if many years have passed.

(And if you wish) to know how much of their life has passed: look to Jupiter <note>original: "al-mushtari"</note> and whoever aspects it—whether by opposition, sextile, square, or trine. Jupiter represents 12 years, and every planet aspecting it adds 12 years if it is in an "angle" <term>watad: The four most powerful houses in a chart (1st, 4th, 7th, 10th).</term>. If it is in a "succeedent" house <note>original: "ma yali watadan," houses 2, 5, 8, 11.</note>, it adds that number in months. If it is in a "cadent" house <term>sawaqit: The 'falling' houses (3, 6, 9, 12), considered the weakest.</term>, it adds that number in days. If Jupiter itself is in a succeedent house, treat its number as months, and if cadent, as days.

(If you wish to know how much of their life remains): look to the Indicator, which is the "Almuten" <term>al-mubtazz: The planet with the most dignity over a specific point in the chart.</term> of the ascendant. See the distance between it and the square or opposition of a malefic <note>The "unlucky" planets, typically Mars or Saturn.</note>. If it is in the quarter of the ascendant or the seventh house using "oblique ascensions" <term>al-matali' al-ufuqiyya: A method of measuring the movement of signs relative to the local horizon.</term>, or if it is in the quarter of the fourth or tenth house using "equatorial ascensions" <term>al-matali' al-istiwa'iyya: Measurement along the celestial equator.</term>. If the Almuten is in a "fixed" sign <note>Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, Aquarius.</note>, it indicates years; if in a "bicorporeal" sign <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces.</note>, months; and if in a "cardinal" sign <note>original: "munqalib," Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn.</note>, days. God knows best.

(Another method) Set the ascendant and the planet, then multiply the degree of the ascendant that has risen by the degree of the ruler of its limit. Divide the total by the Moon’s progress in its sign until the hour of the question. The result is the Indicator. Then take from the beginning of Aries to the minute of the Sun which is...

...within it, at the time of the inquiry, using the degrees of the local rising signs <term>matali‘ al-balad: The degrees of the zodiac as they ascend at a specific geographical latitude.</term>. Whatever the result, divide it by the Indicator you set aside. Then cast it out <gloss>twelve by twelve</gloss>. Whatever remains under 12, keep it. If it is 3 or less, then the birth occurred in the spring; if up to 6, then in the summer; if up to 9, then in the autumn; and if up to 12, then in the winter. Then look at the remaining number under 12 and count it out from the beginning of Aries, assigning one sign to each number. Wherever the count ends, that is the zodiac sign the Sun was in at the time of birth.

If you wish to find the exact position of the Sun, multiply the number of minutes of the Sun's position by 60 <note>This likely refers to the "day of the question" or a specific temporal unit used in horary astrology.</note>, then divide by the Indicator you set aside. Whatever result comes out—such as $3\frac{2}{3}$—and whatever remains below that, is the number of degrees the Sun occupied within that sign. Then look to the Moon: if it is above or below the horizon of the inquiry, the birth was at night; otherwise, it was by day. If the Moon is between Aries and Cancer, it was in the first quarter of the night or day, and so on for the four quarters of the celestial sphere.

### (Knowledge of which hour within the quarters)

If the Moon is in the Ascendant <term>al-tali‘: The first house of a birth chart.</term>, it is the first hour; if in the second house, the second hour, and so on.

To know how much of their life has passed: look at Jupiter. If it is in a "pivot" <term>watad: An angular house (1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th), considered the most powerful positions in a chart.</term>, it signifies 12 years. If it is in a "succedent" house <note>original: "ma yali watad," the houses following the pivots (2nd, 5th, 8th, or 11th).</note>, it signifies 12 months. If it is in a "cadent" house <note>original: "za'il," the houses falling away from the pivots (3rd, 6th, 9th, or 12th).</note>, it signifies days.

If any planet aspects Jupiter—regardless of the type of aspect—and it is in a pivot, add 12 years to the median number of his years, and the total will be in years. If it is in a succedent house, the total is in months. If the aspecting planet is in a cadent house, it is in days. Subtract the total sum backward from the hour of the inquiry, and where it ends marks the years, months, and days that have passed of the person's life; and God knows best.

### (The Summoning of Maymun)

If you wish to perform this work, engrave this image <note>Referring to a talismanic figure usually drawn in the manuscript, though not described in the OCR.</note> upon a shard of pottery with a pen made of red copper. The engraving must be done in the first hour of his day <note>Saturday, as Maymun is the planetary king of Saturday/Saturn.</note>. Then place the shard in the fire. The incense should be castor oil seeds, male frankincense, blue bdellium, and sandarac. Recite the conjuration over it 21 times. This conjuration is proven and effective, so understand this.

### (And this is the conjuration)

> I conjure you, O Maymun, O Aba Nukh, by the right of *Tafyish*, *Shalamin*, *Bizahdah*, *Bifaqdah*.
> *Bahin*, *Shafyahin*, by the right of the Pen and what it has written by the command of God to the best of God's creation, Muhammad son of Abdullah, that you answer, hasten, and listen. By the right of the light of God’s noble face, I swear unto you, O Maymun, O Aba Nukh, by the twilight, and by the night and what it enfolds, and by the moon when it grows full, that you shall surely ride from stage to stage <note>A recitation of Quranic verses from Surah al-Inshiqaq (84:16-19).</note>. What is the matter with them that they do not believe? Answer, O Maymun, and agitate such-and-such person toward such-and-such person. ### Supplication of Lights

In the name of God, the Gracious, the Merciful. In the name of God, the Great in His Kingdom. In the name of God, the Eternal in His Sovereignty. In the name of God, the Glorious above His Throne. In the name of God, the Subduer in His judgment. In the name of God, besides whom there is no deity, the Living, the Self-Sustaining; neither drowsiness nor sleep overtakes Him. He who raises the heaven so it does not fall upon the earth except by His permission. In the name of God, the Cleaver of the dawn, who made the night for rest and the sun and moon for calculation; that is the determination of the Almighty, the All-Knowing. In the name of God, before whose greatness kings have submitted, and before whose power they were humbled, and every king became a servant to His majesty. He knows what is on land and in the sea; not a leaf falls but He knows it, nor a grain in the darkness of the earth, nor anything moist or dry but that it is in a Clear Book. Shamkhina <note>Another magical name for the Divine, emphasizing height or loftiness.</note>, unique in glory and pride. His knowledge encompasses the end and the beginning; He encompasses all things in knowledge and keeps count of all things by number. He is God, besides whom there is no deity, the Creator, the Producer, the Fashioner, My God, the Wise. He created the celestial spheres, directing them as He wills, and placed within them shooting stars to pelt the Jinn and devils. He created the earth and made it firm. He is the Powerful One who sees the creeping of the black ant in the dark night upon the solid rock. There is no deity but He; everything is perishing except His Face. To Him belongs the judgment, and to Him you shall return—the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing, who knows every soul...

# ( Personal Scrying Mirror )
<note>The term "mandal" refers to a form of divination where a practitioner looks into a surface—often water, ink, or a mirror—to see visions of spirits or the unseen.</note>

If you desire this, write the invocation in a vessel using separated letters <note>Writing words as individual letters is a common talismanic technique to "unlock" their power</note> without blurring the ink. Pour pure water into it and cover the vessel with a white cloth. Prior to this, you must have completed a spiritual discipline and fast for a period of seven days, during which you recite the invocation after every prayer 21 times. After the period of discipline, place the vessel before you containing the water and recite the invocation over it 21 times. The water will then begin to effervesce <note>The water should be clean well-water that has not been "seen" by the sun—meaning it was drawn and kept in darkness</note>. Then, uncover it and you shall see what you desire. Write the "verses of unveiling" and place them upon your forehead.

## ( And this is the Invocation )

Sarmis (2) Baris (2) Yanlas (2) As (2) Kamnarush (2) Arsas (2) Karous (2) Masous (2) Baras (2) Qasas (2) Hamsayas (2) Yuqas (2) Salas (2) Jahas (2) As (2) Karakous (2) Minyas (2) Sar (2) Sahis (2) Sahsariyush (2). O Mighty, High, and Exalted One! **God, He is He, there is no god but He.** <note>The number (2) indicates that the preceding name should be repeated twice</note>. Descend, O Jahlaya’il! Descend, O Hantal! Descend, O Haymataya’il! Descend, O Qalaythaya’il! Descend, O Tahtaghaylaya’il! Descend, O Metatron! <note>Metatron is often identified in Western and Middle Eastern occultism as the King of Angels or the celestial scribe</note>. Pierce the veils between me and you; appear to me, O servants of this day and this hour.
The incense: Mastic and Benzoin.

# ( Chapter on the Attraction of the Pigeon )
<note>A "jalb" is a ritual intended to "pull" or attract someone to the practitioner, often for love or the return of a runaway.</note>

This is a great attraction; I found it in a very ancient manuscript and have corrected it. If you wish to work with it, write it on a Thursday during the hour of Venus <note>In planetary magic, the hour of Venus is traditionally chosen for matters of love and attraction</note> with musk, saffron, and rose water. The incense is moist agarwood soaked in rose water and benzoin. At the time of writing, you must be pure in garment, body, and place. Shield yourself with a protective conjuration lest your work be spoiled. If you recite the invocation and any apparition appears to you, do not fear; say to it: "Respond, O Harith, and hasten to attract [Name] to [Name]. Bring them like the snatching of the barren wind <note>A reference to the powerful wind mentioned in the Quran, 51:41</note>, and drive them to this station of mine, by the right of what I have sworn upon you. May God bless you and be upon you."

After writing, hang the charm on a tripod made of sweet pomegranate branches and recite the conjuration 21 times with the aforementioned incense. Then, tie it under the wing of a white pigeon—a male pigeon for a male target, and a female for a female. Beware, O seeker, that the pigeon does not escape from you; for if it flies from your hand, the mind of the person for whom the work is done will "fly" (they will lose their sanity). The pigeon must remain with the practitioner inside a cage or tied by its leg with a long thread. The intended person will come to the place where the pigeon is located within their day or night. If they are far away, the duration of their absence will be three days. If you seek them by night, they come by night; if by day, they come by day.

## ( This is what you write and the conjuration you recite )

Heilu-ish (2) Faliyu-ish (2) Abuy-Dabuy-ish (2) Lawaliyu-yah (2) Anuyab (2) Arakh (2) Radakh (2) Karash (2) Kamandiyush (2) Fashku-ish (2) Katru-ish (2) Katrun (2). I conjure you, O Shamkhayayil and O Metatron, by the name of the Judge...

Tajnahash (2) Tafash (2) Talush (2) Kaynamush (2) Anukh (2). In the name of God, the Subduer, Creator of the night and the day! I adjure you, O Harith <note>Harith is often used as a name for a specific spiritual entity or jinni in these contexts.</note>, make haste and do [such and such] by what I have sworn to you by the Great Names of God. **"It was but a single blast, and behold! They were all brought before Us."** <note>This is a quotation from the Quran, Surah Ya-Sin (36:53), used here to command the presence of the spirit or the person being summoned.</note> Obedience is to God and His Names. **"And it is indeed a mighty oath, if you only knew."** **"It is from Solomon, and it is: In the Name of God, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful: Rise not up against me, but come to me in submission."** <note>These are further Quranic citations from Surah Al-Waqi'a and Surah An-Naml, historically used in Solomonic magic to command spirits.</note> Make haste, make haste! At this moment!

## { Chapter for Extracting what is Beneath the Earth }

If you find a <term>buried treasure (dafin)</term> or something else and wish to extract it, take some old wheat, feathers from a <term>kite (hidaya)</term> <note>A kite is a bird of prey; its feathers are a common ingredient in medieval sympathetic magic.</note>, <term>black seed (habbat al-baraka)</term>, and <term>liquid storax (mi'a sayila)</term>. Grind them all together and knead them into the liquid storax to make small pellets. Burn these as incense and recite the <term>incantation (azima)</term> seven times while walking in a circle around the room. You will then see the earth sink and split open, and a vapor will rise from it. This is Tarish, the King of the <term>Inhabitants (al-ammar)</term> <note>In folk belief, the "Ammar" are the unseen spirits who reside in homes or specific plots of land. Tarish is their designated king in many grimoires.</note>. Keep safe what comes to you.

->( And this is the Incantation )<-

Mushtawish (2) Ahush (2) Bantrush (2) bi-Ahya Hinwi (2) Tayish (2) Talush (2) Dhara Han (2) Dar Dar (2) Lamhan (2) Hayush (2) Shamikh (2). O Lord of the Supreme Light, the Ancient, the One, the Most High! Answer, O Helper appointed over these names! Appear, rise up, and manifest the treasure that is in this place, and fulfill my need by the right of these names.

## { Chapter for Attracting Hearts to You }

If you desire this, perform your <term>ablutions (wudu)</term>, face the direction of prayer <note>The Qibla, or direction of Mecca.</note>, and say:

"There is no god but God; Muhammad is the intercessor of God" <note>Arabic: *La ilaha illa Allah Muhammad shafi' Allah*. This is a variation of the Muslim testimony of faith.</note> 1,000 times, provided that you do not speak to anyone until the number is completed. After every 100 repetitions, say the following:

O God, by the right of the sanctity of "There is no god but God; Muhammad is the intercessor of God," attract to me the hearts of all Your servants until they follow and submit to me with love, affection, and kindness, by Your mercy, O Most Merciful of the merciful. May God bless our master Muhammad and his family and companions and grant them peace.

# The Invocation of the Eternal One and its Explanation
->( The Practical Applications )<-

**( If you desire excitation )** <note>original: "al-tahyij"; this refers to a ritual intended to arouse passion or a state of intense spiritual/emotional attraction in another person.</note> Write the seal and the Names around it on a piece of paper and give it as a drink to whomever you wish, and you shall see a wonder. Or, it may be written upon a <term>trace (athar)</term> <note>An "athar" is an object belonging to the target, such as a piece of clothing or hair, used to establish a sympathetic magical link.</note> of the desired person and burned in a lamp with fine oil on Sunday night, while reciting the Invocation 21 times.

**( If you wish to release someone )** <note>This refers to "untying" (hall) a person who has been magically bound, often used in the context of curing induced impotence or removing a hex.</note> Write the seal and the Names around it on their palm, burn incense, and recite the Invocation. The palms will then begin to fold over one another. You should say: "Separate the fingers," and when they separate, say: "Bring the fingers together," and when they meet, say: "Just as the palms have met, let [So-and-so] be joined to [So-and-so]," and he shall be released.

**( If you wish to reveal a theft )** Gather the suspects and write the seal and the Names on the palm of a young boy. Write them also on a paper and place it on the boy's forehead. Put fine oil in his palm and recite the Invocation; he will then see the thief <note>This is a common form of "mandal" or scrying, where a child is used as a medium to see visions in a reflective surface like oil or ink.</note>.

**( If you wish to confuse the thief )** Write the names of the suspects upon their "trace" along with the seal and the Names. Place them inside a Persian reed, burn incense, and read the Invocation. Place the reed under the <term>rags of a millstone (riqa' al-tahyun)</term> <note>The heavy, grinding pressure of the millstone is symbolically used to compel the thief to return the items.</note>, and the stolen goods will be returned to their place.

**( If you wish for concealment )** <note>original: "al-ikhfa"; referring to invisibility or being overlooked by enemies.</note> The seal and the Names are written around it and placed upon your right arm.

**( If you wish to employ its servants )** Write the seal and the Names and carry them. Pray 11 units of prayer <note>original: "rak'ah"; the standing, bowing, and prostrating cycle of Islamic prayer.</note>. After every two units, recite the Invocation once and say:

> O God, O One, O Most Holy, O Merciful, O Eternal One! Subject to me Your four servants: **Abd al-Wahid** (Servant of the One), **Abd al-Quddus** (Servant of the Most Holy), **Abd al-Rahman** (Servant of the Merciful), and **Abd al-Samad** (Servant of the Eternal). Make them a help to me in what I desire from them, for You are capable of all things.

Then a kingly figure will come to you and make a sign. Say to him: "By God, tell me, what is your name?" If he says to you, "Abd al-Wahid," then say: "Welcome! What has God done with your brothers, O Abd al-Wahid?" He will say, "They are present." Say to him, "Go and bring them to me." But if he says his name is Abd al-Rahman or Abd al-Samad, do not speak with him; instead, continue reciting the Invocation. When they all appear, say to them: "May God reward your efforts." They will ask: "What is your need?" Say: "I desire faith in this world and piety for the Hereafter." You should have already written the <term>Throne Verse (Ayat al-Kursi)</term> <note>Quran 2:255, widely considered the most powerful verse for protection and spiritual authority.</note> on a palm frond before you; then make a covenant for what you desire. You must have preceded this with a <term>spiritual discipline (riyada)</term> <note>A period of fasting, seclusion, and intense prayer to purify the soul before attempting to contact spirits.</note> starting from Thursday until Sunday night, reciting the "Eternal" chapter (Al-Ikhlas) 7 times in every unit of prayer, and the Invocation after every two units. On Sunday night, pray as described before and recite the Invocation 21 times.

**( If you wish to summon a king from its servants )** For any matter you desire, write the seal and the Names, release the incense, and recite the Invocation 33 times. He will appear; then ask him for what you wish.

**( If you wish to summon a spiritual double )** <note>original: "qarin" (male) or "qarina" (female). In Middle Eastern folklore, every person has a spiritual counterpart or "double."</note> Write the seal and the Names in a new red vessel. Place the vessel in a high place and cover it with a cloth. Burn incense and recite the Invocation. You will see the cloth rise and shake; then say: "O Qarin (or Qarina), begin with a greeting." Once this is done, ask for what you wish.

**( If you wish to cause a hemorrhage in an oppressive woman )** Take a paper and write the seal and the Names along with His word, Exalted be He: **"When the sky is rent asunder..."** <note>This refers to the opening of Surah Al-Inshiqaq (Chapter 84 of the Quran). In this context, the "tearing" of the sky is used as a symbolic command for the body to "open" or bleed.</note> (to the end of the verse).

> In the name of God, the Gracious, the Merciful. May the peace and blessings of God be upon our master Muhammad, and upon his family and companions, forever until the Day of Judgment. O Possessor of Majesty and Honor, O Ever-Living, O Self-Subsisting Sustainer. And by the right of Him who said to the heavens and the earth, "Come, both of you, willingly or unwillingly," and they said, "We come willingly." At once, make haste, perform what I have commanded you in every kind, by the right of Him who manifested Himself to the mountain and turned it to dust, and Moses fell down unconscious from the light of His majesty. By <term>Lamqafanjal</term> <note>A powerful talismanic name/acronym often associated with the seven verses of the Opening and the seven days of the week.</note> and by a thousand times "There is no power or might except with God, the High, the Great."

## ( And This is the Seal )

| | | | | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| to Him | was | and not | equal | to Him | certainly | One |
| beget | and not | beget | not | born | and not | equal |
| to Him | God | One | He | God | The Eternal | to Him |
| and not | was | to Him | Say | He | God | One |
| and not | was | to Him | Say | He | God | One |
| to Him | and not | beget | not | born | and not | beget |
| not | born | and not | was | to Him | equal | One |

<note>This table is a "Seal" or talismanic square. It rearranges the words of Surah al-Ikhlas (Chapter 112 of the Quran). By breaking the linear text into a grid, the practitioner "locks" the power of the divine word into a geometric form intended to radiate spiritual influence.</note>

## ( For the Fulfillment of Needs )

If you have a very important matter, go into seclusion that night, remember God much, ask His forgiveness, and increase your prayers upon the Master of the Worlds <note>A title for the Prophet Muhammad.</note> while you are pure of clothes, body, and place, and burn pleasant incense. When you have prayed the Dawn prayer, read the *al-Samadiyya* <term>al-Samadiyya: A reference to Surah al-Ikhlas, the "Chapter of Sincerity," so named for the attribute "al-Samad" (The Eternal/Besought of all) found within it.</term> 1,641 times, then the following prayer 111 times, and ask God, glory be to Him, the Exalted, for the fulfillment of your need, for it will be granted by the grace of God. And this is the prayer you say:

> O God, O He who is *Ahun, Qaf, Adam, Ha-Mim, Ha, Amen*. <note>original: "Ahun Qaf Adam Ha-Mim Ha Amin." These are "Names of Power" and disjointed letters from the Quran (Muqatta'at) used in Islamic esotericism for their vibrational and symbolic weight.</note>

## ( Chapter on the Descent of Light, Unveiling, Joy, and Blossoms )

If you wish to illuminate your insight, and cause the spiritual souls to descend into the cities of your heart, so that secrets are not hidden from you and you see the inward nature of things as you see their outward appearance, and the Truth manifests upon you through Light, so that the dark human existence no longer remains in your being, and your dark veils and walls are lifted—and it has many subtleties:

(If you wish for that) fast for the sake of God the Exalted for a period of 15 days, a fast of spiritual exercise <term>Riyada: A period of ascetic discipline, usually involving a retreat and avoiding animal products, intended to "tame" the lower self.</term>, beginning on a Sunday; and if that is at the start of the lunar month, it is better. Recite these names after every prayer 3,000 times, which are:

(Light, Guide, Inward, Outward, Manifest, Revealer) <note>original: "Nur, Hadi, Batin, Zahir, Mubin, Kashif." These are Names of God chosen specifically for their relation to sight and manifestation.</note> When you finish the recitation, read this verse 10 times, which is:

(God is the light of the heavens and the earth... [until] ...to His light whom He wills.) <note>The "Verse of Light" (Ayat al-Nur) from Quran 24:35, considered one of the most mystical verses in the Quran.</note> Then read this invocation 7 times.

### ### ( And this is the supplication )

O Lord, I ask You by every name that is Yours, which You have revealed in Your Book, or taught to any of Your creation, or kept to Yourself in the knowledge of the unseen with You, that You illuminate my heart with the light of Your illuminating Name, and support me with **unveiling** <term>unveiling (kashf): A mystical state in Sufism where the veil between the human heart and the divine secrets is lifted.</term> and manifestation, so that I may be strong enough to reveal the darknesses of the **Point** <note>In mystical geometry and Sufism, the "Point" often represents the origin of creation or the seed of existence within the darkness of the void.</note> which is within the darknesses of my existence, that which belongs to Adam Your prophet, peace be upon him, and for which You made Your angels prostrate to him. Let my beginning be joined to Your end, and let the manifest be revealed by the hidden, so that the hidden secrets, the latent mysteries, and the guaranteed states are not concealed from me. Reveal from my heart the veil of darkness and illuminate me with the light of kindness and mercy, O Lord of all light, O Revealer of all that is hidden. And God knows best.

### ### ( Description of a spirit-summoning trance found in a Moroccan book )

If you wish to work with this, write the names in the palm of the hand and recite the incantation over it. These are the words you say:

*Batsha* (2), *Bahshata* (2), *Hash-shata* (2), *Samrayil* (2) <note>original: "بطشا بهشطا هششطا صمريل" — these are magical names, likely derived from angelic or Hebrew roots used in theurgy.</note>, by the right of what Your book has been sanctified with, and by the obedience of the angels to You, hasten and do what I have commanded you, and inform me of what I ask you. *Al-Waha* (Haste), *Al-'Ajal* (Speed), *Al-Sa'ah* (This hour). *Hayya* (3). O blessed Forelock, may the blessing of God be in you and upon you.

**The incense:** Male frankincense and dried coriander.

**The dismissal:** <note>A ritual formula used to send spirits back to their realm so they do not linger.</note> The end of Surah al-Jumu'ah and Surah al-Zalzalah until the word "separate" (ashtatan).

### ### ( A benefit for the fulfillment of matters )

If you desire that, read Surah Ya-Sin 10 times after the Dawn prayer and before sunrise. Recite the name 70 times afterwards, and it is this:

( *Ahun, Qaf, Adam, Ham, Ha, Amen* ) <note>A repeated "Great Name" or formula appearing throughout this manuscript, combining Quranic "mysterious letters" with the name of the first man and the word Amen.</note> and ask for what you want. The method of asking is to say after your recitation at the specified time: "I ask You, O God, O He who is *Ahun, Qaf, Adam, Ham, Ha, Amen*, do such and such for me." It is the **Greatest Name** <term>The Greatest Name (al-Ism al-A'zam): In Islamic tradition, a secret name of God which, if used in prayer, ensures the prayer is granted.</term>.

### ### ( A chapter of love for reconciliation and affection )

If you desire that, write the following and place it in the turban. After three days, hang it in the air by a hair from the desired person.

### ### ( And this is what you write )

*Bahruj* (2), *'Awj* (2). Where are you, **Shamhurish**, O judge of the Jinn? Where are you, **Zawba'ah**, O owner of the four heads? Where are you, **Maymun Aba Nukh**? Where are you, *Tamlush*? Where are you, *Mahrush*? Where are you, *Tahrush*, O owner of the great station? Where are you, *Shahwab*, O you whose movements make the mountains tremble in the East and the West? Take control over [Name] and drive her to [Name], and fly with her with the birds between the sky and the earth. Do not let her delay for the blink of an eye, nor let her enjoy food or drink ever until she appears before him. By the right of these names upon you and your obedience to them: *Bahruj, 'Awj, by Ahya Sharahiya Hamuta* <note>original: "بأهيا شراهيا" — This is a common Arabic magical rendering of the Hebrew "Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh" (I Am That I Am).</note>. *Al-Waha, Al-Waha, Al-'Ajal, Al-'Ajal, Al-Sa'ah, Al-Sa'ah*. May the blessing of God be in you and upon you.

# The Conclusion

With the help of God Almighty, the printing of the treatise *The Goal of the Wise* <term>The Goal of the Wise (Ghayat al-Hakim): A famous 11th-century book of magic and astrology, known in the West as the *Picatrix*.</term> by Master **Al-Majriti** <note>Maslama al-Majriti, a 10th-century Andalusian scholar to whom the book is traditionally attributed.</note> has been completed. I have concluded it with a collection of illustrious benefits, and it has been corrected and reviewed with the utmost care—a truly unique pearl, praise be to God.

O noble reader, O possessor of a sound mind: I ask you, by what God revealed in the Glorious Book—to which falsehood cannot come from before it or behind it, a revelation from the All-Wise, the Praiseworthy—that you do not use what is contained herein except for what pleases God and His Messenger. Let your orientation be toward piety, so that your works may be crowned with success and you may find happiness in both worlds. I also hope you will forgive any slip or stumble you find in my book; please overlook them, for forgiveness is a trait of the noble. Turn a blind eye to our shortcomings, and peace be upon you.

<margin>Mahmoud Nassar</margin>
<note>Mahmoud Nassar was likely the editor or the individual responsible for this specific printed edition of the text.</note>

<vocab>The Goal of the Wise, Al-Majriti, Talismans, Invocation, Sending, Summoning, Dismissal, Mahmoud Nassar, Conclusion</vocab>

<summary>This final page of the manuscript provides specific rituals for sending spiritual influences and summoning a spirit named Master Aati, using a blend of Quranic verses and Hebrew-derived divine names. It concludes with the editor's colophon, identifying the work as the famous grimoire "The Goal of the Wise" (Picatrix) and urging the reader to use its secrets ethically.</summary>
<keywords>Ghayat al-Hakim, Picatrix, Master Aati, spirit summoning, Adonai, El Shaddai, Al-Majriti, Mahmoud Nassar, talismans, Islamic magic</keywords>
